# Chauffeur Solver & AI Scheduling Assistant Capabilities

This document outlines the core logic, constraints, and weighting mechanics of the Chauffeur Scheduling Solver. It serves as the primary context layer for the AI Scheduling Assistant when parsing natural language into `Rule` JSON objects or when providing scheduling optimization advice.

## Core Solver Mechanics (CP-SAT)

The solver formulates the schedule by assigning drivers to events in a way that maximizes the global "weight" (score) while strictly adhering to hard constraints. 

### Baseline Event Weights & Penalties
When evaluating whether to assign an event to a driver or leave it unassigned:
- **Base Assignment Reward:** `1,000,000` (modified by `unassigned_penalty_multiplier` in Theme settings).
  - *Leaving an event unassigned scores `0`. If a driver's total score drops below 0 for an event, the solver will refuse to assign them and leave the event unassigned instead.*
  - *This value is deliberately large so that assigning an event at all outweighs routing preferences. Compare bonuses against each other, not against this base — it is identical for every driver on the same event and therefore cancels out when choosing between drivers.*
- **Priority Scaling Bonus:** `+150` points per rank. (e.g., Priority 1 driver gets `+1350`, Priority 10 gets `+0`).
- **Driver in Event (Attendee):** `+50,000,000` (Massive bonus if the driver is also attending the event. This is the largest term in the model apart from manual overrides, and in practice it decides the assignment on its own.)
- **Primary Driver Bonus:** `+2000` (modified by `primary_driver_bonus_multiplier`).
- **Manual Override:** `+100,000,000` (plus the override's `created_at` epoch seconds, so newer overrides win). Dominates every other objective term, including the saturated load-balancing penalty (≤1.44M at its cap). Every hard ban in the model also carries an `overridden_pairs` escape — `unavailable` rules, `required`-rule bans on other drivers, driver personal-event overlaps, the passenger-overlap constraint, and the trip-assignment bans — so a drag-and-drop override always sticks regardless of any other setting. Before creating the override, the dashboard dry-runs the pair through `POST /api/overrides/check` (`matcher.explain_assignment_conflicts`): if the solver would normally refuse it (driver away on a trip, passengers away on a trip, unavailable/avoid/required-elsewhere rules, the driver's own calendar event at that time, preferred-hours violation), a confirmation dialog lists the human-readable reasons and asks "Override anyway?" — informed consent, never a silent block. The check reads trip windows/entities from `trip_metadata` persisted in the combined schedule cache.
- **Group Bonus:** `+1000` when both events of a `group` rule go to the same driver.
- **Stickiness Bonus:** `+5` points if they drove the same event in the previous run.
- **Passenger Continuity:** Up to `+50,000` points if a driver handles consecutive events back-to-back with the same passenger. This decays linearly to `0` dynamically based on the specific travel gap threshold where a driver would typically have enough time to go home for a layover (e.g., 75+ minutes).
- **Location Continuity:** Up to `+5,000` points if a driver handles consecutive events at the exact same location, decaying linearly to `0` at a 3-hour gap.

**Penalties:**
- **Travel Time:** Scaled heavy penalty (approx `-600` points per 10 minutes) of driving from the previous event's location to the next. This penalty *only* applies if the gap between events is 1 hour or less, to avoid unfairly penalizing drivers for taking completely independent trips separated by long layovers at home.
- **Tolerance Overlap:** Heavy dynamic penalty (`-50,000`) if an event relies on a tolerance rule to be feasible for a primary driver. The penalty comfortably overcomes the primary driver bonus (`+2000`) and group bonus (`+1000`) combined, so the solver prefers a clean secondary driver over a primary driver who has to be late. It does **not** overcome the attendee bonus or a manual override, and it exactly ties the maximum passenger-continuity bonus (`+50,000`) — so at a near-zero travel gap, continuity can cancel it out.
- **Preferred Hours Violation:** `-2,000,000` points if an event falls outside the driver's preferred working hours. At default settings this drives the score below 0 (`1,000,000 - 2,000,000`) so the event stays unassigned. It does **not** hold if the driver is an attendee (`+50,000,000` swamps it) or if `unassigned_penalty_multiplier >= 2.0`.
- **Soft Buffer Violation:** `-2,000` points if an event slightly overlaps a buffer zone.

### Load Balancing Mode (optional)
Toggled by `load_balancing_enabled` in Settings (Config → Drivers tab; default **off**), with a companion `load_balancing_metric` combo box choosing what "load" means. By default the solver has **no fairness term**: per-event driver scores are independent, so the highest-scoring driver (primary group, lowest `priority_index`) absorbs every event they can physically fit ("bucket filling"), and the stickiness and continuity bonuses reinforce that concentration across runs.

When enabled, the solver subtracts a **quadratic penalty on each driver's daily load** (sum-of-squares is minimized by an even split, so each additional event on a loaded driver costs more than the same event on an idle one). The metric sets the per-event load value and penalty coefficient:
- **`events`** — 1 per event, coefficient `10,000`. Fair by count, ignores duration and distance.
- **`driving_time`** — round-trip minutes from the driver's home (falling back to the global `home_location`) to the event, clamped to 240, coefficient `5`; unknown routes cost a neutral 60 so a home-less driver never becomes a free sink. This is a **proxy**: true driving is only known after assignment, and chained events share travel, so back-to-back same-location events each count a full round trip. A driver living at the venue genuinely has zero driving load and will soak up those events — by design.
- **`occupied_time`** (default) — summed event durations in minutes (each clamped to 600), coefficient `1`.

Interaction with other weights (all metrics):
- **Overrules:** the Priority Scaling Bonus (`+150`/rank) and Primary Driver Bonus (`+2000`) — a typical one-event move gains ~10k–40k points.
- **Does not overrule:** Attendee bonus (`+50M`), Manual Overrides (`+100M`), or `required` rules. Tight passenger-continuity chains (up to `+50,000`) also survive unless the imbalance is severe.
- **Never causes unassignment:** each driver's load saturates at a per-metric cap (`AddMinEquality`), bounding the marginal penalty of one more event strictly below the `1,000,000` base assignment reward.
- Balancing counts only solver-assigned driving (including self-driven attendee events); personal calendar events that need no driver are not counted.

### Cars & Vehicle Assignment (C1, v2.47.0 — docs/car_entity_design.md)
Cars are an optional entity (Config → People tab → Cars; `/api/cars`; `Car` in `models/schemas.py`). Each car has: `seat_capacity` (passenger seats, excluding the driver), `allowed_driver_ids`, optional `allowed_passenger_ids` (car-seat restrictions; `null` = anyone fits), optional `default_driver_id` (the "usual" driver), `unavailable_ranges` (inclusive ISO date ranges — loaned out, in the shop), and `is_disabled`.

- **Inert when unconfigured**: with zero cars the solver builds no car variables or constraints — behavior is identical to the pre-car model, and `car_assignments` (4th return value of `solve_schedule`, `event_id → car_id`) is empty.
- **Implicit personal car**: a driver listed in **no** car's `allowed_driver_ids` is untouched by every car constraint. Only "pooled" drivers (listed on ≥1 car) must draw a workable car for each assigned event: the car must permit them, fit the event's passengers (count and, if restricted, identity), and not be in an unavailable range. A pooled driver with no workable car is banned from the event — escapable by manual override like every other hard ban.
- **Contention & handoffs**: a car is never in two places at once. Two different drivers may reuse the same car for nearby events only if the gap covers `travel(e1 → home) + travel(home → e2)` — handoffs happen at home; midday non-home handoffs are deliberately unmodeled. A driver chaining back-to-back events keeps the same physical car (and cannot chain into an event their current car can't serve without time to swap at home).
- **Swap penalty `−2500`**: using a car whose `default_driver_id` is someone else. Above priority deltas (≤ ~1,500) so priority alone never forces a key swap; below a `preferred` rule (`+10,000`) so rules can. Car chips render everywhere driver assignments do: calendar week/day badges (short ≤40-min events use a compact single-row layout: title + pax dots + car + driver, no time line), the calendar Agenda view, admin dashboard schedule columns, PWA family cards, the PWA driver view ("Take the {car}" per drive), and the kid lens ("Dad is driving you in the Minivan" — rides and legs carry a `car` object in `/api/member_day`). Swaps get an amber `⇄` accent.
- **`Driver.max_passengers`** (optional): driver-level passenger cap independent of cars (teen graduated-licensing laws). Applies even with zero cars configured; override-escapable.
- **Diagnostics**: unassignment reasons of type `"car"` — "No car this driver may drive works for this event: …", per-car failure detail (seats, car-seat restriction, unavailable + reason), and the `max_passengers` cap. Static bans only; a car merely claimed by another driver at that hour falls through to the generic conflict/optimization reasons.
- **Agent tool** `manage_car` (v1 registry, bridged to the chat widget): create/update/delete cars and `set_unavailable`/`clear_unavailable` date ranges ("the minivan is loaned to Aunt Sarah through Friday"). Cars also appear in `get_current_state`.
- **Photos** (v2.48.0): cars and family members can carry an `image` — a ~128px square data-URL photo, client-side cropped/resized on upload (Config → People / Cars), stored in the entity document (no file hosting). Photos win over emoji/initials wherever the surface has room: config lists, PWA member switcher and driver header, kid-lens driver chips and the "in the {car}" line, the driver-view Take-the-car chip, family-map markers, chores/routines tiles. Tiny calendar/dashboard chips stay icon+name.
- **Not modeled in the solver**: live car location, cars consumed by a pooled driver's personal (non-solver) events, mileage-based energy math.

### Car Telemetry (C2, v2.49.0 — docs/car_telemetry_design.md, services/cars.py)
Cars optionally map HA entities (explicit ids, no auto-discovery — Config → Cars form): `ha_device_tracker`, `ha_battery_entity` (EV %), `ha_fuel_entity` (%), `ha_range_entity` (display only). All unset → the car is untouched by every telemetry feature. **Telemetry informs humans and creates errands; it never moves solver assignments.**
- **Family map**: cars with a tracker appear as rounded-square markers (photo/emoji) with a popup showing zone, 🔌/⛽ levels, range, and the in-progress drive using the car; zone chip when the tracker has no coords.
- **Readiness → approval proposals** (C3, v2.50.0 — docs/car_errand_proposals_design.md): the 30-min sweep (quiet-hours gated) now creates an `add_car_stop` **action proposal** instead of a bare push when battery < `car_battery_warn_pct` (30) or fuel < `car_fuel_warn_pct` (25) **and** the car has drives in the next 24h. Dedupe `car_ready:{car}:{DATE OF FIRST UPCOMING DRIVE}` — so the evening run proposes for *tomorrow* ("still low after today's drives") separately from the morning's same-day proposal. The card posts to the **family channel** (parents approve; chat fan-out pushes phones) and to the **admin dashboard's approvals banner** (`GET /api/action-proposals?status=proposed`). Approval (parent/adult) creates a priority-1, 1-day-window errand tagged `auto_car_fuel`+car id — the errand pass then places it **minimum-detour**: before the first event, between events, drop-off-and-run, or on the way home.
- **Along-route station pick**: `cars.pick_station` feeds the cached route polyline (same-day: last event → home; tomorrow: home → first event) to Mapbox Search Box **category search with search-along-route** (`sar_type=isochrone`, encoded polyline, `time_deviation=8` min — ranked by real detour, no session token, `category` metering). Fallbacks: midpoint proximity search ranked by distance-to-polyline → `car_fuel_station` setting → near-origin search → unlocated proposal.
- **EV charge buffers reserve time, not places**: low-battery proposals hold `car_charge_buffer_mins` (25) at the first event's location (detour ≈ 0) — "car picks the charger". EVs never get a station search.
- **`car_auto_errand`** (default off) now means auto-approve: fuel stops skip the card and land directly, with an informational push.
- **Car-alert settings persistence (v2.56.2)**: the four car-alert fields (`car_battery_warn_pct`, `car_fuel_warn_pct`, `car_auto_errand`, `car_fuel_station`) were missing from the `Settings` Pydantic model, so `POST /api/settings` silently DROPPED them on every save since v2.47 (Pydantic ignores unknown fields; the UI and `services/cars.py` reader both existed). Fields added to the model. Lesson: any new setting needs THREE touches — config.html load/save, the reader, AND `models/schemas.py:Settings` — or saves vanish without error.
- **(shopping, v2.76.6) The "+ List" skip dialog was markup-orphaned**: it shipped OUTSIDE `x-data="shoppingPage()"`, which closed 2 lines above it, so `x-show="drain"` bound to nothing, `x-cloak` was never removed, and the dialog could not open at all — items landed on the list with no explanation, i.e. the exact symptom it was written to cure. Tags were balanced, methods all existed, the route existed: every guard passed. Now guarded by `scenario_alpine_directives_have_a_data_root` in tests/test_shopping.py (HTML-parses the page, flags any Alpine directive with no `x-data` ancestor); verified against the shipped file, which it flags at line 318 plus 10 descendants. No other template has the defect.
- **The sweep never actually ran until v2.76.5**: its caller in `main.py:push_notification_loop` said `datetime.datetime.now()`, but `main.py` binds `datetime` to the **class** (`from datetime import datetime`), so the whole block raised `AttributeError` on its first line every 30s from v2.49.0 onward and the `except` printed "Car readiness sweep error: type object 'datetime.datetime' has no attribute 'datetime'". No proposal, push or away-warning was ever delivered. `services/cars.py` uses `import datetime` and was correct throughout; all 9 C2 scenarios called `run_sweep` **directly**, which is exactly why none of them saw a dead caller. Both spellings are legal in the same file (a nested `import datetime` re-shadows the name back to the module — main.py has 7 legitimate ones), so this cannot be caught by eye or by import. Guarded by `scenario_no_datetime_class_module_confusion` in tests/test_car_telemetry.py: an AST scan of main.py + services/ that resolves the binding of `datetime` per scope. Same family as the v2.56.2 dropped-settings bug and the v2.76.1/2.76.3 dead-UI-call bugs — **a background block whose failure is only ever printed is indistinguishable from one that never fires**, so any new sweep needs a test that enters through its real caller.
- **Home gas-station picker (v2.56.2, map-first v2.56.3)**: `GET /api/cars/stations/nearby` — one Mapbox category search (`gas_station`) around the geocoded `home_location`, haversine-sorted with km distances + coords + home coords. Config → Car alerts "📍 Find nearby" opens an inline Mapbox GL map (dark-v11, 🏠 home marker, ⛽ pins; tap a pin → popup with name/address/distance and a **Select** button that writes `car_fuel_station` and saves immediately). Same quota gate as the trip map (`/config` route passes `mapbox_key` + `allow_map_loads`; one map load billed per picker open via the telemetry endpoint; free tier 50k/mo, self-cap default 45k); tap-to-choose chips are the fallback when the map can't load. GL objects live OUTSIDE Alpine's reactive data (a Proxy-wrapped mapboxgl.Map breaks). Friendly errors when home is unset/ungeocodable or Mapbox is disabled.
- **Away warning** (unchanged, dedupe `car_away:{car}:{event}`): drive within 3h + tracker not `home` + no in-progress drive → push.
- **Evening digest**: tomorrow's drive digest lines include per-driver car notes ("⛽ Minivan at 15% — see the fuel-stop proposal"), built in `build_drive_digests` so the `get_drive_digest` agent tool matches.
- Settings live in Config → Cars → "Car alerts". HA entity mapping is config-UI only (not exposed through `manage_car`).

## Travel Time Data & Caching
Travel times come from Mapbox's Matrix API using the free-flow `driving` profile. Durations are treated as **static**: a cached value for a pair of fixed addresses does not go stale, and the solver reads them without an age check. **As of v2.165.0 this is only the PLANNING layer** — see "Day-of traffic" below for the layer surfaces act on.
- **Day-of traffic (v2.165.0)**: three layers, cheapest first. (1) The static matrix — the solver's baseline, unchanged. (2) A **morning pass** (default 06:00, `traffic_morning_hour`): each of today's driving legs gets one `driving-traffic` Directions request with `depart_at` set to its planned departure — *predictive* traffic for that hour, so the day reads realistic from breakfast. (3) A **T-60 refine**: one live request an hour before the *current* (possibly morning-adjusted) departure. Two requests per leg per day, hard-marked per stage in app_state (`traffic_sweep_done`, marker set BEFORE the fetch — the historic 100k-element month was a cache bug refetching every 10 minutes, and the markers make that failure mode structurally impossible). The sweep (`maps.run_day_of_traffic_sweep`) runs off the push loop, self-throttled to 2 min, work-listed from the pending Time-to-Leave notifications (which now carry `origin`/`destination`/`travel_static_mins` per leg; END-anchored pushes like "Drive Home" carry no route on purpose — traffic doesn't change when a game finishes). Results land in a **date-scoped cache** (`storage.get/set_cached_day_of_traffic` — valid only on the fetch day; yesterday's rush hour never shades today). **Surfaces read the cache, never fetch**: `leave_by.for_run(live=True)` overlays it on the hero/board/screensaver Next-up (`home_board.todays_runs`) and the PWA day view (`member_day`), adding `traffic_delay_mins` (hero card shows "+N traffic" in amber); the push loop fires on `maps.live_adjusted_trigger` and appends "traffic is slow, leave N min early". **Earlier only, today only**: free-flow is effectively the floor, a slower reading advances departures, a faster one never delays a plan someone may be acting on, and the overlay refuses non-today dates so the tomorrow digest stays static. Kill switch `traffic_live_enabled` (maps settings). `/api/debug/travel` reports the pair's `day_of_traffic` row. **The cache is coordinate-keyed (v2.165.1)** — caught live on day one: the sweep stored under the solver edge's address spelling while the overlay/debug read with the settings spelling (same house, "NC, USA" vs "North Carolina 27523, United States"), so every read missed; keys are now geocoded to 4-decimal coords (`maps._traffic_cache_key`, raw-string fallback for ungeocodable pairs), all reads go through `maps.get_day_of_traffic`, and the sweep markers moved to `traffic_sweep_done_v2` so string-keyed buys re-bought once. NOTE: the sweep work-lists from pending notifications, which regenerate only on a SOLVE — after deploying, hit `POST /api/schedule/refresh` once or wait for the next natural solve. Tests: `tests/test_day_of_traffic.py`.
- **Toll policy (v2.166.0)**: Mapbox defaults to tolls-allowed, which priced Apex drives over the NC-540 toll road (a 16-minute route quoted as the drive time whether or not the family takes it). `routing_avoid_tolls` (maps settings, default off) sends `exclude=toll` on every DRIVING request — day-of traffic, route geometry, the pairwise Directions tier — and **skips the Matrix tier entirely** (the Matrix API cannot express the exclusion; pairs route through pairwise Directions instead, still bounded by `mapbox_directions_max_pairs`). Flipping the setting calls `storage.clear_route_caches()` (static distance + day-of + geometry caches + sweep markers, schedules marked dirty): the static cache is deliberately immortal, so a policy flip must burn it or the app quotes the other policy's minutes forever. **v2.166.1**: all three knobs (`traffic_live_enabled`, `traffic_morning_hour`, `routing_avoid_tolls`) are Settings-model fields (the save endpoint silently drops keys the model does not carry — the partial-save lesson) and render in a **Routing & Traffic** card on the config page's maps section. The settings registry is a catalog, not a renderer — an entry there alone produces no UI. Tests: `tests/test_toll_routing.py`.
- **Priming**: each schedule refresh primes only the location pairs it does not already have. Stale-but-valid pairs are never re-purchased — doing so previously exceeded the 100,000 element/month Matrix allowance.
- **`route_cache_duration_mins`**: defaults to `43200` (30 days). It only needs shortening if a traffic-aware profile is introduced.
- **Unroutable pairs**: when a location cannot be geocoded or the routing APIs fail, the pair is cached as `-1` and reported to the solver as `900` minutes (effectively unreachable). This suppresses retry storms against the API quota, but expires after **24 hours** so that a transient outage cannot permanently poison the cache.
- **Geocode precision & self-healing (v2.56.4)**: geocode-cache rows carry `precision` (`exact` | `city` | `failed`) + `ts`. Exact street-level hits are final; city-fallback and failed entries retry daily (`maps._usable_cached`, `GEOCODE_RETRY_SECS`) — a single bad geocode can never weld an address to the city center permanently (the old code cached the city fallback under the full address forever). Legacy pre-precision rows are sniffed on read (address starts with a house number the display_name doesn't echo → treated as city, retried immediately). Re-saving Home Location purges a non-exact home entry (a user-visible fresh-lookup lever); a *changed* home always purges.
- **The amputation bug (fixed v2.56.4)**: `extract_street_address` dropped the FIRST comma part of any >3-part address as a "business name" even when it was the street line itself — so Mapbox-canonical addresses ("123 St, City, State ZIP, United States") geocoded to the city center, poisoning home, event locations, and every Matrix travel time derived from them. Fixed (a digit-leading first part is kept whole) + one-time startup migration (`migrations.migrate_geocode_amputation_v2564`, app_state-gated): purges sniffably-wrong geocode rows and resets distance/route/schedule caches so durations re-derive from healed coordinates. Tests: `tests/test_geocode_cache.py`.
- **Home-pin transparency**: `/api/cars/stations/nearby` returns `home_label` + `home_precision`; the Config station map's 🏠 pin popup and caption show exactly what the home address resolved to, with an amber warning when it's only city-level — a wrong home pin is VISIBLE, never silent.
- **Travel-time forensics (v2.56.5)**: `GET /api/debug/travel?event=<title substring>` (or `?destination=`, `&origin=` override, `&fresh=true`) answers "why does the app think X is N minutes away": both addresses' cleaned form, geocode result (display name + precision — a wrong venue match is visible), straight-line km, the cached Matrix duration the solver uses, and with `fresh=true` a free-flow vs **driving-traffic** Directions comparison — separating "geocoded the wrong place" from "free-flow profile vs rush hour". Location-less events are reported as the problem instead of routed. v2.56.6 adds a `drivers` section: each driver's ACTUAL routing origin — a driver record's own `home_location` OVERRIDES the global home in the solver's initial edges (matcher.py `driver_default_home`), so a stale per-driver address produces leave-at chips that disagree with the global-home pair; `uses_own_address: true` with a different `resolved_to` is the smoking gun. Remember the standing design choice this tool exposes: Matrix durations are static free-flow (no traffic), so rush-hour drives read optimistic by design (softened by the v2.165.0 day-of layer for scheduled drives). **v2.165.2**: `?event=` matches also report `drive_status` — the matched event's id, assigned driver, `is_scheduled_drive`, any OTHER events the title substring matched (a hit on the wrong sibling — "Girls" vs "Girls 2033" — once sent a whole debug session to the wrong venue), and a plain note when the event is not a scheduled drive at all (no driver assigned, usually because no passengers are configured): such an event has no leave-by, no push, and no day-of traffic — its numbers are informational only.

---

## Storage Engine
All persistence goes through `services/storage.py`, backed by **SQLite** (`data/chauffeur.sqlite3` locally, `/data/chauffeur.sqlite3` on the HA add-on) as of 2026-07-16. It is a document store — one table per legacy TinyDB table, each row `(doc_id, JSON data)` — accessed via a TinyDB-compatible table API (`services/storage_sqlite.py`), so callers still pass `tinydb.Query` objects. WAL mode; writes are single-row and take ~1 ms (previously TinyDB rewrote the entire 4.8 MB JSON file per write, ~275 ms).
- **Toggle**: `CHAUFFEUR_STORAGE=tinydb` env var is a one-release escape hatch back to the legacy engine; `CHAUFFEUR_DATA_DIR` redirects the data directory (used by tests, which must never touch `data/`).
- **Migration**: first sqlite boot auto-migrates `db.json` + `routes_cache.json` (route geometry now lives in the same SQLite file), preserving doc_ids and deduplicating the distance/geocode caches; originals are kept as `*.pre-sqlite.bak`. Restore-from-JSON = delete the `.sqlite3` file, place a `db.json`, restart.
- **Backups**: `/api/download_db` zips the data dir, snapshotting the SQLite file via the backup API (safe under WAL).
- **`db_lock`**: the coarse process-wide lock in storage.py is still in place (cheap now); removing it is a separate planned cleanup.

---

## Rule Attributes & Filters
Every rule (Routing Rule or Priority Rule) can filter events using any combination of the following criteria. The solver evaluates these using AND logic across different criteria types.
- **Keywords**: Match substrings in the event title or description. (Match Any or Match All).
- **Passengers**: Match specific passengers who are attending the event. (Match Any or Match All).
- **Days of Week**: Restrict rules to specific days (0=Monday, 6=Sunday).
- **Date Window (`start_date`, `end_date`)**: Restrict a rule to only apply on or after a specific start date (YYYY-MM-DD), and/or on or before an end date. Useful for driver vacations, summer schedules, or holiday-specific rules.
- **Time Window (`time_start`, `time_end`)**: Restrict a rule to a specific time of day window.
- **Location**: Match a substring in the event location.

---

## Supported Constraints (`constraint_type`)

When generating JSON for the frontend to digest, use the following `constraint_type` mappings.

### 1. Driver Assignment
Controls how the solver treats a specific driver for an event. Each of these is its own top-level `constraint_type` value — there is **no** `"assignment"` constraint_type and **no** `assignment_type` property. `Rule` has no such field, so a rule shaped that way is silently discarded by the schema and does nothing.

All four require `driver_id` to name the driver the rule applies to.

* `constraint_type: "required"` (Hard Constraint)
  * The selected driver MUST be assigned. All other drivers are strictly prohibited. The named driver also receives a `+500` weight bonus.
* `constraint_type: "preferred"` (Weight Bonus)
  * Grants a massive `+10000` weight bonus to the selected driver. They will almost certainly get the assignment unless physically impossible.
* `constraint_type: "unavailable"` (Hard Constraint)
  * The selected driver is strictly forbidden from taking the event.
* `constraint_type: "avoid"` (Soft Penalty)
  * Caps the driver's score at `1` — above `0` (the score of leaving the event unassigned) but below every viable driver. The driver is pushed to the absolute bottom of the priority list yet is still chosen as a "last resort" rather than leaving the event unassigned. A driver already excluded for another reason (e.g. a preferred-hours violation, which yields a negative score) stays excluded; `avoid` never resurrects them.

### 2. Duplicate Event Handling (`constraint_type: "duplicate"`)
For recurring events or overlapping schedules.
* `duplicate_action: "schedule_one"`: The solver will only schedule *one* matching event per `grouping_period` (daily, weekly, monthly, all).
* `duplicate_action: "schedule_all"`: Schedule all occurrences as normal.

### 3. Tolerance - Late Arrival / Early Departure (`constraint_type: "tolerance"`)
Allows an event to be "fudged" to make an otherwise impossible schedule fit.
* `tolerance_mins`: Integer. How many minutes the solver is allowed to overlap.
* `tolerance_type`: `"arrival"`, `"departure"`, or `"both"`.

### 4. Group Events (`constraint_type: "group"`)
Forces the solver to assign the exact same driver to a set of different events.
* Uses the `filter_sets` array instead of standard keyword/passenger filters. Each element in the array represents a distinct event match (e.g., grouping Lily's 8:00am swim practice with James's 9:00am tennis practice).

### 5. Buffer Time (`constraint_type: "buffer"`)
Enforces artificial padding around an event for a driver.
* `buffer_before_mins`: Integer.
* `buffer_after_mins`: Integer.

### 6. Event Attendance (`constraint_type: "attendance"`)
* `attendance_action: "stay"`: The driver must stay at the event for its entire duration.
* `attendance_action: "dropoff_pickup"`: The driver will split the event into a distinct Drop-off (at the start time) and a Pickup (at the end time). The driver is free to do other things during the event duration. The Drop-off and Pick-up are treated as independent events and are NOT grouped together, meaning they can be freely assigned to different drivers.

---

## Priority Rules (`PriorityRule` objects)
Distinct from standard rules, priority rules allow dynamic modification of the `Base Assignment Reward` (`1,000,000`) on an event-by-event basis. The `weight_modifier` is added to that base verbatim — there is no rescaling or remapping.
- **Why use this?** If the user says "Doctor appointments are the most important thing to schedule," you create a Priority Rule with `keywords: ["Doctor"]` and `weight_modifier: 100000`. This ensures the solver prioritizes finding *any* driver for the doctor appointment before solving other low-priority events.
- **Choosing a magnitude.** The modifier only matters relative to the *other* terms it competes with, since the base is identical across events. Useful reference points: `1000` is a mild nudge (enough to break a tie, roughly 17 minutes of travel penalty); `100,000` outranks the maximum passenger-continuity bonus (`+50,000`); `500,000` is effectively "schedule this before anything else." Negative values deprioritize. Nothing outranks the attendee bonus or a manual override.

## Matching Logic
- **AND vs OR:** Across different criteria types in a single rule (e.g., Keywords AND Passengers AND Days), the solver uses `AND` logic. An event must meet all configured criteria types.
- **Match Any vs Match All:** For criteria that support multiple selections (Keywords, Passengers), the solver defaults to "Match Any" (OR logic). 
  - For example, if two passengers are selected, the rule matches if *either* is an attendee.
  - This behavior can be toggled using the `keywords_match_all` and `passengers_match_all` boolean flags. When set to `true`, the solver switches to "Match All" (AND logic) for that specific criterion, requiring all listed keywords or passengers to be present on the event for the rule to match.
- **Filter Sets (Groups):** For grouped rules with multiple `filter_sets`, the evaluation across sets is an `OR` logic (matches if it hits Group A OR Group B).

## Best Practices for AI Recommendations
1. **Never use "Avoid" to completely block a driver.** If the driver absolutely cannot do it, use `unavailable`. Only use `avoid` if the driver should be a last resort.
2. **Use Group Events sparingly.** Grouping large chains of events can drastically reduce solver feasibility (making it "too sticky").
3. **Recommend Tolerance for tight schedules.** If the user complains that events are going unassigned due to 5-10 minute overlaps, recommend adding a `tolerance` rule to allow minor late arrivals.


## User Interface Architecture
- The Rules tab is divided into two sub-tabs: 'Routing Rules' and 'Priority Rules', controlled by Alpine.js state.
- Rule creation forms are located at the top of each sub-tab, before the respective rule lists.
- **Kiosk Mode (`?kiosk=true`)**: A streamlined display state that hides the triage inbox and header chrome while maximizing schedule visibility. In kiosk/read-only mode, driver columns with no real events for a day are hidden; background-trip banners (which are injected into every column for context) do NOT count as real events, so rule-unavailable or trip-absent drivers stay hidden on trip days. The regular editable dashboard always shows every real driver column so events can be dragged onto them. Ghost columns are identified by the exact `ghost_` id prefix (a bare `g` prefix check once hid real drivers whose ids start with "g" on their empty days). Driver columns are ordered: primary group first, then secondary, then `priority_index` within each group (1 = highest, first), alphabetically by name within the same priority; ghost columns always sort last. The event modal shows details only — the Matched Rules / Diagnostics tabs (including unassigned-reason diagnostics and suggestion actions) are suppressed in kiosk mode. The standard bottom-center Argyle chat box remains the single chat entry point (the old floating chat FAB/popup was removed from the dashboard — it survives only in the PWA `app.html`, where it is the mobile chat surface).
- **HA Theme (`?theme=ha`)**: Supports Home Assistant integrations via CSS variable propagation without overwriting the core Tailwind configuration palette. State is persisted across navigation views.
- **Identity colors (single source of truth)**: a person's color everywhere (event bars, passenger pills, driver columns/chips) is the family member's `color_code`. Historically calendar/pill colors were a deterministic hash of the Google calendar id (`calendar.get_calendar_metadata` PALETTE) — nobody chose them, so a member could show a color unrelated to their configured one. `main._apply_identity_colors` overlays member colors onto `calendar_metadata` (the calendar-id keys, the injected passenger-id keys, and — since v2.105.0 — the member-id keys a calendar-only person's events are attributed to) and `main._identity_driver_colors` does the same for the served drivers list; both run at SERVE time on every schedule response path (fresh, daily-cache combine, custom-schedule cache) plus `/api/calendars/metadata`, so a color edit shows immediately without cache rebuilds. Editing a member's color also syncs the legacy driver record's `color_code` (`storage.update_driver_fields`, no cache invalidation). One-time migration `storage.ensure_member_colors()` seeded child members (all at the default `#3b82f6`) with the hash color they were already displaying, stamped `color_seeded` so it never re-fires; adult colors were never touched. Calendars owned by no member (e.g. a shared family calendar) keep the hash color.
- **Voice via Home Assistant Assist ("Hey Argyle")**: `POST /api/v2/converse` accepts `{text, language, conversation_id?}` and returns HA conversation-agent JSON. When HA supplies a `conversation_id` (stable across follow-up turns of one voice session), the endpoint threads history through the same conversation store as `/api/chat` (ids prefixed `voice-`, titled 🎙️ from the first utterance — no LLM naming call), giving voice multi-turn memory; replies also mirror onto the `/api/v2/chat/stream` SSE bubbles. When an agent tool mutates the family schedule (`assign_driver_to_event_fuzzy`, which writes a `ManualOverride` straight to storage, and `remove_override_for_event_fuzzy`, which deletes overrides for the fuzzy-matched event id plus its `_dropoff`/`_pickup` leg variants and returns the event to solver control — added because with no removal tool in the v2 stack the model answered "remove the override" by ASSIGNING someone; both share `_find_event_fuzzy`), `process_agent_request` returns `schedule_dirty: true` and both `/api/chat` and `/api/v2/converse` fire `trigger_background_refresh()` (non-blocking, coalescing) so the solver re-runs and the post-solve `LAST_UPDATE_TIME` bump makes dashboards refetch the NEW schedule — without this the UI sat on the stale cached solve until Sync Now / the 5-minute poll. The agent loop (max 3 iterations; models come from the **free-tier model pools** (`services/model_pools.py`, v2.30.0): the interactive tier walks the flash-lite pool (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` — 500 req/day each, seconds per answer; `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` 20/day tail) then overflows into the gemma pool (`gemma-4-31b-it`/`gemma-4-26b-it`, 14,400/day each but 44-180s/call measured 2026-07-30); the scarce 20/day `gemini-*-flash` models are reserved for the heavy tier and never burned on chat turns. A 429 cools the model down — until midnight Pacific when the error names a per-day quota, else 2 min — so the next turn skips straight to a model with quota left; max 4 candidates per call, 60s read timeout on fast models and 90s on gemma, keeping worst case inside the HA 120s budget. Pools overridable via `model_pool_lite`/`model_pool_flash`/`model_pool_gemma` comma-separated settings keys, no UI; the old `agent_primary_model`/`agent_fallback_model` keys are retired) **early-exits after a round consisting only of successfully completed action tools** (assign/trip/route mutations that return their own spoken confirmation) instead of making a concluding LLM call — timing data showed that extra round re-issued the same action instead of concluding; identical repeated tool calls are also deduped (result reused, never executed twice). Query tools (`get_calendar_events`, `get_my_route`) always loop so the model can use the data; `get_calendar_events` includes each event's `assigned_driver` (name, joined from `assignments` + `ghost_assignments`; null = unassigned) so the agent can answer per-driver schedule questions — without it the model saw events with no drivers and reported empty schedules. **Every agent prompt also carries the FAMILY ROSTER (v2.132.0)** — driver names plus other members, with an explicit note that spoken requests arrive via speech-to-text and an unfamiliar name is far likelier to be one of these misheard than a new person. Without it the model had no way to know a name was wrong: Whisper renders Celma as "Selma" and Vovo as "Volvo", and those went straight into tools that looked for a driver who does not exist. The roster fixes every tool taking a person's name at once. Behind it, `agent_tools_v2._match_person` is the shared resolver for both drivers and members (`_find_member_fuzzy` delegates to it): unique-winner tiers of exact → hashtag (the hand path: add `#selma` to Celma and the mishearing is pinned) → substring/first-name → `_fold_name` sound-folding (soft c→s, ph→f, c/ck→k, z→s, doubles collapsed — this is the Celma case) → difflib ratio ≥0.8 **with a ≥0.06 margin over the runner-up** (the Vovo case, where an inserted consonant defeats folding). The margin is not optional: Grandpa and Grandma score 0.857 against each other, so a bare threshold hands over the wrong grandparent — ambiguity must refuse and name the roster instead. A miss returns the real names in the error so the model retries with one that exists rather than guessing again. The old driver match was substring-only plus a lookup of a `hashtag` key that does not exist on a `Driver` (the field is `hashtags`, a list), so aliases silently never worked. Every agent prompt is injected with `CURRENT DATE & TIME` (weekday, YYYY-MM-DD, HH:MM, server tz) plus an instruction to resolve relative dates before calling tools — without it the model cannot resolve "tonight"/"this week" and guesses wrong date ranges; `assign_driver_to_event_fuzzy` also accepts 'tonight'/'this evening'/'tomorrow night' directly, matching `_parse_fuzzy_date`'s handling for route tools. Keep single-action commands to ONE LLM call: the HA conversation component aborts at 120s, so a 2-call run risks the spoken "couldn't reach Chauffeur" while the server still completes the action. Voice always runs the admin toolset (no source/driver_id). The HA-side glue lives in `homeassistant/custom_components/chauffeur_conversation/` (a ConversationEntity that proxies to the endpoint; installed into HA's `/config/custom_components`, selected as an Assist pipeline's conversation agent with "prefer handling commands locally" ON so built-in HA intents like the garage keep working); setup is documented in `homeassistant/README.md`. **The wake word is NOT part of Chauffeur** — routing happens at the pipeline's conversation agent, so any wake word (or a push-to-talk button) reaches Argyle. Worth knowing before advising on one: the two engines are not interchangeable. openWakeWord runs on the SERVER and takes custom `.tflite` models from `/share/openwakeword/`, but only for satellites that stream audio (ATOM Echo, S3-BOX-3, `wyoming-satellite`). **Home Assistant Voice Preview Edition runs microWakeWord ON-DEVICE and has no setting to hand wake-word processing to HA** — its select only lists models compiled into the firmware (Okay Nabu / Hey Jarvis / Hey Mycroft), so a custom word there means a microWakeWord model, taking control of the device in ESPHome Builder, and flashing. The README said to flip a non-existent "process in Home Assistant" setting until v2.131.2.
- **Live-update resilience (dashboard + PWA)**: both pages render the now-line and clock-based past-event fading at render time only (the dashboard's `isCompleted` at ~line 2858 is `ev.end < now`, despite the name), refreshed whenever `/api/stream` emits `update` (`LAST_UPDATE_TIME` bumps after every solve run, so roughly every 5-minute poll). Because `EventSource` gives up permanently when a reconnect gets a non-200 (e.g. expired ingress session after device sleep), both pages recreate the stream on closure, refetch quietly every 5 minutes as a safety net, and refresh immediately on `visibilitychange` when the tab returns to the foreground. **Day re-anchoring (PWA)**: a resumed PWA keeps the drives-timeline anchor (`window.globalStartDate`), the pane strip's `scrollLeft`, and the My Day offset alive in memory across days, which made the app wake on a stale/seemingly random day. `reanchorIfDayChanged()` runs on `visibilitychange`, the 5-minute tick, and bfcache `pageshow`: when the local calendar day has advanced past the anchored day it resets the anchor to today, zeroes `activeDateIndex`/`scrollLeft`/`mydayOffset`, and re-renders. Same-day resumes keep the user's viewed day; day changes always reopen on Today. Separately, the day-pane strip's horizontal position is tracked by INDEX (`activeDateIndex`), never by raw pixels: an iOS PWA cold relaunch replays stale element scroll offsets from its page snapshot after first paint (which used to open the strip on an arbitrary day). `buildTimeline` snaps the first build to today's pane and positions every rebuild at `activeDateIndex * clientWidth`; scroll events in the first 2s of page life that the user did not initiate (no touch/pointer/nav-button interaction yet) are treated as snapshot replay and forced back to the intended pane. Additionally, WebKit re-snaps a `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` container to an arbitrary pane while children are being inserted — so `buildTimeline` disables snapping (`scrollSnapType='none'`) during the rebuild, inserts all panes as ONE DocumentFragment batch, positions by index, and restores snapping in a `requestAnimationFrame` (re-asserting the position in the same frame). The container deliberately has NO `scroll-smooth` class: programmatic corrections must be instant (nav buttons request smooth behavior explicitly) — with it, guard corrections rendered as a visible slow glide across days.
- **Dynamic Drive Times**: The UI intelligently handles drive-time calculations when errands are toggled off. It accumulates the time of the hidden `A -> Errand` and `Errand -> B` edges so the gap accurately reflects the total actual drive time.
- **Degenerate waypoint collapse**: `matcher.py` only emits a `pickup_waypoint`/`dropoff_waypoint` on a route edge when BOTH legs are > 0 minutes. If the pickup/dropoff point coincides with an endpoint (e.g. a split Pickup event where the passenger is already at the venue, so the "waypoint → event" leg is 0), the edge is emitted as a plain single drive with the same total `travel_mins`, and the timeline renders one pill instead of "13M → Pickup → 0M".
- **Calendar Passenger Resolution**: The `calendar.html` view shares the same deep passenger resolution logic as the dashboard, analyzing `matched_rules` to display non-attendee passengers as colorful pills.
- **PWA View Modes (bottom tab bar)**: The driver PWA (`app.html`) has two views switched by a mobile-style bottom tab bar, persisted in `localStorage` (`chauffeur_view`): **My Drives** (the per-driver drive-leg timeline) and **Family** (a day agenda of ALL family events — passenger calendar pills, assigned-driver chips, unassigned warnings, errands, and ✈️ trip banners — rendered client-side from the same `/api/schedule` payload with no extra API calls; pickup/dropoff/unrolled variants are merged back into base events like `calendar.html` does). Both views share the same swipeable day panes, day navigation, and event-details modal (which gains a Driver row in Family view). Tapping a Family card opens details with an Open-in-Maps action and — for calendar events not already assigned to the logged-in driver (not errands, trips, or trip-suppressed events) — an **Assign to Me** button that creates a `ManualOverride` via `POST /api/overrides` (same contract as the dashboard's driver dropdown), after a `POST /api/overrides/check` dry-run that surfaces solver conflicts in a confirm prompt. The backend re-solves in the background and the SSE stream refreshes the app. For split Dropoff/Pickup events the modal instead offers **per-leg buttons** — "Take Dropoff (time)" / "Take Pickup (time)" plus "Take Both" when neither leg is theirs — each claiming the exact variant id (`{base}_dropoff` / `{base}_pickup`), which the resolver treats as an instance override that beats base-id overrides, so the other leg stays solver-controlled; the Family card and modal Driver row show per-leg driver chips. Recurring events are always claimed instance-only (never keyed to `recurring_event_id`).
- **PWA theming (v2.136.0, `static/theme.css`)**: the PWA is fully tokenized — every Tailwind color the app resolves points at a `--c-{hue}-{shade}` CSS variable (space-separated RGB channels via `rgb(var(--x) / <alpha-value>)`, so opacity modifiers keep working), with **dark** (default, byte-identical to the pre-token app) and **light** (warm cream neutrals) themes switched by `[data-theme]` on `<html>`. Per-device 3-state preference (`chauffeur_theme` in localStorage: auto|light|dark, auto follows `prefers-color-scheme` live) cycled by a header sun/moon/auto button; resolved in `<head>` before first paint (no flash), and the `theme-color` meta moves with the theme so the standalone status bar matches. **Shade semantics** (the contract every new PWA surface must follow): gray/accent shades 50–400 are TEXT (light mode darkens them per-hue to the lightest shade clearing 4.5:1 on the light card — warm hues anchor at 800, cool at 600–700), 500–700 are SOLID FILLS (theme-invariant; white text on them is correct in both themes), 800–950 are TINTED SURFACES (light mode mirrors them pale; light cards sit slightly darker than the page, Skylight-style). `text-white` is reserved for text ON solid accent fills or media overlays and never adapts; surface text is `text-gray-100`. Elevation uses `.shadow-nav`/`.shadow-sheet` (near-black in dark, warm+soft in light). `theme.css` is GENERATED — regenerate via `tools/gen_theme.js` (usage in its header) rather than hand-editing; the companion `tools/audit_contrast.js` resolves every text/bg class pair through both token tables and reports WCAG failures. Other pages (dashboard, home, kiosks) still have their own ad-hoc theming — only `app.html` is on the token layer so far.
- **Panel screensaver (v2.140.0)**: after `panel_screensaver_idle_seconds` (default 600, 0=off; independent of the 180s idle-return) any `?panel=true` page dims into a Ken Burns photo slideshow with a clock. Sources (`panel_screensaver_source`): `photos` (Moments photo attachments + video posters from the app's own media store — the default), `media` (image files under the HA `/media` share, optional `panel_screensaver_media_path` subfolder; the bridge for NAS/Immich/Google-Photos-sync since Google's API no longer allows third-party library reads), `background` (the wallpaper slow-panned). Fallback chain photos/media → wallpaper → gradient+clock; never an error on the wall. **Split contract**: the panel PROFILE carries only the knobs (`screensaver` key); the playlist comes from `GET /api/panel/screensaver` AT ACTIVATION so a panel up for weeks shows this week's photos. `/api/panel/media-image/{path}` serves share images with realpath containment re-checked at both list and serve time (settings subpath and URL are both untrusted). Client (nav.html, panels only): double-buffered layers, randomized pan pool, client-side shuffle, decode-before-show, wake-tap swallowed in capture phase, `visibilitychange` re-arms. Coordination: while active, the idle-return `goHome` DEFERS (`_chfSsPendingHome`) and navigation happens on wake — never mid-slideshow. Overlay z-300 (above hearth/moment overlays at 200-220), styles in panel_skin.html, settings UI in the /home panel-setup drawer. Dwell `panel_screensaver_dwell_seconds` (default 20s, clamp 5-600). **v2.141.0**: `panel_screensaver_enabled` master switch (checkbox in the drawer; folds into `idle_seconds: 0` server-side so clients know one flag, and preserves the tuned seconds while off) + a **Next up card** (top-right, opposite the clock; scrim covers both corners): the board hero's `next` — same "next thing that actually happens" the home board leads with — fetched at activation + every 3 min, countdown recomputed locally on the 15s clock tick, hidden when all_done or >10 min past start. **v2.142.0 — one hero renderer**: the Next-up corner is `components/hero_card.html` (`HeroCard.html(next, {compact})`) — the SAME function the home board's hero band renders through (`heroCardHtml()` via x-html; countdown/tone arithmetic moved into the component, home.html delegates). Compact mode = one right-aligned column with its own translucent backing (it sits on photographs), leave-time-leads layout preserved. Included once from nav.html. Never hand-copy hero markup — v2.126.0's one-renderer lesson, now tested (`scenario_one_hero_renderer_not_a_smaller_copy`). Tests: `tests/test_screensaver.py` (jsdom asserts the big leave number + pill through the shared card) and `tests/test_board_render.py` (the board hero via the same component). **v2.142.2 (family ruling, supersedes the v2.142.1 grace)**: the hero's subject is the EVENT and the event's end time is when it ends — `over = done or end < now`, the live flag NEVER extends an event's life on the board (an away game an hour from home would read "happening now" the whole ride back; whether people are home is presence's business). Same rule client-side: `HeroCard.onNow` reads the clock only. Encoded in `test_home_board.scenario_the_events_end_time_is_when_the_event_ends`. **v2.155.2 — next = the one you leave for first**: `todays_runs` sorts by `(leave_at or start, start, id)` — the departure is the moment a family ACTS, so of two same-start events the earlier leave time is the hero (and an earlier-start event yields to a known earlier departure). The id tail makes the order total: ties used to fall through to the assignments dict's order from the last solve, which flipped the hero between two same-time events on every refetch. One sort, so the hero and the drives tile can't disagree. **v2.155.1**: the compact card's ink is exempt from panel_skin's blanket ink remap (`html[data-panel] .text-white -> --panel-fg`), which turned the title/leave-time navy-on-dark in the LIGHT panel theme — the card sits on a photograph behind its own fixed dark band, and a dark band with theme-following ink is the exact mix the photo-ink note forbids. `#panel-ss-next` keeps the dark-surface vocabulary (white ink, dark pill, dark-theme accent label) whatever the theme.
- **Arrival auto-complete (v2.143.0, `services/drive_arrival.py`)**: a STARTED drive leg completes itself when the tracked driver reaches the leg's destination — the family's fix for stale in_progress flags at the source ("my location is tracked already; my completing the drive could have been recorded"). Swept every 30s from the push loop (`check_arrivals`, cheap no-legs early-exit). Destination: `final_*` legs → home, everything else → the event's location (dropoff/pickup variants fall back to the base event). **Proof requirements, each deliberately strict because a false complete un-tracks a real drive**: leg must be human-started (in_progress — arrival never starts/guesses legs); geocode must be CACHED and street-precision (never buys lookups from a polling loop; 'city' pins prove nothing); the position fix must be fresh (≤15 min) and precise (≤300 m accuracy); distance ≤ max(175 m, fix accuracy). Position source: driver's `ha_person_entity` first, assigned car's `ha_device_tracker` fallback; untracked households keep the manual button, unchanged. No setting — this is data hygiene, not a preference. Tests: `tests/test_drive_arrival.py`.
- **Kid shell (v2.139.0, Buddy-aesthetic arc phase 3b)**: when the signed-in PWA member is a `child`, `applyKidShell()` (called from `applyRoleTabs`, the one hook that always runs on identity change) sets `data-kid` on `<html>` + `--kid-accent` (the member's `color_code`). Theme `auto` resolves LIGHT for the kid shell (head `resolveTheme`); an explicit light/dark pick from the theme button still wins. **Kid My Day** (`renderMyDay` branches on role): greeting header (first name, `today_done/total`, streak, tappable ⭐ balance pill → chores tab; balance+chore stats fetched today-only, fire-and-tolerate), a ring row (SVG progress rings per time-of-day bucket + a chores ring: my done/claimed; empty=dim; taps scroll to `#kid-bucket-{key}` anchors / open chores), and bucketed routine sections — Morning `<12:00` / Afternoon `12–17` / Evening `≥17` / Anytime (no time). **The editing rule that makes big elements work: per bucket only the FIRST unfinished task is the hero card (64px glyph on a `kidTint()` accent band); everything else is a compact row.** Past/future days: rows only, disabled, no header/hero. **Kid chores**: accent-tinted points header, `choreCard` branches to a glyph-forward layout (text-4xl glyph, stacked buttons). Adults/helpers see zero change. Verified by `tools/`-style smoke harness (scratchpad `smoke_kid_day.js` pattern: vm-load the script block, drive pure renderers with fixtures).
- **Routine copy (v2.143.1)**: `POST /api/routines/copy {from_member_id, to_member_id}` copies one member's routine onto another — set one kid up, copy, edit the differences. MERGE with **lineage first** (v2.143.3): every copy records `copied_from` (the source item id), so a copy the target has since retimed/renamed is still recognised and a re-copy never re-imports the original — this resolved the edit-then-recopy balance. Second test: case-insensitive **(title, time_of_day)** content key (same title at two times = two real items, both copy; day masks stay out of the key). Deleting a copy + re-copying brings it back on purpose (re-copy = "make it like theirs again"). Re-copy tops up, never doubles; emoji/time/day-mask ride along; copies get their own ids so check history/streaks never transfer. UI: "⧉ Copy from…" picker in each member's block header on /routines (only lists members who actually have items). Tests: `test_routines.scenario_copy_routine_between_kids` (both storage backends).
- **Task glyphs (v2.138.0, `templates/components/kid_glyphs.html`)**: RoutineItem and Chore carry an optional `emoji` field (create/edit APIs pass it through; `_clean_emoji` caps at 8 chars, blank → None). Render paths resolve via the shared `kidGlyph(item, fallback)` — hand-picked emoji wins, then a title→emoji keyword map (~50 rules, specific-multiword-first), then the caller's fallback (✅). Shown in: PWA routine rows + chore cards, routines-page display rows + kiosk lanes, chores-page admin rows. Editors (routines inline-edit, chores form) have an Emoji input whose PLACEHOLDER is the live keyword guess — blank means "accept the guess". Add new keyword rules to the component, never inline maps; any kid-facing surface that draws a task should include the component so the same title always gets the same glyph.
- **PWA density & accessibility floor (v2.137.0)**: the PWA enforces a **13px type floor** — `text-xs` is redefined to 13px/18px in `app.html`'s tailwind.config and every arbitrary `text-[9..13px]` was collapsed into it (incl. `music_widget.html`); don't reintroduce sub-13px sizes. **Form fields are all ≥16px** (`text-[16px]` on the proposal edit panel, pool chip-in, prompt textarea, Argyle chat input; the rest inherit 16px) — that's what let pinch-zoom come back (viewport `maximum-scale`/`user-scalable` removed; the zoom block was masking the iOS focus-zoom those small inputs triggered). **Touch targets**: icon buttons (header, day-nav arrows, thread back) are `p-3` (44px); JS-card action buttons get a CSS `min-height` floor (`button.rounded-lg` 40px, `button.rounded-xl` 44px — padding classes untouched, hit area only; `rounded-full` deliberately excluded, tiny inline chips' rows are the target); routine/task checkboxes are `w-7 h-7`; count badges `min-w-[20px]`. **Radius scale is softened app-wide** via tailwind.config (`lg` 12px, `xl` 16px, `2xl` 20px, `3xl` 28px — Skylight/Hearth direction, same markup). Body is `h-dvh` (not `h-screen`/100vh, which put the tab bar under dynamic browser chrome). Message thread controls (v2.139.1): the per-message ☺+/⋯ toggles are 18px glyphs in 40px circular hit boxes with negative margin (rhythm cost ~8px, not 24), reaction chips are 15px/28px, and the on-demand popups (quick-react palette, Edit/Delete) use full 40px+ targets since they cost no thread height while closed.
- **Assignment-change push notifications (batched per re-solve run)**: the progressive solver writes the combined cache once per solved day, so each non-draft write COLLECTS its assignment diff into a buffer (`_collect_assignment_changes`, main.py) and the end of the whole background run FLUSHES it (`flush_assignment_notifications`, called in `trigger_background_refresh`'s runner; a 300s fallback timer covers stray paths). Each driver gets at most ONE push per run: detailed `+ gained / - lost` lines (capped at 5) for TODAY's changes, and a single summary line naming affected future dates ("Also changes on: Sun 08/02, Tue 08/04" / title "Schedule Updated" when future-only). Per event the buffer keeps first-old vs latest-new driver, so churn within a run nets out to silence. Only events present in both snapshots count (calendar adds/removals don't fire), upcoming events only. A gain from the driver's own PWA claim is suppressed via the override's `source: 'pwa'` field (matched by event id, `original_event_id`, or `recurring_event_id`); losses always notify. Today's changes also land as telemetry (`assigned`/`removed`, removed renders red in the PWA bell) plus one `updated` summary entry for future dates.
- **Evening "tomorrow" digest**: once per day after `tomorrow_digest_time` (Settings, default `20:00`; disable via `tomorrow_digest_enabled: false`), the push loop builds each driver one digest listing tomorrow's assignments sorted by time (events + scheduled errands, capped at 6 lines). **Empty-state confirmation (v2.56.8)**: a driver who drove on any of the last 7 settled days (from the durable `daily_stats` snapshots — today's isn't taken until 21:00, after the 20:00 send) but has nothing tomorrow still gets a "🎉 You're free — nothing on the schedule." note, so a silent evening never reads as a broken digest (an empty day and a failed send used to be indistinguishable). Drivers with no recent activity stay silent — no nightly "nothing" ping. Scope decided as a delivery policy in `_send_tomorrow_digests`; the shared `build_drive_digests` content builder stays pure (real drives only), so the `get_drive_digest` agent tool is unaffected. **Delivery as of v2.29.2 is the driver's Argyle DM** (`_post_chat_message` as the Argyle member into `get_or_create_dm(argyle, member)` — persistent/scrollable, and the chat fan-out delivers the push + HA notify for free); the raw web-push (title `Tomorrow: N drives`) survives only as the fallback for drivers with no linked member record (and still requires a push subscription). The already-sent marker persists in the new `app_state` storage table (`get_app_state`/`set_app_state`), so restarts don't re-send. Event lines append prep-kit items (` (bring: …)`, v2.28.0). **Builder/delivery split (v2.32.1, generalized v2.32.2)**: the digest CONTENT comes from `family_digest.build_drive_digests(target_date=None)` (default TOMORROW; `{date, label, weather, drivers: {driver_id: {title, lines, count}}}`, titles label-aware "Today:/Tomorrow:/Fri 08/07: N drives"); `main._send_tomorrow_digests` only delivers. The same builder feeds the **`get_drive_digest` agent tool in BOTH stacks** — a READ-ONLY answer for ANY day (`target_date` 'today' default / 'tomorrow' / weekday / YYYY-MM-DD via `_parse_fuzzy_date`; `member_name` optional, driver chat defaults to the logged-in driver, never posts to any channel; terminal in the router). Lesson (two releases of crossed wires): with only `post_weekly_digest` tool-shaped, 'show me the tomorrow digest' fuzzy-matched to it and BROADCAST the weekly recap into the family chat (fixed v2.32.1 with a tomorrow-only tool) — whereupon 'today's digest' matched the tomorrow tool. Digest-shaped asks are a FAMILY of day requests: one general tool with a date param beats stacking per-day tools, and both digest tool descriptions explicitly disambiguate weekly-recap vs day-preview. **Weather line (v2.28.1)**: the digest body is topped with a one-line forecast (`family_digest.weather_line(date)`, moved from main.py in v2.32.1: condition emoji + high/low + rain %) from `ha_api.get_weather_forecast(entity_id, kind='daily')` — a `weather.get_forecasts` service call with `return_response`, auto-detecting the first `weather.*` entity when the `weather_entity` setting is empty; any HA failure returns `[]` and the line silently drops (weather never blocks the digest). At ≥50% rain probability the line appends "— pack rain gear ☔" (v2.32.0 — say the actionable thing, not just the number). Config → General gained a **Digests & Weather** section: Tomorrow Digest toggle + send-after time (first UI for these settings) and a weather-entity dropdown fed by `GET /api/ha/entities?domain=weather`. Tests: `tests/test_weather.py`.
- **Weekly family digest (v2.28.3, `services/family_digest.py`)**: once a week (Settings `weekly_digest_enabled` default on, `weekly_digest_day` 0=Mon..6=Sun default Sunday, `weekly_digest_time` default 19:00; UI in Config → General → Digests & Weather) **Argyle posts a "📊 Family Week in Review" into the family chat channel** — reusing the messaging rails means SSE + push/HA fan-out and a permanent readable thread come free, no new surface. Sections (each omitted when empty; nothing at all → no post): 🚗 driving per driver (drives + occupied minutes), 🏃 kid activity counts, ✅ chores verified + points earned per kid (positive `reason='chore'` ledger entries only), 🎁 rewards granted, 📋 routine days-complete + current streak. **Data**: the schedule cache is forward-looking, so a `daily_stats` snapshot row (per-driver drives/minutes incl. errands, ghost assignments excluded; per-kid activity counts using My Day's three-way passenger binding with split legs collapsed to one activity) is recorded once each evening (push loop, ≥21:00, `daily_stats_last_date` marker) via `record_daily_stats` (upsert by date — `post_weekly_digest` re-records the send day first so it counts); chores/rewards/routines need no snapshots (durable ledgers). The weekly gate sets its `weekly_digest_last_sent` marker BEFORE posting so a half-failing post can't retry every 30s and spam the channel. **Formatting (v2.28.5)**: sections are blank-line-separated blocks with a header line and one `•` bullet per person — and PWA chat bubbles gained `whitespace-pre-wrap`, so newlines render in ALL chat messages (they previously collapsed into one run-on blob, which is how the first digest looked). **On-demand resend (v2.28.6)**: `post_weekly_digest` agent tool in BOTH stacks (`agent_tools_v2.post_weekly_digest_now`, terminal in the router) — "@argyle send the weekly digest" posts it right now via the same builder; children/helpers are refused (family chat gates on `acting_member`, PWA driver chat resolves the logged-in driver's member so a helper driver can't broadcast; the no-actor dashboard/HA-voice context is trusted open-admin), and an empty week answers "nothing to report" instead of posting. Tests: `tests/test_family_digest.py`, `tests/test_agent_family_tools.py`.
- **Proactive parent watchers (v2.32.0, `services/watchers.py`)**: a deterministic sweep (push loop, every 30 min, `watchers_last_run` marker set BEFORE the run — same spam-guard reasoning as the weekly digest; runs via `asyncio.to_thread` so a slow LLM call never blocks pending pushes) that surfaces STUCK state to parents as **ONE consolidated Argyle heads-up DM** (all role=parent members; the DM rails give push + HA fan-out free). Findings: 🚨 unassigned events in the next 3 days (from the cache's `unassigned`, skipping trip-suppressed/errands), 📥 intake proposals pending 3+ days, ✅ done chores awaiting verification 48h+, 🧹 open chores unclaimed 7+ days (batched into one line), 🎁 reward requests pending 48h+, 🛒 past-due errands, and a **weekly** 🎒 prep-kit idea check — the sweep's only LLM use (`prep_kits.suggest_kits` grew a `tier` param; the watcher passes `'background'` so gemma quota is burned, never the interactive lite pool; suggested kit names are fingerprinted in `prep_suggest_seen` so an idea is never re-announced; any failure skips silently). **Anti-nag guarantees**: every finding notifies exactly once (`watcher_notified` dict in app_state, keys pruned after 45 days; marked only after a successful post), one consolidated DM per parent per sweep, quiet hours 21:00–08:00 (findings defer to the morning sweep, never dropped). The stale-state nudges are deliberately the SAFETY NET behind the instant lifecycle notifications (chore done / redemption requested already push immediately) — hence the grace periods. Master toggle `proactive_watchers_enabled` (Settings, default on; UI block in Config → General → Digests & Weather). Funded-pool nudges were deliberately excluded (pool-fill already notifies parents instantly and there is no funded-since timestamp to age against). Tests: `tests/test_watchers.py`.
- **Kid evening digest (v2.33.0, kid-support arc K1 — design brief `docs/kid_support_design.md`)**: every child gets a "🌙 Tomorrow, {name}!" preview built by `main._build_kid_digests(target_date=None)` (default tomorrow) — which **reuses `member_day`'s ride resolution verbatim** (three-way passenger binding, split-leg collapse, per-leg drivers, prep items) so the digest always matches the kid's My Day. Per ride: time, title, a REASSURANCE driver phrase ("🚗 Dad is driving you"; split legs with different drivers → "Dad takes you, Mom brings you home"; an UNASSIGNED ride deliberately omits the phrase — the parent watcher chases missing drivers, a kid digest must never alarm), and `(bring: …)` prep items. Plus the weather line and a routine/streak line ("📋 2 routine things tomorrow — 🔥 6-day streak, keep it going!"). **Kids with no rides AND no routine items that day are omitted entirely** (nothing means nothing, never an empty ping). **Dual delivery — decided 2026-08-05: support phone and phone-less kids simultaneously, no per-kid config**: (1) one **Argyle DM per child** (`_send_kid_digests`, push loop, once per day after `kid_digest_time` default 19:30, marker-first `kid_digest_last_sent`; the DM rails push to kids with devices for free and the thread waits for shared devices); (2) the SAME builder feeds `GET /api/kids/digests` (`?date=today|tomorrow|YYYY-MM-DD`), rendered as a **🌙 per-kid card strip on the `/routines?kiosk=true` board** (below the lanes, independent of routine lanes so routine-less kids still appear; own 5-min refresh cadence). **Kid quiet hours** (`kid_quiet_start`/`kid_quiet_end`, default 20:30–07:00, wraps midnight, equal=disabled; `family_digest.in_kid_quiet_hours` — the gate for the digest AND every future kid-facing push, K2+): a digest time inside the quiet window never fires that day (deliberate — a "Tomorrow" digest deferred to the next morning would preview the wrong day). Settings + UI in Config → General → Digests & Weather ("🌙 Kid Digest" block: toggle, send-after, quiet-hours range). Tests: `tests/test_kid_digest.py`.
- **Kid pickup-clarity pushes (v2.33.1, kid-support arc K2)**: two push moments answering "who's getting me?", both delivered via `_notify_member_lanes` (member-keyed web push + HA notify) to child members bound to the event by My Day's three-way passenger binding (`main._kid_members_for_event` over `family_digest._kid_event_match`). (1) **On-the-way** (`_notify_kids_ride_started(event_id)`): "🚗 Dad is on the way!" the moment the ride's FIRST drive leg goes `in_progress` — hooked into BOTH start paths, the PWA Start Drive button (`POST /api/drive_status`, background task, leg id collapsed via `_leg_event_id`) and the agent `start_route` tool (lazy `import main`, silently skipped in tests); deduped once per event per day via the `ride_started_notified` app_state marker (pruned after 2 days) so route/final legs stay quiet. (2) **Driver-change** (`_notify_kids_driver_changes(buffered)`): "Mom is taking you to Swim Practice tomorrow at 4:00 PM 🚗" rides the netted assignment-change flush buffer (`flush_assignment_notifications`), so churn within a re-solve nets to silence. **Rules of calm** (deliberate): GAINS ONLY — a ride losing its driver never alarms the kid (parent watchers chase unassigned events); next-48h only — far-future churn is noise a kid can't act on (the evening digest covers it); ghost drivers never notify; kid quiet hours SKIP rather than defer on both paths (a stale on-the-way push is worse than none; changes reappear in the digests). Tests: `tests/test_kid_pushes.py`.
- **Kid-as-sensor proposals (v2.33.2, kid-support arc K3)**: kids are often FIRST to know logistics changes; their reports now have a parent-gated path in. The family-chat agent already offered `propose_family_action` to every member (approval was always the gate) — K3 adds the two missing pieces. (1) **Card visibility**: a proposal card created from a child's PRIVATE Argyle DM was invisible to the approvers — `_run_argyle_mention` now mirrors the card into the family channel ("💡 Addison flagged this for a parent:", normal fan-out notifies parents) and RE-BINDS the proposal's `channel_id` to the family channel so the Approve outcome follow-up lands where parents saw the card. Mirror fires only for kind='dm' + sender role='child' (family/group/event mentions are already visible; a parent's own DM proposal stays private to them). (2) **Kid persona** (router prompt, `role == 'child'` branch): treat reports as valuable, propose via card (create_event / add_errand / reassign_driver mappings) and SAY you've flagged it for the parents, at most ONE clarifying question (missing info → propose anyway and note the gap in the summary), warm and brief, never lecture or guilt-trip about chores/points/streaks, never execute directly for a child. The tool description also names kid-report phrasings so the router matches them. Child Approve taps remain refused server-side (`requires_admin`). Tests: `tests/test_kid_sensor.py`.
- **Kid school tasks (v2.34.0, kid-support arc K4a — full K4 design `docs/k4_school_design.md`)**: a kid-owned school/deadline domain. **KidTask** (`kid_tasks` table): member_id (child), title, `due_date` YYYY-MM-DD, `kind` homework|test|project|bring|other (emoji 📚📝📐🎒📌), source manual|agent|intake|ics|classroom (+`source_ref` reserved for K4b feed dedupe), status open|done. **Scope guards (deliberate)**: due dates only — NEVER grades or missing-assignment shaming; NO points and NO streaks (school pressure is what we're reducing); overdue is worded gently ("still open — was due Fri", `_task_due_label`) and shown in place, never pushed. REST: `GET/POST /api/kid-tasks`, `PUT/DELETE /api/kid-tasks/{id}`, `POST .../complete` (per-action identity: a child 403s on a sibling's task; parents can check off or reopen). **Surfaces**: My Day gains `due_soon` (open tasks due within 7 days of the viewed date + overdue, sorted overdue-first) rendered as a "📚 Due Soon" checkbox card in the PWA (strike-through in place on check, row leaves on next render); the kid digest + kiosk cards append task lines (due within 3 days of the digest day + overdue). **Agent tools in BOTH stacks** (`get_kid_tasks`/`add_kid_task`/`complete_kid_task`, all terminal): kids manage their OWN list directly — no approval friction, it's their list (a kid naming a sibling is refused); parents name any child (two kids + no name → ask, never guess); helpers refused; `add_kid_task` resolves fuzzy due dates ('Friday') via `_parse_fuzzy_date`. Kid-persona prompt additions: school list is direct (vs. propose_family_action for calendar/rides), OFFER milestone breakdowns for far-out projects (add only on yes), never nag about overdue. K4c (school hours → end-of-day pickup push) is next per the design doc. Tests: `tests/test_kid_tasks.py`.
- **Kid-task intake (v2.34.1, kid-support arc K4b)**: the task domain now fills itself. (1) **Task-mode ICS feeds**: `ics_feeds` gained `target_kind: calendar|tasks` + `member_id` — an assignment feed (Canvas/Schoology per-student URL) lands on the kid's school list as due-dated KidTasks, NEVER on a calendar (assignment "events" would pollute the driving solver; `_sync_feed_tasks` in ics_sync.py never touches gcal). Diff semantics mirror calendar mode via `source_ref = "{feed_id}:{ics_key}"`: new item → open task, title/due change on an OPEN task → patch, vanished FUTURE item → delete; **a DONE task is final** — never patched, resurrected, or deleted (the kid finished it); ancient items (> PAST_GRACE old) never import; kind heuristic `_task_kind_for` (test/quiz/exam → 📝, project → 📐, bring/wear/return → 🎒, else 📚). Unsubscribe cleanup (`remove_feed_events`) deletes only OPEN tasks. Create validation: task feeds require a child member; calendar feeds still require a calendar. UI: the passenger-profile ICS block's target select gains "📚 School task list ({kid})" when the member is a child (feed rows show "N 📚 tasks"; task feeds list under the member, not a calendar; `icsFeedsFor(calIds, member)`). (2) **Email-intake task routing**: `POST /api/proposals/{id}/approve` accepts `calendar_id: "tasks:{member_id}"` — a To-do proposal approved there becomes a KidTask (source='intake', source_ref=proposal_id, notes preserved) instead of an all-day 📌 calendar event; both approval surfaces (/intake page + PWA parent queue) list "📚 {kid}'s school list" options for `kind='task'` proposals only (events still need calendars). (3) **Google Classroom: deliberately deferred** — this district has feeds, and Classroom needs a per-kid OAuth consent flow (a real build); revisit if a feed-less school appears. Tests: `tests/test_kid_task_intake.py`.
- **School hours + dismissal push + morning launch (v2.35.0, kid-support arc K4c+K5 — the arc's finale)**: `FamilyMember` gains optional `school_hours_start`/`school_hours_end` (HH:MM; Config → People identity editor shows the "🏫 School hours" inputs for child-role members; flows through the free-form member PUT). They unlock two moments: (1) **School-day-end pickup push** (`_send_school_end_push`, push loop): weekdays only, once per child per day at dismissal (`school_end_push_sent` app_state marker), the kid's first ride starting within 3h after school end with a KNOWN driver → "🚗 Dad has you after school / Swim Practice at 4:00 PM" (split rides name the dropoff-leg driver). **Silence rules**: no ride, ride beyond the horizon, or unknown driver → send NOTHING (a 'nobody scheduled' push would alarm; silence is what a quiet afternoon looks like); a >45-min-late window (server was down) stays silent — stale pickup info is worse than none; kid quiet hours honored. (2) **Morning launch line** (K5): `member_day` gains `launch` — when the day's FIRST ride's assigned driver starts from home (the solver's `initial_edges[driver][event]` covers exactly that case; split rides check the `_dropoff` leg id), leave-by = start − `travel_mins` − `buffer_before_mins`. Rendered as a "🚀 Leave by 7:35 AM with Dad" card atop the PWA My Day, and the kid digest/kiosk cards LEAD with the same line. No edge (driver mid-chain, unknown route) or ghost-only suggestion → no line — the ride card already shows its start; never fabricate precision. Tests: `tests/test_kid_school_hours.py`. **This completes the kid-support arc as designed (K1–K5)**.
- **Kid-board polish (v2.35.1, family feedback 2026-08-06)**: (1) the routines-kiosk **digest strip moved ABOVE the routine lanes** — the day preview is what kids should see first (header emoji is ☀️ for Today, 🌙 for Tomorrow). (2) The kiosk strip's default day is now **TODAY until a configurable cutover, then TOMORROW** (`kid_digest_cutover_time`, default 19:00, Settings + "Board shows tomorrow after" input in the Config kid-digest block; `_kid_digest_default_date` powers `GET /api/kids/digests` when no `?date=` is passed — an explicit date always wins). A "Tomorrow" board at 3pm answers the wrong question; the evening Argyle DM is unchanged (it IS the look-ahead). (3) The **chores-kiosk lane order** became points header → Family Goals → Rewards → My chores → Available — a long chore pot was pushing the rewards off the bottom of the wall panel, hiding the incentive (the PWA Chores tab already led with rewards; unchanged).
