- **Errands light-theme fix / ha_theme opacity coverage (v2.52.1, family feedback)**: the errands Inbox showed a hard-dark properties bar (`bg-gray-800/80`) and "All caught up!" empty-state box (`bg-gray-800/50`) on light HA themes — the `ha_theme.html` gray-utility mapper only covered the `/40` and `/60` opacity steps. The mapper now covers the full common opacity range (`/30 /40 /50 /60 /70 /80 /90`) for `bg-gray-900/800/700`, adds the opacity-suffixed gray BORDER variants (`border-gray-800/700/600/NN` → divider — this also fixed slightly-dark borders on the chores/routines kiosk boards), and maps `bg-red-900/40,50` (mirroring the existing blue rule) so the errands edit-modal "prohibited" constraint toggles stay legible. Reinforces the standing rule from v2.35.2: any surface must use ha_theme-mapped utility classes or add its classes to the mapper. (1) **Person toggles now hide assigned events.** The calendar filter counted the assigned driver as a participant, so any ride with a visible parent-driver survived every passenger toggle (only driverless trip-suppressed events ever disappeared). Passengers now define who an event is "for"; the driver counts only when the event has no passengers (their own event). (2) **Event-details modal readable on light HA themes**: the modal's `text-white/40..80` labels are Tailwind opacity-variant classes that `ha_theme.html`'s bare `.text-white` remap never matched — now remapped (modal-scoped only; the calendar grid's white/NN chips sit on event-colored backgrounds and must stay white) to `color-mix` of `--ha-primary-text`. (3) **Vehicle edit form populates Usual Driver**: the select's options are `x-for`-generated from `allowed_driver_ids`, and a native `<select>` silently drops a value applied before its option exists — `editCar` now sets `default_driver_id` in a `$nextTick` after the options render. (1) calendar legend **driver pills were hardcoded `#1F2937`** (dark slate — an unreadable blob on light HA themes); they now use the driver's real `color_code`. The **hidden-pill state** switched from 40%-opacity grayscale (ghost-white text, unreadable light, ambiguous dark) to a tinted outline in the member's own color (12% color-mix bg + colored border/text) — readable in both themes and still color-coded. (2) `ha_theme.html`'s Tailwind→HA-variable mapper gained the utilities the kid boards use: **opacity-suffixed grays** (`bg-gray-900/30..60` → bg, `bg-gray-800/40,60` → card, `bg-gray-700/40,60` → header — these staying hard-dark while mapped classes went light was the "more gray than white" mismatch on Chores/Routines), accent borders (`border-indigo-900/60` etc. → divider), and accent text (indigo/emerald/green/orange/red/blue-400 → color-mix toward the theme's text color, keeping contrast without losing the color coding). Rule of thumb recorded: **any new page surface must stick to mapped utility classes or add its classes to ha_theme.html** — the kiosk runs inside HA in whatever theme the family picked.
- **Trips in the kid digest: span-aware + away-window suppression (v2.43.0)**: a week at camp read as "2:00 PM – Camp Kesem" — a one-hour-looking line (family feedback). The digest builder now intercepts `background_trip` rides: `_kid_trip_span` recovers the full span from the cache's sibling `_slice_N` events (window-clipped spans are honest-but-short), and `_kid_trip_line` renders by phase — departure day **"🧳 Camp Kesem — 7-day adventure begins! 🎉 Arriving at 2:00 PM"** (destination-time convention: trip start/end EXCLUDE travel — start = arrival there, end = departure from there; + "pack: …" prep items), mid-trip **"🏕️ Camp Kesem — day 3 of 7"**, last day **"🏠 Coming home from Camp Kesem!"**. Trip lines lead the digest (after the leave-by line) and flow to the DM and kiosk cards identically. **Away-window suppression** (same feedback): home events whose start falls inside the kid's trip span are DROPPED from the digest — nothing during camp says "5:00 PM – Academy"; a post-departure event on leave day is dropped, a post-return event on the homecoming day is kept. Tests: `scenario_trip_digest_lines`.
- **Kid binding fix: event-config attendance now reaches every kid surface (v2.42.1)**: kids attached to an event via the **event config / triage inbox** (`config.passenger_ids` — the solver's FIRST-priority attendance source) were invisible to My Day, the kid digest, the kiosk cards, and the kid pushes — the kiosk showed "No rides — free day! 🎉" while the calendar showed a full day (user screenshot). Root cause: the kid-side binding (`member_day`, `family_digest._kid_event_match`) was three-way (passenger calendars / hashtags / matched_rules), but configs aren't rules and never reach `matched_rules`. Fix exploits an existing invariant: the solve pipeline REPLACES a matched event's cached `calendar_ids` with the RESOLVED passenger ids (`e.calendar_ids = [p.id …]`, originals kept in `original_calendar_ids`) — so both match sites gained a fourth check, **resolved-passenger-id membership in the cached calendar_ids**, which authoritatively covers every binding path the solver honored (config, rule, calendar, hashtag). Regression test: `scenario_config_bound_event_binds_kid` in `tests/test_kid_school_hours.py`.
- **School-year auto-detection + config layout fix (v2.42.0)**: the school year is now **pulled from the calendar itself** (family feedback: "the calendar has First/Last Day of School events — just pull those"). `school.py` scans the designated calendar (one ±370-day fetch shared with closure detection, 6h cache) for marker events — `FIRST_DAY_KEYWORDS` ("first day of school/for students", "first day of class(es)", "school begins/starts", "classes begin", "1st day of school") and `LAST_DAY_KEYWORDS` ("last day of school/for students", "last day of class(es)", "school ends", "classes end", "end of school year"), all-day OR timed — and pairs consecutive first→last dates into school-year intervals (`_pair_intervals`); a first day with no published last day gets an open-ended ~330-day interval. **(v2.51.0)** the "class(es)" wordings were added after a real district's "Last day of classes" went unmatched — the silent miss surfaced as a synthetic first-day+330d year end (a July date with nothing on the calendar) presented as "detected". Intervals are now `(start, end, end_estimated)` triples; `detected_years` carries the flag and the config status line appends "(end estimated — no last-day event recognized)" so a synthetic end is never mistaken for a real event again. Days outside every interval (summer, between years) are out. The `school_year_start/end` settings became a **manual override**: setting either disables auto-detection entirely (the parent took control) and they remain the fallback for calendars without recognizable markers. **Silent-miss protection**: `GET /api/school/status` reports in-session-today, detected years, and cached closure count, rendered as a live status line in the Config 🏫 block ("✅ School is in session today. Detected school year: Aug 12, 2026 – May 28, 2027. 23 no-school days recognized." — or a ⚠️ telling the parent to set the override when no markers were found). Also fixed (user screenshot): the **🌙 Kid Digest settings row** rendered its description as a one-word-per-line sliver — the shrink-0 controls and the paragraph shared one flex row; controls now sit in their own wrapped row above the text. Tests: `tests/test_school.py` (+2 scenarios: marker pairing/summer-between-years/timed markers, open-ended year + manual-override-wins).
- **School-calendar awareness + platform-agnostic bus entities (v2.41.0)**: (1) **`services/school.py` — `school_in_session(day)`**, the single primitive every school-day feature now gates on (the K4c dismissal-push scheduler and the bus launch line — both previously fired on holidays and all summer, a gap dating to v2.35.0). Three layers: weekends never; optional **year bounds** (settings `school_year_start`/`school_year_end`, updated once a year — how summer is known WITHOUT guessing from event titles); optional **designated school calendar** (settings `school_calendar_id` — any calendar in the system incl. an ICS-fed district calendar; Config → General → "🏫 School Calendar" block with calendar dropdown, year dates, keyword list): an ALL-DAY event whose title contains a no-school keyword (`school_closed_keywords`, default "no school, holiday, break, closed, teacher workday, staff development, conferences") marks the day out; multi-day spans expand (Google's exclusive ends honored); timed events never close a school day. Nothing configured = plain weekday behavior. Lookup cached 6h over a ~10-week window (callers are hot paths: member_day, the 30s scheduler), 10-min retry backoff, stale cache served on failure, and finally **fails OPEN** — a broken calendar fetch must never silently kill the features. Settings keys added to the `Settings` model (the merge endpoint drops undeclared keys — `exclude_unset`). **v2.41.1**: the school-calendar picker gained a free-text "any calendar ID" input alongside the family-calendar dropdown — the fetch is native Google Calendar API (service account), so a district's PUBLIC Google calendar works by pasted ID with no sharing (a private one must be shared to the service-account email like the family calendars); crucially the district calendar must NOT be added to `calendar_ids`, which would pour school events into the solver. (2) **Bus live-data is now platform-agnostic**: per-kid explicit entity fields `bus_am_eta_entity`/`bus_pm_eta_entity`/`bus_active_entity` (member editor, collapsed "Other bus platforms" details) accept ANY HA integration's stop-ETA sensor (HH:MM or ISO-timestamp states — `_parse_time_state` handles both) and is-running binary sensor; blank = HCTB first-name auto-discovery as before. Chauffeur still never speaks any bus vendor's API directly. Tests: `tests/test_school.py` (+ bus/kid suites green).
- **School-bus support (v2.40.0, bus arc B1 — `services/bus.py`)**: bus kids were the majority case the kid arc's car-centric launch/dismissal machinery skipped. Chauffeur does NOT track buses — the family's Here Comes The Bus data reaches HA via the pcartwright81 HCTB integration (per-student entities named by first name: `sensor.{first}_bus_am/pm_stop_arrival_time`, `binary_sensor.{first}_bus_in_service/_ignition`, a `device_tracker`); Chauffeur is the TRANSLATION layer and the bus NEVER enters the solver (kid-facing info, like weather). **Config** (Config → People, child members, "🚌 School bus" block): `bus_am_stop_time` HH:MM (the opt-in switch for the whole feature), `bus_pm_stop_time` (optional), `bus_walk_mins` (default 5), `bus_entity_prefix` (HCTB entity prefix override; blank = the member's lowercase first name — auto-discovery). **Surfaces**: (1) `member_day` produces a **bus launch** dict (same `launch` shape, `bus: true`, `driver: null`, `title: "🚌 Bus at 7:22 AM"`, leave-by = stop − walk) when no car launch exists — flowing unchanged into the PWA 🚀 card, the kid digest, and the kiosk strip; skipped on weekends and whenever a car ride starts before school start (a bus line under a "Dad is driving you" card would contradict it). (2) The kid digest/kiosk leads with "🚌 Bus at 7:22 AM — out the door by 7:17 AM" — and unlike the car launch line it stands alone with zero ride lines (the bus IS the ride), so bus kids appear on the board every school day. (3) The dismissal push's no-car-ride fall-through now answers "🚌 Bus home today" (live PM estimate while the bus is out > static drop time > plain reassurance) — kids with no bus config keep the original silence. **Live rules**: HCTB estimates apply only for TODAY and only while `bus_active()` (in-service/ignition on); lateness is reported only ≥4 min beyond the static baseline (estimate jitter is not news) and is worded as permission to relax ("running ~6 min late — no rush"), never urgency. Everything degrades to static (or silence) without HA. Tests: `tests/test_bus.py`. B2 (kiosk live chip during the AM run, opt-in get-ready push, running-late push) is designed but not yet built — see roadmap.
- **Intake vision capture (v2.39.0, intake phase 3 — THE INTAKE ARC AS DESIGNED IS NOW COMPLETE)**: photos and screenshots feed the same proposal queue as email. `email_ingest.run_photo_ingest(image_b64, mime, caption)` runs the SAME extraction prompt/normalize/dedupe/propose pipeline with the image attached; proposals get `source='photo'`, `source_from=''` (no sender → no learned-route write; prefill = member guess > default calendar), and the activity log rows show `from='📸 photo'`. **Model path**: `llm._call_llm_json` gained an `images` param (Gemini `inline_data` parts; ollama ignores) threaded through `model_pools.call_pool_json`, and a new **'vision' tier chain `['flash', 'lite']`** — gemma is text-only and excluded; flash first because flyers/screenshots are the hard case and family volume (dozens/week) sits comfortably inside the ~100/day flash quota. **Capture surfaces**: "📸 Add from photo" on /intake; the PWA parent Family-view intake strip is now ALWAYS visible for parents (collapses to a one-row "📸 Snap a flyer" strip when nothing is pending); and an **Android PWA share-target** (`manifest.json share_target` → `POST /share`, first image only, title/text become the caption, 303 → `app?view=family`) — iOS has never supported PWA share targets, so the in-app buttons are the iOS path. New deps/limits: `python-multipart` (requirements.txt — add-on rebuild picks it up), 8MB image cap, images-only. Known v1 extraction ceiling (same as email): single + simple-weekly events; recurrence-with-exceptions is where extraction embarrasses.
- **Intake: learned routing priors + weekly digest section (v2.38.1, intake phase-2 items a+d, both reshaped)**: (a) was originally "learned per-sender/topic priors" — built DETERMINISTIC, no LLM/training: approving a proposal records sender → target in app_state `intake_learned_routes` (`main._record_intake_feedback`, last approval wins) and the ingest prefill tiers became explicit sender default > **learned prior** (`email_ingest.learned_route` — never prefills 'errand' since it needs per-proposal fields; `tasks:` targets only prefill for kind=task) > LLM member guess > default calendar. Ignoring increments `intake_ignore_streaks[sender]` (approval resets it); at 3+ consecutive ignores `GET /api/proposals` stamps `sender_ignores` and both approval surfaces show a "🤔 you've ignored the last N from this sender" hint (visual only — never auto-suppression, and the real fix is upstream Gmail filters). (d) was originally "a scheduled weekly digest push" — cut as a redundant channel (watchers already nudge stale pending items): instead the **weekly family digest gained a "📬 Intake" section** (new proposals this week with approved/ignored counts + still-pending count; `build_weekly_digest`). Tests: `test_family_digest.py`, `test_kid_task_intake.py` green.
- **Intake: edit-before-approve + errand target (v2.38.0, intake phase-2 items b+c)**: both approval surfaces (/intake cards and the PWA parent queue) gained (1) **inline editing** — an "✎ Edit details" toggle exposes title/date/start–end time fields initialized from the extraction; clearing the start time makes the item all-day (and adding one to a To-do makes it a timed event). The approve body now always carries the card's current values plus an explicit `all_day` (`ProposalApprove` gained `all_day/location/duration_mins`; the endpoint honors `all_day` overrides instead of trusting the stored flag), so a wrong extraction is fixed in place instead of ignore + manual re-entry; in-flight edits survive the surfaces' quiet refreshes. (2) **A '🚗 Drive errand' approval target** (`calendar_id='errand'`) for both kinds: revealing location (prefilled from the proposal, required) + duration (default 30m) fields, approval builds an `Errand` via the same `create_errand` path as /api/errands (ErrandRules apply), with `window_days` = days until the proposal's date (inclusive) so the solver must fit it before the deadline; title strips the 📌 prefix; proposal records `created_errand_id`. Tests: `tests/test_kid_task_intake.py` still green (tasks: path unchanged). the Calendar tab gained a fourth display mode, **Agenda** — a compact multi-day grid (day cards in a responsive `auto-fill minmax(270px)` grid; each card lists its events ordered by start time, all-day pinned first as "Entire day") for the quick day/week scan the proportional timeline views can't give. Implementation: the FullCalendar `events` callback body was extracted into a shared `fetchCalEvents(startStr, endStr)` (same api/schedule fetch, split-leg/unrolled merge, passenger resolution, legend population, member filtering) and the `eventClick` body into a shared `openEventModal(evObj)` (normalizes FC EventApi vs plain objects with ISO-string dates) — so the Agenda view is a second renderer over the identical pipeline, and **tapping an agenda event opens the same details dialog** (the modal's Event Configuration button now hides in kiosk mode; note the calendar has never supported dragging — FC runs non-`editable`, edits happen via the config modal). Agenda chrome: prev/next/today paging by the day-count, a 3/5/7/10/14-day select (localStorage `chauffeur_agenda_days`), Month/Week/Day/Agenda switcher, TODAY card ringed in the accent color, "Today"/"Tomorrow" labels. **Per-day weather chips** ("88° / 77° 🌧️") from new `GET /api/weather/daily?days=N&start=YYYY-MM-DD` (ha_api daily forecast + the digest emoji map; failure → `[]` → chips silently omitted). **URL-forced views**: `?view=month|week|day|agenda` (+`agenda_days=N`) pins the calendar page to a view so each HA kiosk card can BE a specific calendar view — no param falls back to the last-used view (localStorage `chauffeur_cal_view`), then month. **Persistence rule (v2.37.2)**: localStorage is shared across the whole origin (under HA ingress that means every card iframe AND every tab in that browser), so a URL-forced load NEVER writes to it — only manual view/day-count changes on an unconfigured page are remembered. Without this, one `?view=agenda` card load flipped the remembered default for all future plain calendar tabs. Event rows use a 4px member-color left bar (red = unassigned + "⚠️ Needs driver", driver chip on the right, amber "Errand"), themed via `.agenda-*` classes with explicit `body.ha-theme` overrides. Calendar trip banners (JS-built links) also now carry the page's query params into `/trip`, same fix as trips.html in v2.36.0.
- **Kiosk theming completion + kiosk trip view (v2.36.0)**: (1) **Map tab finally themes** — `map.html` was one of the pages missing the `ha_theme.html` include, so `?kiosk=true` never applied the HA theme there (the "default color" header). Include added; the shared `components/family_map.html` also themes Leaflet popups under `.ha-theme` (wrapper/tip → card bg + primary text) and its inline popup colors now use `var(--ha-secondary-text)` / `var(--ha-primary-color)` with the old hex values as fallbacks (the PWA, which has no HA theme, keeps its dark look via those fallbacks). (2) **Intake is hidden from the kiosk nav** — it's an admin surface (mail approvals, IMAP password/settings) with no business on a wall display; nav.html hides the tab when `kiosk=true` unless a card explicitly opts back in via `?tabs=...,intake`. (3) **Kiosk state no longer drops when opening a trip**: nav.html's param propagation only rewrites links present at DOMContentLoaded, but trips.html injects its trip cards later — the cards (and the create-trip redirect) now append the page's query params themselves; the New Trip button is hidden in kiosk mode. (4) **Read-only kiosk trip page** (`trip_kiosk.html`): the `/trip` route serves it when `kiosk=true` instead of the 4,000-line editable "travel poster" page (which has zero theme support and full edit affordances). Compact, HA-themed (mapped utility classes only), fetching only `api/trip/{id}`. Layout: hero header (unsplash background, title/location/dates/countdown — hero text forced white over the photo gradient regardless of theme) above four horizontal snap **carousels** so no section grows tall: itinerary day columns (auto-scrolls to today, today ringed blue), flights, accommodations, attractions (priority + scheduled badges; v2.37.1 added the POI image atop each attraction chip — same `image_url` sourcing as trip.html's cards, leading slash stripped for ingress, no image block when the POI has none). Sections hide when empty; arrows render only when content overflows. Flexible drafts show "Day N" labels, never mock dates — same rule as trip.html. **Interactive read-only (v2.51.0, family feedback)**: the kiosk regained the trip **map** (Mapbox dark style, same monthly map-load quota gate as trip.html — `/trip` now passes `mapbox_key`/`allow_map_loads` to both templates; section hidden when no POI/accommodation has coords, key missing, or quota exhausted) with POI markers (green = scheduled, else priority colors) and purple 🏨 accommodation markers; **day cards are tappable** — selecting a day filters the map to that day's stops (POIs matched via `poi.event_id` suffix match, accommodations by check-in/out date range) and lazily fetches + draws its leg routes (`api/maps/route_geometry`, walking dashed green under 1.2km straight-line else driving solid blue, cached per leg pair; tap again to clear) — including (v2.51.1) the purple-dashed stay commutes mirroring trip.html's semantics exactly: morning stay→first-stop only when you woke up at a stay (never on arrival day), last-stop→stay only when you sleep there tonight (never on departure/gap days), and old-stay→new-stay transfer legs on switch days; and **accommodation/attraction cards + marker-popup Details buttons open a read-only details modal** (shared markup: image header, chips, labeled rows — POI experience/why-picked/notes/duration/ideal time/hours/website/phone/link, accommodation dates/price/notes/website/phone/Google-Maps link). Still zero edit affordances — details only, no footer actions. **Shared trip logic (v2.52.0, family feedback: "no duplicated logic that leads to missing features")**: `components/trip_logic.html` defines **`TripLogic`** — the single source of truth for trip SEMANTICS, included by BOTH trip.html and trip_kiosk.html: day grouping/keys + flexible-draft day numbers, POI↔activity matching, category emoji, day date strings, background-theme detection, the stop-to-stop leg set (bg-theme + missing-location exclusions), the stay-commute legs (sleep-tonight base, arrival/departure/switch-day rules), the walking-vs-driving route fetch (1.2km straight-line / 15-min acceptance), marker classification (🌟 day-theme star vs priority-colored pin, hotel icon), and route line paint. Both pages were refactored onto it; the kiosk thereby gained the day-theme star markers it was missing. **Rule: the two trip pages may differ in PRESENTATION only — new trip semantics goes in TripLogic, never inline in one page** (the kiosk silently missing commute lines and star markers is what divergence looks like). **A way back OUT (v2.88.3)**: both trip pages now carry an "← All trips" link — in the header panel on trip.html, as a pill over the hero on trip_kiosk.html (where it also needs the `hero-text` class, or the HA theme darkens the label into its own dark backdrop). The Trips **tab** was the only route back, and `?tabs=none` hides the nav entirely while `?tabs=…` can omit trips — so an HA card could open a trip the family then could not leave. Deliberately a bare `<a href="trips">` carrying no query string of its own: nav.html's rewriter stamps the current params onto every `a[href]` **before** it hides any chrome, so kiosk/theme/tabs follow us back and neither template has to know they exist.
- **Push subscription plumbing (multi-device)**: subscriptions are stored one row per device endpoint (previously keyed by driver_id, so enabling push on a second device silently replaced the first). The PWA silently re-syncs its subscription on every app load when permission is already granted (`syncPushSubscription`), healing stale registrations after VAPID changes or pruning. `send_push` prunes subscriptions the push service rejects with 404/410, and logs loudly when a driver has no subscription at all. Diagnostics: `GET /api/push_subscriptions/debug` lists driver → device endpoint hosts; the PWA notifications modal has a "Send Test Push Notification" button (`POST /api/telemetry/test_push`).
- **Suggested Driver Routes (ghost drivers)**: unassigned events can get a suggested "extra driver" route (`solve_ghost_routes`), rendered as ghost columns on the dashboard. Toggleable via `suggested_routes_enabled` in Settings (Config → General tab, "Solver Behavior" section as of v2.22.0; default **on**) — when off, unassigned events just stay in triage. Ghost generation applies the same trip-conflict semantics as the main solver (shared `compute_event_trip_entities`): a ghost driver is never on a trip, so in-window events whose passengers are away on a background trip (hashtag- or calendar-matched, or any event during a `global` whole-family trip) get no suggestion.
- **Family Members (overlay entity, v2.9.0)**: a `members` storage table holds one record per human, linking the legacy `drivers`/`passengers` records via `driver_id`/`passenger_id` (which remain the solver's untouched source of truth). Fields: name, color_code, avatar (emoji), bio, `can_drive`, `is_child`, and reserved HA mappings (`ha_person_entity`, `notify_service`, `media_player_entity` — populated by later releases) plus an unused `pin`. `storage.ensure_members()` (idempotent, runs at import and after every `add_driver`/`add_passenger`) creates members from drivers, merges passengers onto same-named members (case-insensitive), defaults passenger-only members to `is_child`, and backfills `id` onto legacy passenger docs that predate the field. API: `GET/POST /api/members` (GET enriches with linked driver/passenger snapshots), `PUT/DELETE /api/members/{id}` (DELETE refuses linked members without `force=true`), `POST /api/members/merge` `{keep_id, absorb_id}`, `POST /api/members/{id}/split` `{link}` (undo a bad name-match merge; refuses to split the only link), `GET /api/members/resolve?driver_id=|passenger_id=`. Config → **People** tab (named Family until v2.22.0) manages avatar/color/child flag with Merge/Split. The PWA stores `chauffeur_member_id` in localStorage alongside the legacy `chauffeur_driver_id` (resolved automatically on load; both set on driver selection) — existing endpoints still key on driver_id, member_id is the hub-level identity for upcoming messaging/passenger features.
- **Home Assistant bridge (v2.10.0, `services/ha_api.py`)**: the add-on's first OUTBOUND HA API path — a thin REST client for the HA Core API shared by notifications, the family map, and the Music Assistant bridge. Auth resolution: supervisor proxy (`http://supervisor/core/api` + `SUPERVISOR_TOKEN`; `homeassistant_api: true` added to config.yaml) → `HA_BASE_URL`/`HA_TOKEN` env vars → `ha_base_url`/`ha_token` settings (dev fallback, UI shown only outside HA). All helpers degrade gracefully (None/[]/stale cache) when HA is unreachable — the add-on runs fully without it. `get_states()` has a short TTL cache (5s default, 30s for entity dropdowns) and serves the stale copy if HA goes down mid-poll. `call_service(domain, service, data, return_response=True)` supports HA's REST `?return_response` wrapper (needed for `music_assistant.search`/`get_library` later). API: `GET /api/ha/status` (mode + availability + entity counts; the deploy smoke test for supervisor access), `GET /api/ha/entities?domain=`, `GET /api/ha/notify_services`, `POST /api/ha/test_notify` `{member_id}`. Config → People shows an HA connection chip and per-member dropdowns mapping each member to `ha_person_entity` (location), `notify_service` (phone), `media_player_entity`, with a Test Notify button. New Settings: `public_base_url` (family-facing HTTPS origin, used to build absolute tap-through links in notifications — Settings tab "Public App URL").
- **Family Messaging (v2.11.0)**: member-to-member text chat with four channel kinds — the singleton **family** channel (implicit everyone), **DMs** (canonical sorted member pair, indexed `dm_key`), **groups** (v2.29.1, see its bullet), and **event threads** (get-or-create per calendar event instance via the PWA event modal's "Discuss" button; title/end snapshot refreshes on re-open; auto-archived when the event ended >7 days ago, checked lazily in the channel-list read). Tables `chat_channels`/`chat_messages`/`channel_reads` (message `type` and `attachment` fields reserved for future voice memos; 500-message retention cap per channel). Unread = per-member `last_read_ts` cursor; sender auto-reads own sends. **Empty channels are not listed (v2.20.2)**: Discuss/DM get-or-create the channel the moment a thread is opened, so `GET /api/channels` filters out channels with no messages (except the always-listed family hub) — an accidentally-opened thread no longer clutters the whole household's Messages tab; it appears for its audience once the first message posts. Because empty channels are absent from the list payload, the PWA's Discuss/DM flows pass the freshly created channel object into `openChannel(id, channelObj)` for the thread header. API: `GET /api/channels?member_id=` (previews + unread, family pinned first), `POST /api/channels/dm|event`, `GET|POST /api/channels/{id}/messages`, `POST /api/channels/{id}/read`, `GET /api/messages/stream?member_id=`. **Delivery is three lanes**: (1) addressed SSE `/api/messages/stream` — in-memory seq-numbered ring buffer (`MESSAGE_EVENTS`, trimmed to 200), events carry recipient member ids (None = everyone), clients refetch only the affected channel; foreground-only on iOS by design; (2) **web push** via `send_push_to_member` (member-keyed subscriptions — `push_subscriptions` rows now carry `member_id`, stamped by a startup migration and sent by the PWA on subscribe; same VAPID keys + dead-sub pruning as driver pushes; deep link `/app?open_channel=<id>` rides the existing sw.js default-click handler — **web-push deep links are RELATIVE as of v2.28.2**: the service worker navigates on whatever origin the PWA is actually installed from (LAN/ingress/tunnel), so a mismatched or stale `public_base_url` can never 404 the tap (the bug that broke intake-proposal notification taps); only the HA companion lane, which has no origin context, gets the absolute `{public_base_url}` link — applies to both `_fanout_message_notifications` and `_notify_member_lanes` (chores/rewards/intake nudges)); (3) **HA companion notify** via `ha_api.call_service('notify', member.notify_service)`. Members with both get two notifications (accepted v1 tradeoff). Fan-out runs as a FastAPI background task; recipients = channel members minus sender. PWA: third bottom-bar tab **Messages** (unread badge; `setView` now toggles per-view containers and hides the day-nav in Messages) with channel list → thread bubbles; `?open_channel=` (from push) and `?view=messages` (from the nav "Family Chat" links, desktop + mobile) deep-link straight in. `messageMember(memberId)` opens/creates a DM (reused by later map/passenger features). Agent messaging tools landed in v2.25.0 (see the family-hub agent tools bullet).
- **Family Map (v2.12.0)**: realtime map of family members powered by HA `person` entities — Chauffeur collects no GPS of its own. `GET /api/family/locations` returns every member with state/coords from their `ha_person_entity` (router-based trackers have no lat/lon → the UI shows a zone chip strip instead of a marker; unmapped members return nulls), enriched with `driving: {leg_title}` for drivers who have an in-progress drive leg (leg id collapsed via `_leg_event_id`, joined through the combined schedule cache's assignments). Rendering: **Leaflet + OSM tiles via CDN, lazy-loaded on first map open** (no API key/quota; Mapbox stays geocoding-only) in the shared partial `templates/components/family_map.html` — colored `L.divIcon` avatar markers, popups with state + driving context + a Message button (opens/creates the DM via `messageMember`, PWA only). Surfaces: dashboard page `GET /map` (`map.html`, nav "Map" links desktop + mobile) and a fourth PWA bottom-bar tab **Map**. Refresh: 20s `setInterval` while visible, paused when hidden (deliberately NOT the SSE tick — locations move independently of solver updates). `initFamilyMap(focusMemberId)` supports centering on one member's marker (used by the passenger view's "Where are they?").
- **Passenger/kid lens (v2.13.0)**: the PWA is role-aware by member — same `/app` route. The identity picker now lists **passenger-only members** (members without a `driver_id`) under the drivers; picking one enters passenger mode (`selectPassengerMember`: `chauffeur_member_id` set, `chauffeur_driver_id` cleared). Tab bar swaps to **My Day / Messages / Map** (drivers keep My Drives / Family / Messages / Map; `applyRoleTabs()` handles both directions incl. restore-on-load). **My Day** shows one day at a time with the same prev/next day-nav header drivers get (label Today/Tomorrow/weekday, offset clamped 0–13, reset to Today on tab entry) from `GET /api/members/{id}/day?date=`: the member's rides pulled from the combined schedule cache, matched via their passenger record's `calendar_ids` intersection, hashtag-in-title, **or `matched_rules` passenger_ids binding** (same resolution calendar.html uses — family-calendar events bound to a kid by a rule; checked against both the event id and its base id with `_unrolled_`/`_dropoff`/`_pickup` collapsed), excluding errands and trip-suppressed events. **Split events collapse to one card**: `_dropoff`/`_pickup` variants fold into their parent event as `legs: [{type, start, driver, status}]` (each leg's driver resolved to a member — dropoff and pickup can differ; a leg in progress bubbles up to the card status; variants whose parent isn't in the schedule stay standalone). Non-split rides carry a single `driver` + `status`. The day-nav arrows disable at the Today/13-day bounds (re-enabled when returning to driver views). Cards show "🚗 <driver> is driving you", an "On the way" chip for in-progress legs, a **Where?** button (Map tab centered on the driver's marker) and **Message** (DM deep link). Push subscriptions work in passenger mode (`driver_id: ''`, member-keyed).
- **Music Assistant bridge (v2.14.0, control + search over REST — no WebSocket client, no in-app playback)**: MA's HA services are called via `ha_api.call_service(..., return_response=True)`; `search`/`get_library` need the MA integration's config entry id, resolved once via `ha_api.get_config_entry_id('music_assistant')` (HA config-entries HTTP view) and cached in `_MA_CONFIG_ENTRY`. API: `GET /api/ha/media_players` (rich list: state, media_title/artist, entity_picture, volume, supported_features), `POST /api/ha/media_players/{entity}/command` (`play|pause|next|previous|volume_set|volume_mute` → `media_player.*` services), `GET /api/music/search?q=&media_type=&limit=` (→ `music_assistant.search`, service_response unwrapped), `GET /api/music/favorites?media_type=` (→ `get_library` favorite=True), `GET /api/music/queue?entity_id=`, `POST /api/music/play` (`{entity_id, media_id, media_type, enqueue?}` → `music_assistant.play_media`). 503 when the MA integration is absent; 502 on HA call failure. `GET /api/ha/media_players` defaults to **MA players only** (entities carrying the `mass_player_type` attribute; graceful fallback to the full list when none exist; `?ma_only=false` for everything) and includes `device_class` + `is_ma_player`. UI (v2.15.0): a dedicated fifth PWA bottom-bar tab **Music** (both driver and passenger modes) hosting `templates/components/music_widget.html` — player dropdown with device-type emoji (📺/🔊/🎚️/🏠 from device_class + name heuristics; defaults to the member's `media_player_entity`, sticky via `localStorage['chauffeur_music_player']`), now-playing artwork/title, transport buttons, debounced (500ms) volume slider, **rich debounced search** (artwork thumbnails from MA's serialized image urls, artist·album subtitles, media-type chips, stale-response guard) and a **Favorites shelf** (favorite playlists + albums, artwork grid, tap-to-play). (Dropped from the dashboard `/map` page in v2.25.2 — that page lives inside Home Assistant, which already controls MA natively; the PWA Music tab is the family's music surface.) 10s state poll only while the widget is visible. **"This phone" Sendspin player (v2.16.0)**: the PWA registers as a REAL Music Assistant player via MA 2.7's Sendspin protocol, using the official `@sendspin/sendspin-js@3.2.1` browser client (ESM from jsDelivr; MA's own web player uses the same lib — it handles the Noise encryption, CPace pairing, Kalman time-sync, and Opus decode). Transport: browsers on the https origin can't open MA's plain `ws://:8927` (mixed content), so the add-on relays — `wss://<origin>/api/sendspin/ws` (FastAPI websocket, `websockets` dep) pumps frames bidirectionally to the MA server, resolved from candidates: `ma_server_url` setting (Settings tab "Music Assistant Server", normalized to ws://host:8927/sendspin) → official add-on hostname `d5369777-music-assistant` → the HA host → `homeassistant.local`; successful URL cached, invalidated on failure; each candidate probe is wrapped in an outer `asyncio.wait_for` because `open_timeout` does not bound stalled Windows DNS lookups. UI: "📱 This phone (<member>)" pinned first in the player dropdown (full remote list kept — decision: personal endpoint is an ADDITION, not a lock); selecting it starts the client in that user gesture (`unlock()` then `connect()`), registered as "<member>'s phone" with a persistent per-device id (`localStorage['chauffeur_sendspin_id']`); transport/volume act locally via `sendCommand`/`setVolume`; now-playing renders from `onStateChange` (defensive field probing — lib state shape not pinned); tap-to-play targets the phone's HA entity (matched by clientName once MA registration propagates; toast if not yet); the phone's HA entity is hidden from the remote list to avoid duplication. iOS caveat: playback with the app open is the supported case; lock-screen/background playback awaits the native wrapper. Disconnects on pagehide. **One-time per-device setup (field-confirmed)**: MA registers new Sendspin clients as hidden personal players NOT exposed to HA — in MA → Settings → Players → "<member>'s phone", enable "Expose this player to Home Assistant" (and optionally un-hide it in MA's UI); until then tap-to-play toasts these exact instructions. Auto-toggling via MA's server API was considered and rejected (version-fragile config keys for a one-time step).
- **Artwork rule (hard-won)**: only absolute `https://` image urls load directly; HA-relative `entity_picture` paths (wrong origin) AND absolute `http://` urls (MA's LAN image proxy — iOS Safari silently blocks mixed content on the https page; desktop browsers don't, which masked it) route through the add-on proxy `GET /api/ha/image64/{base64url}` (legacy `?path=` form kept; base64url because encoded-slash query strings tripped middleboxes). The proxy allowlists HA image path prefixes and, for absolute urls, LAN/private hosts only (RFC1918/loopback/localhost/.local) and never sends the HA token to non-HA hosts; every request logs `[ha_image] OK/REJECTED/UPSTREAM-FAIL` with bytes/ms/UA. Failed artwork hides the img and toasts the HTTP status+detail once per URL (field diagnostic). Deferred: HA's generic `browse_media` folder tree (WebSocket-only, low value with MA present).
- **Member roles & PINs (v2.17.0, groundwork for chores)**: `FamilyMember.role: parent|adult|child|helper` replaces `is_child` as authority (is_child kept in sync for display; `ensure_member_roles()` backfills is_child→child else adult; parents promoted in Config → Family via a Role select; role changes validate server-side). **Helper** = external (hired driver/nanny): keeps My Drives/claims/pushes, but PWA hides Family/Map/Music/My Day tabs; server-enforced: `get_channels_for_member` returns only their DMs, DM creation allowed with parents only (403 otherwise), posting outside DMs 403, family/event fan-out excludes helpers, `/api/family/locations` omits them (live helper location = native-app deliverable; drive status still shows). Kid→helper contact relays through the family channel (`relayViaFamilyChannel` prefills "To <driver> (driving <event>): "); My Day hides the Where? button for helper drivers (day API driver now carries `role`). **PINs**: per-member, optional, 4–8 digits, pbkdf2+salt (`pin_hash`/`pin_salt`, NEVER in API responses — `_public_member` exposes `has_pin`); PWA prompts on identity switch (device remembers current identity via a per-device token from `POST /api/members/{id}/auth`; members without PINs auth freely and get a one-time set-a-PIN offer); 5 wrong attempts → 30s lockout (429). `POST .../pin` sets/changes (change needs current PIN), `POST .../pin/clear` = parent reset from the dashboard-trusted config page (also revokes that member's tokens). `require_parent_token()` is the guard chores verification/rewards admin will use; note the dashboard/config surface itself remains open-admin as it always has been.
- **Chore economy (v2.18.0)**: marketplace model — chores sit in a family pot and are self-claimed, NOT solver-assigned. Tables `chores` + append-only `points_ledger` (balances are sums; entries carry chore title + verifier). Lifecycle `open → claimed → done → verified`, with reject (`done → claimed` + `rejected_reason`, no forfeiture) and lazy maintenance on every read (`_chore_maintenance`): recurring verified chores reopen clean when `reopens_on` (daily +1d / weekly +7d / monthly +1mo) arrives; claims stale >48h release back to the pot. Claim cap 3 concurrent (claimed+done both count). Eligibility: per-chore member list (empty = any non-helper); helpers 403; **children earn points, adults are claimable-but-pointless, parents verify** (`require_parent_token` via `X-Member-Token` header — the PIN-backed integrity gate; chore CRUD itself is open-admin on the config page, same trust as the rest of the dashboard, which is fine because payouts still require parent verification). API: `GET/POST/PUT/DELETE /api/chores`, `POST /api/chores/{id}/claim|unclaim|done|verify|reject`, `GET /api/points` (children leaderboard), `GET /api/points/{member}` (balance + ledger). **Manual reopen (v2.20.1)**: `POST /api/chores/{id}/reopen` returns a `verified` chore to the pot early (needs doing again this period; prior points always stay — undoing a payout is a separate explicit adjust) or releases a `claimed` chore without waiting out the 48h stale timer; `done` is deliberately NOT reopenable (finished work awaiting verification gets verified or rejected, never silently discarded) → 409. Reopening re-fires the "chore posted" notification to eligible kids. Surfaces: green **Reopen** button on `/chores` rows (verified/claimed states), and a `reopen_chore(chore_title)` agent tool in BOTH stacks (fuzzy title match; already-open = friendly no-op, done = steered to verify/reject). Notifications (`_notify_member_lanes`: web push + HA notify, deep link `/app?view=chores`): posted → eligible children; done → parents ("ready to verify"); verified/rejected → claimant (with points or reason). UI: PWA **Chores** tab (all non-helper roles; badge = open-and-eligible count, for parents = awaiting-verification count) with points header (child: my balance; parent/adult: kids' leaderboard chips), sections Needs-your-OK (parent, ✓/✗ with reason prompt), My chores (Done!/unclaim, rejection reason shown), Available (Claim), Taken; the pot (title/points/recurrence/description/eligibility checkboxes) is managed on the dedicated **/chores** page (v2.20.0 — formerly a Config tab).
- **Daily routines + streaks + rewards store (v2.19.0)**: **Routines** are per-member personal checklists (`routines` templates: optional `time_of_day` HH:MM, `days_of_week` mask 0=Mon..6=Sun, empty = every day; `routine_checks` one row per item/day) — deliberately NO points (personal duties ≠ paid work), streaks instead. Rendered as a "My Routine" card atop **My Day** (per-day checkbox list, time chips, n/total progress, 🔥 streak chip; interactive today only, read-only on other days; completing the last item toasts the streak). Streak semantics (`compute_streak`, 90-day window): a day is complete when every item scheduled that day is checked; days with nothing scheduled are NEUTRAL (bridge, don't break); an incomplete *today* doesn't break the streak — it just doesn't count yet. API: `GET/POST/PUT/DELETE /api/routines`, `GET /api/routines/day?member_id=&date=` (items+checked+streak), `POST /api/routines/{id}/check` (own routines only). **Rewards**: parent-defined items with point costs (`rewards`; NO money integration); kids request (`POST /api/rewards/{id}/redeem`, children only, spendable = balance minus pending requests so no double-spend), parents approve/deny (`POST /api/redemptions/{id}/decide`, parent token) — approval writes a negative `redeem` ledger entry, denial deducts nothing, decisions are final. PWA: Rewards grid in the Chores tab for kids (cost, Redeem disabled when unaffordable, "Asked ⏳" while pending); pending redemptions appear in the parent's Needs-your-OK section (✓ Grant / ✗) and count in the Chores tab badge. Notifications: request → parents; approved/denied → kid. Routines and Rewards admin live on the dedicated **/routines** and **/chores** pages (v2.20.0 — formerly Config → Chores tab sections). This completes the chores arc (roles+PINs v2.17, economy v2.18, routines+rewards v2.19).
- **Chores & Routines main pages + points admin (v2.20.0)**: chores/routines are operational state, not configuration — they moved out of Config into two dedicated dashboard pages on the main nav (desktop + mobile, kiosk param propagates like every nav link). **`/chores`** (chores.html, Alpine): points **leaderboard** cards per child (rank/avatar/balance, pending-redemption note, per-child ledger history view), **point admin tools** per child (add/subtract N with a note, set-to absolute, zero-out) + a global "Reset all points…", pending reward requests list (read-only pointer — approve/deny stays in the app behind the parent PIN), full chore-pot CRUD and rewards-store CRUD (ported from the old Config tab). With `?kiosk=true` the page collapses to the leaderboard only (auto-refresh 60s). **`/routines`** (routines.html): the editor is **grouped one block per member** — "New Routine" offers only members without one (a deliberate one-routine-per-person UX; the data model stays flat `RoutineItem`s, the block is pure presentation — an empty just-started block is client-side until its first item), each block has a streak chip + today progress, inline item edit, and a quick-add row that keeps time/days between adds for rapid entry; "Delete routine" deletes all of a member's items (+checks). Kiosk mode = **interactive checklist lanes** (v2.26.1): a compact horizontal streak strip over one lane per member showing today's items, each tappable to check off as that child (per-lane `member_id` → `/api/routines/{id}/check`, which validates ownership) — previously a read-only streak board. **Points adjust/reset API**: `POST /api/points/adjust` `{member_id, delta | set_to, note}` (children only, exactly one of delta/set_to, |delta| ≤ 100k, no-op writes nothing) appends an `adjust` ledger entry — the ledger stays append-only, corrections are entries, never edits; the note rides in `chore_title` so every ledger renderer shows it; the kid gets a push/HA notification. `POST /api/points/reset` `{member_id?}` (omit = all children) zeroes via compensating `adjust` entries and **auto-denies pending redemptions first** (a pending request against a zeroed balance could never be approved and would wedge future redemptions since pending counts as spent). `GET /api/routines/streaks` = per-member streak summary for the pages. Open-admin trust on these endpoints matches the config page precedent (which can already clear PINs). **Agent tools in BOTH stacks** (v2 Gemma router + v1 loop, shared implementation in agent_tools_v2): `get_point_balances` (no args) and `adjust_points(member_name, delta|set_to, note)` with fuzzy child-name matching (ambiguous → error listing children); both are terminal actions in the router (their message is the complete spoken answer). Shared UI dialogs: `components/control_center.html` gains a **`promptInput`** modal (promise-based `prompt()` replacement, sibling of `promptConfirm`/`showGlobalAlert`) — Chauffeur never uses browser `alert()`/`confirm()`/`prompt()`; the new pages and the Config members/PIN handlers all use the shared modals.
- **ICS feed subscriptions (v2.21.0, intake arc phase 1 — `services/ics_sync.py`)**: subscribe once to a team/school ICS (or `webcal://`) URL and its events are created on a chosen member's Google calendar, so the whole downstream pipeline (attendee detection → solver → pushes/digests/My Day) runs with zero new code. Routing is a **subscription-level fact**: each feed doc (`ics_feeds` table: url, name — defaults to the feed's `X-WR-CALNAME` — target `calendar_id`, enabled, event_map, last_synced/last_status/event_count) targets exactly ONE calendar, chosen at subscribe time; no per-event intelligence, zero LLM. Sync (hourly `ics_sync_loop` in main.py + on-subscribe + manual): fetch (10MB cap, 30s timeout), parse via `icalendar` + `recurring-ical-events` (RRULE expansion, window now−2d → now+400d), diff against the stored `event_map` (ICS key → created GCal event id + content fingerprint): new → insert, fingerprint change → patch in place, vanished **future** event → delete (cancellation). Key scheme: single events keyed by bare UID (time change = patch); recurring occurrences keyed `uid::start` (one rained-out occurrence never disturbs siblings). **Past events are frozen history**: never deleted when feeds drop them (feeds routinely truncate history), pruned from tracking after a 2-day grace, and exempt from delete-feed cleanup. Created events carry `extendedProperties.private.ics_feed_id`/`ics_key`; all-day events map to GCal `date` bodies (calendar.py gains generic `insert_event`/`patch_event`/`remove_event`, the latter treating 404/410 as success so manually-deleted events don't retry forever; transient delete failures stay tracked and retry next sync). Changes trigger `trigger_background_refresh()`. API: `GET/POST /api/ics_feeds` (POST validates by fetching/parsing first — returns event count; initial sync runs as a background task), `PUT /{id}` (rename/pause; retargeting calendars is refused — delete and re-add), `POST /{id}/sync`, `DELETE /{id}?remove_events=` (optional cleanup deletes future created events only). UI: **inline on the person, not a separate tab** — Config → People → Identity → 📅 Google Calendars shows a "Subscribed ICS Feeds" block directly under the person's calendar list (it lived in the two role forms until v2.105.0, when calendars became person-level): feed rows (name, count, status/last-sync, Sync/Pause/Delete) filtered to that person's calendars, plus a paste-URL + Subscribe row; the target calendar defaults to the person's only calendar, with a picker shown just when they have several. Tests: `tests/test_ics_sync.py` (parse: single/recurring/all-day, key scheme, fingerprints; sync lifecycle: create/patch/delete, past-event protection, failed-delete retry, fetch-error isolation, idempotent re-sync, future-only feed-deletion cleanup).
- **People tab (v2.22.0 — unified member + role editing)**: Config's Drivers/Passengers/Family tabs collapsed into one **People** tab (`activeTab === 'family'` internally). The member list (formerly the Family tab) is now the hub: each member card carries Identity (the existing inline avatar/color/role/PIN/HA editor), **🚗 Driving** and **🧒 Passenger** buttons that open the person's role profile — the full former driver/passenger forms, preserved intact as on-demand sub-panels (`showDriverForm`/`showPassengerForm`, ids `driverFormPanel`/`passengerFormPanel`, flex `order` puts them under the list; opening one closes the other and smooth-scrolls to it). A member without the role gets the blank form prefilled with their name (and color for driving) — `ensure_members()` links the saved record back to the member by name. A single **"+ Add a Person"** button (v2.22.1) opens an inline mini-form (name, role, color, avatar) that POSTs a bare FamilyMember — people need NO driver/passenger profile to exist: role-less members (college kid, at-home nanny) sign into the PWA (the identity picker lists all members without a `driver_id`), chat, map, music, and routines all work, My Day just shows no rides; role profiles are added later from their card's + Driving / + Passenger buttons. To make that flow safe, `ensure_members()` now links unlinked **drivers** onto same-named members missing a `driver_id` (v2.22.1 — previously only passengers merged by name and a driver profile added to an existing person silently DUPLICATED them; this was also the longstanding source of Merge-button busywork). Profile deletion moved into the forms ("Remove Driving/Passenger Profile", `promptConfirm` — this also replaced the tabs' old browser `confirm()` calls); role saves/deletes reload members so chips stay truthful. The Drivers tab's two global solver toggles (Balance Driving Load, Suggested Driver Routes) moved to General → **Solver Behavior** — they were never people-properties. Backend untouched: drivers/passengers records, APIs, and merge/split all work exactly as before.
- **Email intake (v2.23.0, intake arc phase 2 — `services/email_ingest.py`, `/intake` page)**: a dedicated family Gmail (polled over **IMAP with an app password** — Gmail's API disallows service accounts on consumer accounts, so the Calendar credential pattern can't be reused) receives forwarded/auto-forwarded school & team email; Chauffeur turns it into **proposals** a parent approves before anything touches a calendar. Poll loop every 10 min (`email_ingest_loop`, enabled via `ingest_email_enabled`) plus a "Check mailbox now" button — both serialized under a run lock since they share the UID cursor (v2.23.3); a manual check finding nothing says "the automatic poll may have just processed things" (the run response carries `pending` so the dialog can distinguish the race from a genuinely quiet mailbox), and the /intake page quietly refreshes proposals/log every 60s while visible (preserving in-progress target selections). First contact with a mailbox stores the max UID and skips backlog. **Every message in the mailbox is analyzed — the mailbox IS the filter (v2.23.1)**: the family curates upstream with Gmail auto-forward filters and manual forwards, so there is no Chauffeur-side sender gate (one would also break manual forwards, which arrive From the forwarding parent, not the original sender). Pipeline per message: **target-calendar prefill** (v2.23.2: three tiers) — `ingest_sender_defaults` `{pattern, calendar_id}` (case-insensitive From-substring like `@school.org`; an explicit parent routing decision, wins) → the LLM's `member_name` guess resolved to that member's calendar (`_calendar_for_member_name`: passenger calendar first, driver fallback; the prompt sets member_name null for multi-member/whole-family items) → `default_calendar_id`, the family's shared calendar starred (★) on one of the Google Calendar IDs in Config → General (whole-family events land there; attendees get tagged after approval) — → LLM extraction via the **background model-pool tier** (gemma first — nobody waits on ingest, and its 14,400/day quota keeps the fast lite pool free for chat; the prompt is also the relevance gate — "most emails contain NOTHING actionable", resolves relative dates, kinds `event`/`task` where task = date-bound to-do like "send $12 by Friday", becomes an all-day 📌 event on approval) → normalize (confidence floor 0.4, past/+400d window, all-day vs timed) → **dedupe** against ALL prior proposals regardless of status (an ignored proposal must not reappear on a newsletter re-send) and same-day schedule-cache events → stored in `event_proposals`. **Dedupe is fuzzy (v2.45.4)**: beyond exact/substring title matches, `_is_duplicate` compares TOKEN SETS (word order and punctuation never matter; one title containing all the other's words matches outright) with Jaccard overlap ≥0.75, relaxed to ≥0.6 when start/end times agree within 30 min — an email reminder rephrasing an event an ICS feed already created ('Game vs Eagles - U12 Blue' vs 'U12 Blue game vs. Eagles', same times) is now caught, while 'Game vs Eagles' vs 'Game vs Hawks' in the same 9am slot (two kids' teams, overlap 0.5) stays two events. Known limit: the schedule-cache side only covers days the cache holds (~30d); a proposal far beyond that has nothing to dedupe against. Every processed message writes an `ingest_log` row (proposed N / no actionable items / error) capped at 200 — the accountability trail that makes false negatives auditable instead of silent. **Consecutive identical rows collapse (v2.28.4)**: `add_ingest_log` folds a row matching the newest row's from/subject/outcome into it (`count` increments, `ts` bumps to latest, `first_ts` keeps the start) — in practice repeated `(poll)` errors while the mailbox is unreachable (DNS outage on the HA box hitting every 10-minute poll), so an hours-long outage is one log line ("×9 since 10:44 AM" chip on /intake) instead of flooding the capped log; an interleaved different row starts a fresh run so chronology stays honest. Poll failures happen before the UID cursor moves, so no mail is ever lost to an outage — the next successful poll catches up. New proposals push a review nudge to role=parent members, deep-linking into the **PWA Family tab** (`/app?view=family` — the dashboard /intake page isn't reachable from a phone tap on the public origin). **PWA approval queue (v2.24.0)**: for role=parent members the Family tab shows a "📥 Proposed events" section above the day agenda (`proposals-container` in app.html; hidden when empty/non-parent) — compact cards with kind chip, when/where/who-guess, source subject, a target-calendar select (options from passenger+driver calendars, plus a "Family calendar" entry when the prefill isn't among them; Approve disabled until a target is picked) and ✓ Approve / ✗ Ignore hitting the same `/api/proposals` endpoints as /intake. All email-derived text is HTML-escaped before injection (email content is untrusted). Queue loads on app start for parents and on entering the Family tab; `?view=family` joins the recognized deep-link params. **`/intake` page** (nav Intake, desktop+mobile): proposal cards (kind chip, when/where/who-guess, confidence, source From/Subject; target-calendar select prefilled per the routing above; ✓ Approve → GCal event via `insert_event` with `intake_proposal_id` extended prop + background re-solve; ✗ Ignore), activity log, and mailbox settings (enabled toggle, host/user/app-password — password write-only, blank keeps stored; sender-defaults editor). API: `GET/POST /api/ingest/config` (password never echoed, `has_password` flag), `POST /api/ingest/run`, `GET /api/ingest/log`, `GET /api/proposals?status=`, `POST /api/proposals/{id}/approve|ignore`. Settings live as real `Settings` model fields (the model is a strict whitelist — extras would be silently dropped on config-page saves) but are saved via `storage.patch_settings`, a merge that skips `update_settings`'s schedule-cache invalidation. **`POST /api/settings` is MERGE, not replace (v2.23.2)**: it applies `model_dump(exclude_unset=True)` onto the stored settings — clients send only the fields they manage, and the old blind replace filled every unsent field with model defaults, silently wiping intake mailbox credentials (and any legacy extra keys) on every config-page save. Tests: `tests/test_email_ingest.py` (MIME plain/html extraction, allowlist, normalization incl. task pinning + windows, end-to-end run with mocked IMAP/LLM, ignored-dup suppression, extraction-failure isolation).
- **Family-hub agent tools (v2.25.0 — messaging/chores/routines in BOTH stacks, shared impls in agent_tools_v2)**: six tools registered in the v2 Gemma router AND the dormant v1 loop (agent_tools.py schema/handler registry, same lockstep pattern as the chore-points tools). `send_family_message(message_text, from_member?)` and `send_direct_message(recipient_name, message_text, from_member?)` post real ChatMessages through the normal storage path AND fire the same SSE + push/HA fan-out as the /api/channels endpoint (`_post_chat_message` lazily imports `main` — the running app module — for `_push_message_event`/`_fanout_message_notifications`; absent in tests → fan-out skipped silently). **Identity rules**: in PWA driver chat the logged-in member is the trusted sender (`sender_driver_id` injected server-side at dispatch, never from the LLM; driver-mode prompt says never ask who's sending); in admin/voice the speaker must be named via `from_member`/`member_name` — the non-driver prompt instructs the model to ask BEFORE sending/claiming, never guess ("tell Mom Dad is on his way" → from Dad is acceptable inference). Helper rules mirror the endpoints: no family-channel posts/reads, DMs with parents only, no chore claims. `get_family_messages(limit)` reads recent family-channel messages with sender names/timestamps. `list_chores()` summarizes the pot (open/claimed/awaiting-verification). `claim_chore(chore_title, member_name?)` fuzzy-matches an OPEN chore, enforces the per-chore eligibility list and the storage claim cap, self-claims in driver chat. `get_routine_status(member_name, target_date)` reports n/m done, missing item titles, and the streak. All six are TERMINAL in the router (sends are actions; the reads return the complete spoken answer — same reasoning as get_point_balances), keeping voice requests to one LLM call. **Argyle FAB restored on the PWA Messages tab** (hidden since v2.15.3): visible on the channel list now that the agent can actually message; still hidden inside an open thread where it overlaid the composer's Send button (openChannel/closeThread re-run applyViewVisibility). Deliberately NOT included: chore verification by voice (the parent-PIN-equivalence open question stands). Tests: `tests/test_agent_family_tools.py`.
- **Nav/chrome cleanup (v2.25.2)**: the Settings gear sits LEFT of the Control Center hamburger in the top nav; the hamburger (`#control-center-nav-item`) hides while the sidebar is open (the sidebar has its own close button — two visible hamburgers read as a bug) and re-appears on close. Kiosk mode hides the Control Center entirely (desktop button + mobile menu entry `#mobile-cc-nav-item`, alongside the long-hidden settings gear) and never restores an open sidebar from `localStorage['controlCenterOpen']` — kiosk is a display surface. The Music Assistant widget was removed from the dashboard `/map` page (redundant inside HA); PWA Music tab unaffected. **Per-embed tab filtering (v2.25.3, `?tabs=`)**: aimed at HA iframe cards — each card's URL is its own config channel (nav links propagate all query params, so the filter survives navigation). `?tabs=none` hides the nav bar entirely (pure single-purpose card, e.g. `/chores?kiosk=true&tabs=none`); `?tabs=schedule,chores` shows only the named tabs (slugs: schedule, calendar, errands, chores, routines, intake, trips, map — desktop strip and mobile menu both filtered; unknown slugs ignored). Client-side only in nav.html; no server config.
- **Interactive kiosk chore board (v2.26.0)**: `/chores?kiosk=true` grew from a read-only leaderboard into a self-serve family board for the wall panel. The leaderboard collapses to a **compact horizontal strip** (rank/avatar/name/points) so it takes minimal vertical space, and below it sits **one interactive lane per child** — each showing that child's claimed/in-progress chores (Done! / release ↩ / "Waiting for OK"), the chores available to *them* (respecting `eligible_member_ids`), and the rewards they can afford (Redeem, disabled when unaffordable, "Asked ⏳" when pending). The design fix for the multi-user wall panel: **identity is per-lane, not per-session.** The old failure was N PWA iframes sharing one origin's `localStorage` identity (`selectedMemberId`) — last selection wins and stomps the rest. Here there is no stored identity at all; each lane passes its child's `member_id` explicitly to the *same* per-action endpoints the PWA uses (`/api/chores/{id}/claim|unclaim|done`, `/api/rewards/{id}/redeem` — all take `member_id` in the body, the backend was already per-action-identity). Kiosk mode now loads chores/rewards/redemptions (not just points) and auto-refreshes all of it every 30s so lanes reflect siblings' taps and parent approvals. Safety rests on the existing downstream parent gates (chore *done* awaits parent verify; reward *redeem* is a parent-approved request, not an instant spend) rather than a login — matching the PWA, which also PIN-gates none of these. Non-kiosk `/chores` (the parent admin view: point tools, chore/reward CRUD) is unchanged. Pure frontend (chores.html) over existing APIs. Optional follow-up: a per-child PIN on the wall panel (members already carry `pin_hash`; `/api/members/{id}/auth` verifies) if pranking becomes a problem.
- **Kiosk board polish + routines lanes (v2.26.1)**: (1) `?chat=none` (also `off`/`0`/`false`/`hide`) hides the Argyle chat bar on any page — a per-instance switch for display-only wall-panel cards (control_center.html, works alongside `?kiosk=true`/`?tabs=none`). (2) Kiosk chore/routine boards now use **full page width** (`max-w-none` in kiosk) and **balance lanes across it**: `laneColumns()` returns the child count when they all fit in one row (each ≥ a ~320px min, lanes just get narrower as the panel shrinks), otherwise the fewest even rows (`ceil(n / ceil(n / maxFit))`) so a 4th child never drops to a lonely row — the column count is computed at render (reactive to `winW`) because pure-CSS `auto-fit`/`flex-wrap` can shrink-to-fit but can't balance groups. Applied as an inline `grid-template-columns: repeat(cols, minmax(0,1fr))`. (3) The **routines kiosk** got the same interactive treatment as chores: compact streak strip + per-child lanes with today's checklist, tap-to-check as that child.
- **Status tiers + kiosk simplification (v2.27.0)**: light-touch gamification and a UI cleanup on the kiosk boards.
  - **Auto-earned status tiers** (`services/status_tiers.py`): kids earn a recognition status shown as a badge on their lanes. **Two independent single-metric ladders (v2.27.3)** so each board's badge honestly reflects THAT board's performance (the earlier shared OR-ladder let 500 chore points display "Legend" on the routines board): the **chore ladder** keys on lifetime **points earned** (Rising Star 25 → Super Helper 100 → Everyday Hero 250 → Legend 500) and the **routine ladder** on **best streak** (Getting There 3 → Consistent 7 → Streak Star 14 → Unstoppable 30). Both inputs are **monotonic** (`get_points_earned` sums only positive deltas; best-streak only rises) so a status is never taken away. Surfaced as `status` on `/api/points` (chore track) and `/api/routines/streaks` (routine track). Each ladder (name/emoji/threshold rows) is editable via the shared `components/status_tiers_editor.html` — the including page sets Jinja var `tier_kind` (`chore`/`routine`) before the include (NOTE: never put a `{% set %}` example inside an HTML comment — Jinja executes tags in comments; this bit us). Settings fields `chore_status_tiers`/`routine_status_tiers` (the v2.27.1 shared `status_tiers` key is ignored/abandoned); `GET`/`PUT /api/status-tiers?kind=` (stored ascending; falls back to that track's defaults when unset/empty; `patch_settings`, no solver invalidation). A **threshold of 0 is valid (v2.27.4)** and acts as a default level every kid starts with (the editor rejects only blank/negative, so an empty box can't silently become a default). **PWA badges + tier-up celebration (v2.27.5)**: a child sees their chore status under "My points" on the Chores tab and their routine status beside the streak chip in My Day (`/api/routines/day` + the `/check` response carry the routine-track status — as `tier_status` on `/check`, whose `status` key was taken). `maybeCelebrateStatus(track, status)` in app.html fires a full-screen confetti overlay when the logged-in child's tier changes UPWARD: last-seen tier kept per member+track in localStorage, seeded silently on first sight (no stale celebration on a new device), compared by threshold (ladder renames/config edits don't false-fire); hooks at chores render, My Day load, and the routine check-off response (so the overlay lands at the moment of the tap that earned it). **Kiosk celebrations (v2.27.6, `components/status_celebration.html`)**: the wall-panel boards fire a **named** overlay ("Addison is now Super Helper!", ~7s, tap dismisses) — routines instantly on the check-off response (`tier_status`) with the 30s poll sweep as backstop, chores on the `/api/points` poll (points land via parent verification elsewhere, so a phone-side verify celebrates publicly on the wall within one poll). Same localStorage transition rules per member+track on the kiosk device (silent first-sight seed, threshold-compared); the instant path writes localStorage first so the sweep can't double-fire. Tests: `tests/test_status_tiers.py`.
  - **Kiosk UI folded together**: the chores kiosk **leaderboard strip was removed** — rank (#1/#2…) folded into the per-child lanes (already points-ordered), so the board is just the lanes. The routines kiosk **streak strip was removed** too (it duplicated the lane headers). Both lanes show the status badge.
  - **`?title=none`** (also off/0/false/hide) hides the page `<header>`/title — for HA dashboard single-tab embeds where the dashboard already supplies a title. Same value grammar as `?chat=none`.
- **Prep kits (v2.28.0, rule-filter matching v2.29.0 — `services/prep_kits.py`)**: packing lists that kill the five-minutes-before-departure "what do we need?" scramble. A kit is `{name, items, enabled}` plus the SAME filter criteria routing rules use — keywords (+`keywords_match_all`), `passenger_ids` (+`passengers_match_all`), `days_of_week`, `time_start`/`time_end`, `start_date`/`end_date` (seasonal gear), `location` substring. **Matching delegates to the solver's `does_event_match_rule`** via lightweight adapters (`_event_obj`/`_kit_rule_obj`/`passenger_objs`), so kit semantics are identical to rule semantics by construction (AND across criteria types, passenger resolution through calendar ids); validation requires ≥1 item and ≥1 criterion. Surfaces: (1) `/api/members/{id}/day` rides carry `prep: [items]` (deduped in kit order across matching kits), rendered as amber "🎒 Bring" chips on PWA My Day cards; (2) tomorrow-digest push lines get ` (bring: a, b, …)` appended (first 4 items). **Matched-event visibility** (`GET /api/prep-kits/matches`): per kit, the upcoming cache events its filters hit (split legs collapsed, errands/trip-suppressed excluded, cap 20) — shown as an "N upcoming" chip on each kit row that expands to the matched titles/dates, same feedback loop rules get. API: `GET/POST /api/prep-kits`, `PUT/DELETE /api/prep-kits/{id}`. **Agent-assisted setup** (`POST /api/prep-kits/suggest`): ONE `_call_llm_json` request over deduped upcoming event titles proposes keyword kits; the server prunes ungrounded keywords, drops already-covered kits, and returns them UNSAVED for review cards (✓ Save / ✗ Dismiss). Management UI is the "🎒 Prep Kits" section on **/routines** (non-kiosk; deliberately NOT the Config Rules tab — rules configure the solver, kits configure reminders): kit rows with filter chips, the edit panel mirrors the group-rule filter-set form (keyword chips + Any/All, passenger buttons, day buttons, date/time windows, location), quick-add row stays keywords-only. Tests: `tests/test_prep_kits.py`.
- **Group chats + New-chat picker (v2.29.1)**: fourth channel `kind='group'` — an explicit member subset ("parents only"), vs the family channel's implicit-everyone. Get-or-create keyed on the sorted member set stored in the same indexed `dm_key` field (DM keys have exactly 2 ids, group keys ≥3, so they never collide). `storage.get_or_create_group(member_ids, title='')`; a provided title refreshes the name (groups without a title render as the other members' names client-side). API `POST /api/channels/group` `{member_id, member_ids, title?}` — creator always included, ≥3 distinct people required (else 400 → use a DM), **helpers refused 403** (helpers stay DM-with-a-parent only, same reasoning as their family-channel exclusion; `get_channels_for_member` group visibility = membership). Send/SSE/fan-out treat groups like DMs (recipients = `member_ids`; push title `Sender · GroupName`). PWA: **"New chat" button** atop the Messages tab opens a type-ahead picker over `membersData`; selections become removable pills — 1 pill starts/opens a DM, 2+ creates the group. Non-parents never see helpers in the picker; parents see helpers only while the selection is a single person (DM). Like DMs, an empty group is hidden from everyone's channel list until its first message.
- **Argyle DMs (v2.29.2)**: the assistant is now a first-class DM peer. `POST /api/channels/dm` accepts `argyle` as either side (the system member is `ensure_argyle_member()`-created lazily; helpers still refused — their DMs are parent-only and Argyle isn't a parent). **Every message sent in an Argyle DM routes to the agent with no @mention** (`send_message` detects `kind='dm'` + argyle in `member_ids` and reuses `_run_argyle_mention`), so the thread is a private agent conversation with role-scoped tools; `@argyle` mentions elsewhere (family/event/group channels) work as before. PWA: Argyle appears in the New-chat picker (✨, violet `#8b5cf6`) and Argyle DMs render with the proper name/avatar (`channelDisplay` special-cases the peer id since system members are filtered out of `membersData`). The evening tomorrow-digest posts into this DM (see the tomorrow-digest bullet).
- **Prep kits on event surfaces + driver checkoff (v2.29.3)**: kit items now show everywhere events render, not just My Day. `GET /api/schedule` attaches `prep_by_event` (`{event_id: [items]}`, computed per request by `main._prep_by_event` over the payload's events — kit edits show without a cache rebuild; errands/background trips skipped) and `prep_confirmed` (list of confirmed parent event ids). PWA My Drives timeline cards show amber 🎒 item pills plus a **"Confirm packed" checkoff button** (green "✓ Packed" once tapped) — state keyed by the PARENT event instance id (`prepEventKey` strips `_dropoff`/`_pickup` so split legs share one checkoff; `_unrolled_` instances stay distinct), persisted via `POST /api/prep_status` `{event_id, confirmed, member_id}` into the `prep_status` table (`set_prep_confirmed` upserts; unconfirming deletes the row; `get_confirmed_preps()`). Family-view day cards and the PWA event modal ("🎒 Bring" row) show the pills read-only; the desktop calendar's event modal also gained a Bring section (items unioned across merged legs/instances). My Day ride cards and the tomorrow digest keep their existing prep lines.
- **Routable locations for agent-created events/errands (v2.29.4, `maps.resolve_routable_location`)**: LLM-extracted locations are usually bare venue names ("Mills Park Middle School") that the solver's routing can't handle. Creation paths now resolve them once through the normal geocoder (Mapbox with home-proximity bias → Nominatim fallback, `geocode_cache`-backed) and store the full display name (venue name prepended if the geocoder's display name lacks it). Wired into: intake proposal approval (`approve_proposal`), chat/agent `create_event` (`chat_actions._create_event` — the shared executor for both stacks), and agent errand tools (`handle_add_errand`/`handle_update_errand`). Safety rails: locations already containing a digit (street number, zip, "Field 3") pass through untouched, and any geocoder failure returns the original string — resolution never blocks creation. Tests: `tests/test_location_resolve.py`.
- **Pooled rewards / family goals (v2.31.0)**: rewards can be flagged `pooled` — children pool pledges toward one shared reward ("Family Movie Night", "Family Eats Out"). **A pledge is a HOLD, not a payment**: like a pending redemption it reduces spendable points (`get_spendable_points` = balance − pending redemptions − pledges, enforced on both redeem and contribute) but writes nothing to the ledger until a parent grants the funded pool — so withdraw/cancel need zero refund machinery. Pledges live in `pool_contributions` (one row per child per goal; repeat chip-ins add up; contributions **clamp to what's remaining** so the pool never exceeds cost and a grant deducts each child exactly their pledge). Optional per-goal `min_share` (each child must pledge ≥ N before grant; parent can force-override) — the anti-free-rider gate, alongside the visibility: **thermometers are segmented per contributor by member color** everywhere they render. Grant (parent token, `POST /api/rewards/{id}/pool/decide` `{approve, force?}`): one negative `redeem` ledger entry per contributor + ONE approved redemption row (`pooled: true, member_id: null`, per-child split in `contributions`) for history — the weekly digest renders it as "Family goal — {title}"; deny/cancel releases all pledges (nothing was spent). Releases also happen on points reset (same wedge-avoidance as auto-denied redemptions), reward deletion, and un-pooling a reward. API: `POST /api/rewards/{id}/contribute` `{member_id, amount}` (children only), `POST .../withdraw`, pool status embedded on `GET /api/rewards` (`pool: {pledged, remaining, funded, contributions, short}`); `GET /api/points/{id}` gained `spendable`/`pledged`. Notifications: chip-in → the OTHER children (peer visibility is the motivator), pool-fills → parents ("grant it in the app"), grant/cancel → contributors. Surfaces: PWA Chores tab "Family Goals" cards (child: inline chip-in + withdraw; parent: ✓ Grant when funded — a min-share 409 flips the button to "Grant anyway ⚠️" — and ✗ Release; funded goals count in the parent badge), kiosk board gets a shared **Family Goals thermometer strip** above the lanes plus per-lane chip-in rows (identity stays per-lane), /chores admin gets the 🎯 Family-goal checkbox + min-share on the reward form and pledge progress on reward rows. Agent tools in BOTH stacks: `get_family_goals`, `contribute_to_family_goal(reward_title, amount, member_name?)` (children only; driver-chat actor resolved server-side; same notification fan-out as the endpoint). Tests: `tests/test_pooled_rewards.py`.
- **Triage Diagnostics**: When an event cannot be scheduled, it falls into the "Unassigned" triage bucket. The solver provides granular diagnostics per driver. If an event has multiple passengers and one of them causes a physical overlap conflict (double booking) with another scheduled event, the UI intelligently catches the `passenger_conflict` diagnostic type and presents a one-click button to drop the conflicting passenger from that specific event instance.

## Free-Tier LLM Model Pools (services/model_pools.py, v2.30.0)

Every Gemini call in the app routes through quota-bucket pools so same-ability models combine their free-tier daily limits (each MODEL has its own quota; caps as of 2026-08-02):

- **lite** — `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (500/day), `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (500/day), `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` (20/day tail) → ~1,020 fast requests/day.
