Asked for from the house and correct: going dark 30 minutes *after* sunset matches going light 30 minutes *before* sunrise — the same darkness of sky is on the other side of the event in the morning — so the symmetric setting is `+30`/`−30`, which one shared number cannot express.
- **`_next_after()` steps by whole days in BOTH directions**, because an offset moves a sun event either way and both cases are real: `−30` drags a still-future `next_rising` into the past ten minutes before sunrise, and `+30` has to catch tonight's switch five minutes *after* sunset, when `next_setting` has already rolled over to tomorrow and the naive reading says "goes dark in 24 hours" — the panel would stay light all evening. Sunrise drifts a couple of minutes a day, well inside what anyone sees happen to a wall panel.
- **The flip is a timer, not a poll.** `profile()` returns `next_flip`; `nav.html`'s `scheduleThemeFlip()` re-**asks** at that moment rather than toggling the attribute itself, so a tablet that slept through the boundary and a household that changed the offset this afternoon both come back correct. The delay is capped at 6h — `setTimeout`'s 32-bit delay fires *instantly* past ~24.9 days, which would be a poll loop rather than a timer — and cleared on navigation so pages do not stack one timer each.
- **No HA, no sun entity, or timestamps in an unrecognised shape → `dark`** (after trying the entity's own `above_horizon`/`below_horizon` state). A panel that has lost Home Assistant at 2am must not decide to light up the kitchen.
- Hand path: **/home#panel-setup**, theme dropdown plus the two offset fields, which appear only under Sun. Pinned in `test_home_board.py` (six scenarios: which side of the next change we are on, both offset directions including the roll-over trap, the fallbacks, that the profile never leaks `sun`, and that the client re-asks under a capped timer).

## Argyle speaks INTO a room — announcements over the HA bridge (v2.144.0)

- **The ask, verbatim**: "Hey Argyle, Lily is in the pool house. Tell her it is time for dinner." The conversation-agent channel cannot do this by itself — it is inbound-only (HA calls `/api/v2/converse` and the reply is spoken on the device that ASKED) — so reaching a different room is a service call from our side: `assist_satellite.announce` speaks through the target satellite's own pipeline (same voice Argyle answers in), and rooms with only a media player fall back to `tts.speak` (first `tts.*` entity), which ducks what is playing and resumes it.
- **Rooms come from HA's area registry over REST via the template API** (`ha_api.render_template` → `get_area_map()` cached like the states cache, `resolve_area_id()`), because the registries proper are WebSocket-only and this app deliberately speaks REST alone. `area_entities()` resolves device-inherited membership; `area_id()` is the only REST-reachable way to see the family's area ALIASES.
- **Matching skews toward finding a room** (`services/announce.py: match_area`): folded-name equality → HA alias lookup → containment either way → edit distance at cutoff 0.6. Everything arrives through STT; "pool hose" is the pool house. A wrong room is recoverable out loud; a refused match just fails the dinner call.
- **One target per room, never a blast** (`pick_target`): the family's pin (`settings.announce_targets`, area_id → entity, membership in the HA area NOT required — the pin exists for rooms the registry has wrong) → voice satellite → the player already PLAYING (audibly on, somebody near it) → idle beats off, Music Assistant players (`mass_player_type`) beat bare cast targets, name order for determinism. Unavailable entities are skipped everywhere, including a dead pin.
- **`assist_satellite.announce` blocks until the words finish** — HA holds the call open — so `call_service` grew a per-call `timeout` and announce passes 30s; the 8s default would report a played announcement as failed.
- **Dual delivery**: `announce_and_echo` drops "📢 (said in the Pool House): …" into the recipient's DMs (sender = resolved actor, else Argyle) so headphones don't eat the call. A failed echo logs and stays a success — an error result would send the agent around the loop to announce dinner twice.
- **Agent path**: `announce_to_room(room, message, recipient_name?, from_member?)` in agent_tools_v2 + router (terminal action). Unlike the message tools the sender is OPTIONAL — an announcement is the house talking, and the wall panel doesn't know whose finger tapped it.
- **Hand path** (`/map`): the 📢 Announce bar — room picker (only rooms `/api/announce/rooms` says have a speaker; the button hides when none do), optional DM-copy recipient, message, and the ⚙️ per-room speaker pin that saves `announce_targets` (partial settings POST; all pins travel together so saving one room cannot drop another). `POST /api/announce` returns the agent-tool shape, not HTTP errors — "rooms I know: …" is the useful thing to show.
- Pinned in `tests/test_announce.py` (8 scenarios: STT mangles + aliases, satellite-first, audible-player heuristics, pin + dead-pin fallback, both service-call shapes + the timeout, honest failure sentences, echo-never-retries, tool wiring).

## One voice for the whole house (v2.144.1)

- **Reported immediately**: the pool house (tts.speak fallback) answered in a different voice than the kitchen satellite. The satellite path speaks with its pipeline's TTS voice for free; the fallback grabbed the first `tts.*` entity with its default voice.
- **`ha_api.ws_command()`** — the first WebSocket use in the app, and deliberately a ONE-SHOT (connect → auth → one command → close): the REST-only rule was protecting against a persistent client to keep alive, not the transport. `websockets` was already a dependency (Sendspin relay). **`get_pipeline_tts()`** reads `assist_pipeline/pipeline/list`, picks the pipeline whose conversation agent is ours (entity id carries the config-entry title, so it matches 'chauffeur' OR 'argyle'), else HA's preferred pipeline; cached 300s with stale fallback.
- **`announce._tts_config()`**: when the pipeline names a real `tts.` entity, its engine + `language` + `options.voice` ride the `tts.speak` call; a legacy engine name ('cloud') cannot carry its voice over tts.speak and degrades to the old first-entity behaviour rather than smuggling one engine's voice id onto another (engines reject foreign voice ids).

## Argyle's voice is a dropdown, and it is not a setting (v2.145.0)

- **The ask**: change Argyle's voice from Chauffeur, "based on the options available in HA, and force it everywhere." The second half was the design trap: a stored Chauffeur voice setting would split-brain the first time somebody edited the pipeline in HA's own UI, and "forcing" means an enforcement loop fighting the other writer.
- **So the picker is a REMOTE CONTROL, not a copy.** `GET /api/announce/voices` asks the Argyle pipeline's own TTS engine for its voice list (`tts/engine/voices` over the one-shot websocket) plus the current choice; `POST /api/announce/voice` → `ha_api.set_pipeline_voice()` writes `tts_voice` onto the pipeline itself via `assist_pipeline/pipeline/update`. HA stays the single source of truth, and "everywhere" happens by construction: satellite replies, satellite announces, and the tts.speak fallback all read the pipeline (v2.144.1).
- **The update is a whole-object PUT in websocket clothing** — omitting a field nulls it on the pipeline, unknown extras fail validation — hence the `_PIPELINE_FIELDS` allowlist that sends every field back with only `tts_voice` changed. Success busts the pipeline cache so the very next announcement speaks in the new voice.
- Hand path: the ⚙️ area of the /map announce bar grew an "Argyle's voice" dropdown (loaded lazily — it is a websocket round-trip HA-side; hides itself when the engine offers no list, e.g. a legacy engine name). Pinned in `tests/test_announce.py` (10 scenarios now: + our-pipeline-beats-preferred, whole-object PUT, cache bust, refused-update honesty).

## The dismissal tap was clicking the page below (v2.145.1)

- **Reported from the panel**: waking the screensaver navigated — the tap landed on whatever shelf button or tile sat under the finger.
- **Why the existing swallow didn't swallow**: the overlay cancelled `pointerdown` (which suppresses the compatibility mousedown/mouseup) and removed itself — but per the pointer-events spec the synthesised **`click` still fires**, and it is **hit-tested at release**. With the overlay already gone, the click resolved to the element underneath.
- **The fix is a shield, not a faster removal**: on the press the overlay goes invisible (`opacity: 0`, still hit-testable) and stays put until the gesture is over — the click dies on it (capture, preventDefault), or `pointerup`/`pointercancel` passes with no click and it leaves 400ms later; a 10s fallback covers a pointer that never reports release. Long-press wakes are covered because the shield waits for the release rather than a fixed timer.
- Pinned both ways in `tests/test_screensaver.py`: template contract (shield listeners present) and the jsdom probe now walks the full gesture — press stops the slideshow but must NOT remove the overlay, the click must arrive `defaultPrevented`, and only then does the shield leave.

## Outside hands — work this household doesn't do itself (load arc A1, v2.146.0)

Design brief: `docs/household_load_design.md`. The first arc of the load program, and the first shipped piece of it.

- **The finding**: carpool is how families actually cover, and the app could not see it. A carpool-covered event was either assigned to a family driver (a lie the solver then planned around) or left unassigned — firing the watcher DM `🚨 No driver yet` at both parents for a ride that was always handled. **And it was never only carpool**: the neighbour who comes to do the dishes had no place in the app either. So the noun is `AssistContact`, not `CarpoolDriver` — **carpool is a kind of help, not a kind of person**.
- **`AssistContact`** (`models/schemas.py`): `{name, phone, email, relation_label, kinds[], relationship, color_code, notes, active}`. `relation_label` is what the family actually says out loud ("Emma's mom") and it **leads every surface** — a child knows "Emma's mom" and may not know her name. **NOT a `FamilyMember`**: no login, no PIN, no headcount, no wall board, no DMs. That is the line against the `helper` ROLE, an external person who *does* hold the app — three points on one axis: a contact we only record, a helper with driving surfaces, a full member.
- **`kinds` is a free tag list and NOTHING branches on it.** Behaviour comes from what REFERENCES the contact: one referenced by a drive is a carpool driver, one referenced by a chore is house help. Branch on the tag and every new kind of help becomes a code change.
- **`relationship`** (`reciprocal | paid | volunteer`) exists so the turn ledger (slice 2) fires for a carpool parent and stays silent for someone you pay — you owe her money, not a turn. No amounts are tracked; the money cut stands.
- **Coverage is the third assignment state.** `storage.assist_assignments` keyed by event_id (one coverer per event; setting replaces rather than stacks). The refresh filters covered events out of the set handed to CP-SAT and **puts them back into `events` afterwards** — the solver never sees them, the timeline still draws them. They ride the daily payload, both combined-payload paths, and the `/api/schedule` custom-range assembly as `assist_assignments`, with `assist_contacts` alongside so no surface needs a second request.
- **Coverage rides the daily hash** (`hash_events(events, assist_map=…)`). Handing a drive to a carpool parent changes nothing about the EVENT, so without this the day's cache stayed valid and the solver kept a household driver on a ride somebody else was making — the change would have appeared to do nothing until the next forced refresh.
- **Two false alarms retired.** `watchers._unassigned_findings` skips covered events (`🚨 No driver yet` for a handled ride), and `status_protocols.send_coverage_reports` **names the carpool parent** instead of printing "⚠️ needs a driver" — telling the co-parent a carpool run is open is exactly the wrong thing to hand them on a hard week.
- **The kid is told who they're riding with.** `_ride()` carries `assist`, and the digest phrase **leads** with it (`— 🚗 riding with Emma's mom`) because no household driver name will ever appear to answer the question. This was invisible before: a covered ride had no driver, so the digest said nothing.
- **The timeline gives them a column**, injected in the SHARED renderer (`components/schedule_timeline.html`) exactly as ghost drivers are, so the dashboard and the wall-panel tile cannot disagree. `group: 'assist'` sorts them after the household and before the ghosts — real coverage outranks a hypothetical extra person — and the existing empty-column hiding means a contact covering nothing today draws nothing.
- **Hand paths, both halves.** Config → People → **Outside hands** manages the contact book (deleting warns how many drives come back to the family); on the dashboard, **dropping a drive on a contact's column hands it over**, and dragging it out to Unassigned takes it back — deliberately routed through `/api/assist-coverage` rather than the override path, because an override says "this driver whatever the solver thinks" while this says "not ours at all".
- **The picker had to be FINDABLE, which took three fixes (v2.174.0).** It existed and worked, and still could not be found. (1) It was drawn only in the event modal's **edit** mode, filed between "Start Time Override" and "Attendance Mode" — but handing a drive away is a scheduling decision, not an event-config detail, so it now sits in the event **view** (`populateAssistView`, suppressed on kiosk/read-only). (2) It **hid itself whenever the contact list came back empty**, so a family with nobody set up saw no control and no hint that a prerequisite existed; an empty list is now a thing to say (`ASSIST_EMPTY_HINT` names Settings → People → Outside hands) rather than a reason to vanish. (3) The list it read was `currentData.assist_contacts` — the schedule payload, **rebuilt only by a solve** — so a contact added a minute ago did not appear until an unrelated re-sync happened to land; `loadAssistContacts()` now fetches `/api/assist-contacts` when the modal opens, keeping the payload copy as the offline fallback. The general rule: a hand path assertion must ask whether the control is *reachable in the state the family is actually in*, not whether the function exists.
- **The phone can do it too (v2.174.0).** The PWA event sheet had no coverage display and no handoff at all — and the phone is the surface you are holding when a carpool actually gets arranged. It now shows a **Covered by** row (label first, tap-to-call number) and a **Hand to someone outside** button (`handDriveToOutsideHand`) that opens the shared `promptChoice` picker, fetching contacts fresh for the same staleness reason. Coverage is keyed by the EVENT while the phone opens **legs**, so the id is normalised (`baseEventId` strips `_dropoff`/`_pickup`) — handing away "the drive" means both halves. Kids do not get the button: reassigning the family's drives to the neighbours is not their decision.
- **Agent tools in both stacks**: `cover_with_assist(event_name, contact_name?, target_date?, clear?)` and `get_assist_coverage(target_date?)`, both terminal, both setting `schedule_dirty`. Contacts resolve by **what the family calls them** — `_find_assist_contact` feeds `_match_person` the label plus punctuation-stripped variants, because speech-to-text writes "emmas mom" and a tag of "Emma's mom" never matches it. An ambiguous tag ("mom", with two carpool parents) falls through rather than handing the drive to the wrong household.
- **`get_assist_coverage` reads coverage from STORAGE, not the schedule cache.** Coverage is written the moment somebody says "Emma's mom is taking them"; the cache only learns about it when the background re-solve lands, so reading the cache would answer "nobody is covering anything" to a question asked ten seconds after the change. Events come from the cache; the truth does not.
- **Recurrence: instance or series, the same question everything else asks (v2.175.0).** Coverage was per-occurrence only, which made the standing arrangement inexpressible — "Emma's mom has Tuesdays" meant clicking every Tuesday, and only the ones already fetched into the sync window, so the arrangement silently lapsed. Scope now mirrors event configs and overrides exactly (`promptConfirm("Recurring Event", …, "Entire Series", "This Instance")`, dashboard and phone): an instance row is keyed by the event id, a series row by the bare `recurring_event_id`. **Resolution is instance-first** (`assist.coverage_for`, two dict lookups, never a scan), which is what makes the useful exception sayable — "she has Tuesdays, except this one". **Clearing takes BOTH keys**: "we're driving it after all" answered per-occurrence would leave the series row quietly re-covering the same event on the next solve. The series key is not the event's own id, so `hash_events` resolves the same way or a standing hand-over would leave every daily cache valid and appear to do nothing.
- **The recurrence model stops at one line.** The refresh resolves coverage to INSTANCE keys before building the payload, so `assist_assignments` still reaches the digest, the coverage report, the timeline and the phone keyed by the event in front of them. Six consumers were already reading that map; none of them learned what a series is.
- **Active vs history (v2.175.0).** `assist_assignments` holds only coverage that can still change a solve; `assist_history` is append-only and never read by the solve path. `archive_past_assist_assignments` runs at the top of every refresh and moves spent INSTANCE rows across — a series row has no single date to be past and never archives, since that would silently end the arrangement. Rows carry `event_date`/`event_title` written at hand-over time, so archiving is a date comparison over one table rather than a lookup that would make the sweep depend on a solve. Everything lands in history — covered, cleared, archived, contact_deleted — so deleting a contact removes them from the app but not from what actually happened. `GET /api/assist-history` is the read; nothing in the hot path calls it.
- **The agent tool stops lying about ranges.** Its description offered *"the Kellys have practice this week"* as an example while the implementation covered exactly one occurrence and reported success — a false confirmation, which is worse than a missing feature because the family stops thinking about it. `cover_with_assist` now takes `scope`, and `get_assist_coverage` resolves instance-then-series or a standing arrangement reads as "nobody is covering anything" while the solver honours it.
- Pinned in `tests/test_assist.py` (16 scenarios, run against **both** storage backends): stored-not-typed, carpool-is-a-kind-of-help, one coverer per event, deleting takes coverage with it, the covered event leaves the solver but returns to `events`, the daily hash including the series key, both false alarms, the kid's phrase, the agent round trip including both error paths, label matching, a series arrangement covering an occurrence further out than the sync window has ever reached, one occurrence escaping the series, take-back ending the arrangement, archive-moves-instances-never-series, and the hand-path guarantee — which now asserts the empty-state hint, the on-demand contact fetch, the view-mode picker, and the phone's handoff, because "the function is present" is what let an invisible picker pass for two versions.

## Household tasks — work with a deadline and no destination (load arc A2, v2.147.0)

The keystone of the load program. Design brief: `docs/household_load_design.md`.

- **The finding**: `Errand` requires `location` and `duration_mins` because an errand IS a drive the solver routes. So "sign the permission slip", "call the pediatrician", "renew the passports", "$12 for picture day" had **nowhere in the app to live** — they became a fake all-day calendar event or nothing. And `assigned_to`/`assignee`/`owner_member` returned zero hits repo-wide, so nothing an adult owned could be handed to the other adult.
- **`HouseholdTask`** (`models/schemas.py`): `{title, notes, due_date?, assigned_to?, created_by, status, completed_at/by, recurrence, category, effort_mins, occasion_id, member_id, event_id, assist_contact_id, source}`. **Task = do something. Errand = go somewhere** — the line stays crisp so "renew the passports" never becomes a routing job competing for a Tuesday slot.
- **`assigned_to` is optional, and unassigned is a REAL STATE** meaning *the household owes this*. That is where delegation lives; an object that forces an owner at creation just moves the deciding back onto whoever typed it. `claim_household_task` is the delegation path, and the errands page renders "nobody yet" as a tappable state rather than a blank.
- **Yearly recurrence, which errands cannot express** (they offer daily/weekly/monthly) and which is exactly the life-admin cadence: inspection, physicals, passports, registration windows. Completing a recurring task **regenerates the next one** (the same shape recurring errands use), with month-end and 29-Feb clamping so the 31st of a 30-day month lands on the 30th rather than raising.
- **Intake's fourth target.** `calendar_id: 'household_task'` on proposal approval lands the item on the household list — the capture layer's extraction prompt has always named *"permission slip due, payment due, picture day"* and had nowhere to put them (not a calendar event, not an errand which demands a location, not a `KidTask` which is a child's own school list). Deliberately created **unassigned**: picking an owner at approval would move the deciding back onto whoever tapped the button.
- **Two watcher findings** (`watchers._household_task_findings`): past due (saying *how* late), and — the one that earns the object — **dated, due within 2 days, and still belonging to nobody**. An undated task is never chased: "sort the garage" is real work, not a deadline.
- **The load ledger** (`GET /api/household-load`, `get_household_load` tool). Tasks completed + drives driven from the existing daily snapshots. **States it, never scores it** — the occasions `_load_balance` voice: *"7 of the 9 things done in the last 30 days were Lorena's."* No percentages, no leaderboard, no chart; adults need division of labour, not gamification, and a fairness chart between two spouses is a fight generator.
- **`FamilyMember.assist_tier`** (`household | assist`) ships with this. **Covering is not carrying**: a teenager who drives and cooks, a hired nanny, a grandparent are named SEPARATELY in the ledger and never folded into the household's split. The tier is member-level, not on `Driver`, because assisting is not driving-specific — hanging it on the driver record would have leaked the moment a teenager cooked.
- **Hand paths**: the errands page is now **Errands & tasks**, and its header teaches the distinction ("an errand is a drive… a task is work with a deadline and nowhere to drive"). Add/claim/complete/delete inline, with the ledger's one sentence in the panel header. Wall-panel tile **"The household owes"** — past due first, then unclaimed, then the rest, with "nobody yet" rendered as a state.
- **Agent tools, both stacks**: `add_household_task`, `get_household_tasks`, `complete_household_task`, `claim_household_task`, `get_household_load`. All terminal; actor resolution is attribution, never a gate.
- Pinned in `tests/test_household_tasks.py` (12 scenarios, both storage backends): no destination, undated-is-real-and-never-late, the household-owes finding, past-due wording, yearly recurrence, recurrence clamping edges, delegation, the agent round trip, the ledger's separation of assist tier, the intake target, the hand-path guarantee, and the wall tile's unclaimed band.

## Requests — the ask as a first-class object (load arc A3, v2.148.0)

- **Two problems, one shape.** A kid could REPORT ("practice moved") but not ASK — *"can you get me at 3 instead of 4"*, the single most common kid-to-parent logistics message in existence, lived only as free text. And an adult who wanted out of a drive could only TAKE it from their partner: the override landed and the other parent got a bare "Schedule Updated" push with no indication it was an ask, which is a large part of why the non-driving parent feels informed-at rather than invited. One object for both, because the state machine, the surfaces and the rails are identical.
- **`Request`** (`models/schemas.py`): `{from_member, to_member?, kind, subject_ref?, subject_label, body, status, reason, decided_by/at, expires_at}`. `body` is their own words, never generated. Kinds: `ride_change | pickup_early | swap_drive | take_task | cover | permission | other`.
- **A request is ALWAYS answered.** Unanswered asks expire after 20h and say so **loudly** — to the asker, who otherwise learns nothing, and to whoever owed the answer. Swept on its own 30-minute cadence in the push loop. Silence is the failure mode this object exists to fix, so a quiet evaporation would defeat the whole point.
- **Accepting PERFORMS the change.** `take_task` assigns the task; `swap_drive` writes the override and flags `schedule_dirty`. Only kinds that NAME a subject act — a `permission` or a bare `other` is a conversation, and inventing an action for it would be the app guessing at what a family meant.
- **Declining is first-class and blameless.** `reason` lives on the object rather than being lost in a chat message, and the asker is told *why* — "🙅 Jeff can't — in a meeting until 5". A no with a reason beats silence every time.
- **An unaddressed ask belongs to every adult.** "Somebody please take this" is a real ask and must not require picking a victim, so `to_member: None` fans out to all adults and appears in each of their lists.
- **Hand path** in the PWA My Day, above the day because an unanswered ask is the most time-sensitive thing on the screen: incoming asks with one-tap Yes/Can't (a reason is *offered*, never demanded), your own outstanding asks with withdraw, and a **"🙋 Ask for something…"** button offered only on TODAY — an ask about a day you are merely looking at would arrive without its context.
- **Agent tools, both stacks**: `make_request`, `get_requests`, `answer_request`. `answer_request` refuses to guess between two open asks and lists them instead. The kid persona now separates the two explicitly: *"propose_family_action for FACTS they are reporting; make_request for things they are ASKING for"*, with an instruction to say plainly that nothing is decided yet and **never promise the answer**.
- Pinned in `tests/test_requests.py` (12 scenarios, both backends): kid-can-ask, accepting performs both actions, a kind that names nothing performs nothing, blameless decline with the reason reaching the asker, loud expiry (both directions, once only), double-answer refusal, withdraw ownership, both-directions summary, the agent round trip, the which-one refusal, the hand-path guarantee, and the persona line.

## Stages — the child that grows (load arc A4, v2.149.0)

- **The finding**: `role == 'child'` was the entire model — no birthdate, age or grade field existed anywhere, nothing branched on a child's age, so a six-year-old and a sixteen-year-old got the identical 64px-glyph card, the identical "Hi James! 👋", the identical points economy. The only exit was a role flip to `adult`, which deleted the kid lens in one move.
- **Four stages, named for what changes** (`services/stages.py`), mapped to the shape a family already thinks in: **Sprout** (<6, preschool — glyph-led, today only), **Explorer** (6–11, elementary — points and streaks land honestly; today's Buddy UI), **Navigator** (12–14, middle — points stop leading, request rights, two-week horizon, assignable jobs, location privacy), **Copilot** (15+, high — driving, own commitments).
- **The bands are three CONFIGURABLE cutoffs** (`Settings.stage_cutoffs`, default `[6, 12, 15]`), not four ranges — contiguous bands defined by cutoffs cannot develop gaps or overlaps the way independently edited ranges can. Configurable because school systems differ: US middle school usually starts at 11-turning-12, so a fixed 12 puts a sixth grader in the wrong stage for half the year. Garbage settings (non-increasing, wrong length, wrong type) fall back to the defaults rather than making a band unreachable.
- **A birthdate only ever SUGGESTS.** `stage_override` pins a child wherever suits them (and a pinned child is never nagged to move — the pin IS the answer); `capability_overrides` bends single switches, so a mature eleven-year-old can hold request rights without being aged up wholesale. No birthdate lands in the safe middle (Explorer), never a crash or a Sprout lockdown.
- **The switch list** (`CAPABILITIES`): glyph scale, density, horizon days, show_points, show_streaks, can_request, assignable_tasks, can_drive, private_location. **Surfaces ask for a capability BY NAME** — `stages.can(member, 'can_request')` server-side, `kidCan('show_points')` in the PWA (capabilities ride the `/api/members` payload) — so no surface ever computes an age.
- **Growing up is GRANTED, never silently switched.** `stage_acknowledged` is what a parent has confirmed; the suggestion moving past it raises a *moment* — a watcher line ("🌱 Lily is 12 now — ready to be a Navigator", deduped per transition, not per sweep) and a Config → People → **Growing up** card that names the new capabilities out loud before a parent taps "Yes, they're growing up".
- **Nothing is deleted when a child moves up.** The points ledger, streaks and tier badges survive a Navigator — `show_points: false` is the difference between *hiding* and *erasing*, and the member stays `role='child'` with the whole kid lens.
- **The gate is server-side**: a Sprout's `make_request` is refused in `requests.create` itself ("this one goes through a grown-up in person"), not merely hidden in the UI. The PWA shell reads the same switches for the greeting ("Hi ⭐👋" vs "Hey"), the points pill, streak visibility, the look-ahead horizon (Sprout: today; Explorer: a week; Navigator/Copilot: a fortnight), and the Ask button.
- **Every switch now has its surface (v2.167.0** — the Navigator/Copilot grants stopped being ceremony):
  - **`assignable_tasks`** — the errands-page owner dropdown opens to stage-capable kids, and `stages.refuse_task_assignment` refuses everywhere else: `POST/PATCH /api/household-tasks` and `agent_tools_v2.add_household_task`/`claim_household_task` (one door, both agent stacks). A `capability_overrides` bend opens it for a mature eleven-year-old without aging them up.
  - **`private_location`** — `/api/family/locations?viewer=<member_id>`: the PWA map sends its selected member (any family member sees the pins); the wall panel and /map send nothing and are the kiosk, where a private-stage kid's zone and pin are withheld and the chip says `🔒` instead (never a silent absence). `home_board._tile_map` applies the same rule to the board's injected payload, skipping the HA read entirely. Driving context survives either way — "en route to practice" is the schedule speaking, which the kiosk already shows.
  - **`can_drive`** — "+ Driving" on Config → People only exists for a kid at Copilot (or already linked); `create_driver` refuses a first-time driver named for a below-stage child member server-side, and when a Copilot's profile IS created their member lands on `assist_tier='assist'` — the teen and the wheel: they COVER drives without CARRYING the household's share, so their driving never makes the parents' split look even.
  - **`glyph_scale` + `density`** — the kid My Day shell (`kidShell()` in app.html) scales with the stage: Sprout gets an 80px hero glyph and roomy rows; Explorer keeps today's Buddy tuning (64px/roomy); Navigator shrinks glyphs and tightens rows (40px hero, tight padding); Copilot drops the glyph column and the picture-banner hero entirely — every item a plain row, a real schedule, not a picture book.
- Hand path (reshaped v2.152.0): **birthdays live on the identity card** (Config → People → member → Identity), for *every* member — a birthdate is a fact about a person, not a stage setting, and adult birthdates are what make birthday tracking possible later. **Config → People → Growing up** keeps the stage machinery: per-child stage pins, the confirmation moment, and the **band timeline** — four stage-colored bands over one age axis (1–20y), each stage's glyph/label above its band, three draggable cutoff handles (snap to whole years, clamped one year inside their neighbours so the strictly-increasing invariant holds by construction, auto-save on release), and the kids themselves plotted at their ages (avatar/photo, ring colored by live-recomputed band, pinned kids sit mid-band with a 📌). Moving a handle shows which child it flips before you drop it. Member rows wear a read-only stage chip. `stage_cutoffs` registered in the settings index; stage glyphs (🌱🔍🧭🚗) live on `STAGES`.
- Pinned in `tests/test_stages.py` (14 scenarios, both backends): the school-shaped bands, configurable+sanitised cutoffs, suggestion-vs-pin, bundle+single-switch overrides, adults-are-not-staged, the granted transition, once-per-transition watcher, nothing-deleted, the server-side Sprout gate, capabilities on the members payload, household-jobs-arrive-with-Navigator (both doors + the override bend), whereabouts-to-the-family-not-the-kiosk (viewer keying, adults untouched, invented viewer = kiosk), driving-arrives-with-Copilot (refusal, assist tier, non-member names ignored), and the hand path.

## The dual-income safety net (load arc A5, v2.150.0)

- **School days get KINDS** (`school.school_day_kind`): `full | half | delayed | closed | weekend | break`. `break` (v2.152.1) is a weekday outside the school year — manual bounds or detected intervals, via `school._in_school_year` — and `closed` is reserved for a closure DURING the year; blurring them made every August weekday read as a surprise closure and Argyle announced each one. The boolean `school_in_session` was blind to the day that bites a two-income household hardest — **the half day nobody has PTO booked for** — and to late starts. New all-day keyword sets (`HALF_DAY_KEYWORDS`: early release/early dismissal/minimum day…, `DELAYED_KEYWORDS`: late start/two-hour delay…) parse from the same school calendar with the same discipline: a TIMED event never shortens a day, closure keywords on another event outrank a delay, and kinds **fail toward `full`** exactly as the boolean fails open — a broken calendar must never invent closures.
- **Aftercare as a care window** (`FamilyMember.aftercare_days/until/place`, children only, edited on the member card beside school hours and the bus). The actual fallback most two-income families use, previously inexpressible. On an aftercare day the **dismissal push leads with the truth of the afternoon** — "🏫 Extended Day today / Extended Day until 5:30 — Mom gets you" — naming the place what the kid calls it and the driver whose ride sits near the aftercare end, instead of pushing a ride at a bell that isn't the real deadline. No cost/sessions/billing: we don't do money.
- **Childcare-gap detection over 21 days** (`school.care_gap_days` + `watchers._care_gap_findings`) — the app knew school was closed and knew both parents' calendars and never computed the intersection. Deliberately far outside the 3-day watcher window, because closures are known weeks ahead and PTO needs lead time. `break` days are never gaps — summer is a season the family plans for, same as weekends. **Framed as a decision with named options** (a parent takes the day, a grandparent, a carpool family, a camp day), never a 🚨 alarm; keyed per (day, kind) so it is said once. **Aftercare softens a half day** (that is what it is for — a half day where every child has aftercare that weekday is not a gap, and a partial gap names who still needs covering, not who is fine) **but never a closure** (a closed school closes its aftercare with it). A house with no children has no childcare gaps.
- **The status→solver injection now runs the full build horizon** (was hardcoded 14 days against a 30-day `days_to_build`): a cover day 20 days out was announced to the whole family while the solver went right on scheduling that parent for it.
- Pinned in `tests/test_care_gaps.py` (9 scenarios, both backends): day kinds, fail-toward-full, the all-day-only keyword parse, gaps found weeks ahead, summer-is-a-break-not-a-wall-of-closures (manual bounds AND detected intervals), aftercare softening (and the who-needs-covering naming), decision-not-alarm framing, the aftercare dismissal push, and the horizon fix.

## Outlets, quiet hours on the identity, and the household briefing (load arc A6, v2.151.0)

The last arc of the load program. The finding: **an adult's own life existed in the app only as an obstacle** — a personal calendar event's entire function was making you unavailable to drive. Nothing said your time was FOR something, noticed when it eroded, or made it real by covering it.

- **`ProtectedCommitment`** (`models/schemas.py`, `/api/commitments`, Config → People → **Protected time**): a run club, therapy, choir — `{member_id, title, days_of_week, time_start/end, needs_coverage}`. Injected into every solve as recurring time-windowed `unavailable` Rules (same machinery as status days; mutations invalidate the schedule caches, and the endpoints force a refresh, because protecting an evening that stays scheduled is not protecting it).
- **`clear_deck` and `give_space` finally have teeth.** The design doc promised "the solver protects the evening" and the code never emitted anything for them. They now emit an **evening-scoped** ban (17:00 on), not a whole-day one — "keep the evening free" is a claim about the evening, and banning the school run too would overshoot the family's own words.
- **Erosion watch** (`watchers._commitment_findings`): the solver is banned from the window, so a drive can only land there through a manual override — exactly how an outlet dies quietly, one reasonable-seeming exception at a time. The watcher names it before the evening is lost ("⏳ Thursday's run is about to be lost to a drive — that took an override, so make sure it was worth it"). `needs_coverage` additionally flags open drives inside the window days ahead: wishing for time does not produce time; covered time does.
- **Quiet hours on the IDENTITY** (`FamilyMember.quiet_start/quiet_end`, edited on the member's own card): a kid's window is a protection set by someone else and stays in household config; an adult's is a preference owned by the self — a night-shift parent, a 6am riser and a hired driver share no window. **Absent = the household default (21:00–08:00), never "off"**; `start == end` disables (kid grammar). Honoured in `_notify_member_lanes` (non-urgent sends **skip, never defer** — kid rule) and in the chat push fan-out (the message still lands in the thread; only the ping is skipped). **`urgent=True` escapes** — a 5:30am departure must fire at 5:10 — and prep reminders stay deliberately urgent (a family that set 20:30 asked to be told at 20:30).
- **`notify_lanes`** (`all | push | ha`) settles the stale backlog item: members with both web push and HA notify got everything twice. Same card, same sentence: how and when to reach me.
- **The household briefing** (`family_digest.build_household_briefing`) replaces the per-driver evening digest for parents/adults: tomorrow for the WHOLE family — drives with drivers, carpool coverage named ("Emma's mom (covered)"), household tasks due, each open item flagged. **Openings first, handled underneath**, with the rule that keeps it from nagging: *it shows openings, not assignments* — "⚠️ Open — nobody has these yet" is an invitation to tap, never an allocation. The title carries the count ("🏠 Tomorrow: 2 things still open" / "all handled"). **Helpers and unlinked drivers keep the per-driver digest** — another family's nanny has no business receiving this household's whole picture — and any briefing failure falls back to the per-driver path for everyone.
- Pinned in `tests/test_outlets.py` (11 scenarios, both backends): default-on-never-off, per-member windows + the disable grammar + kids excluded, skip-vs-urgent at the notify path, lane preferences, the commitment→rule injection, clear_deck's evening teeth, the erosion call-out, needs-cover flagging, openings-not-assignments, carpool-as-covered in the briefing, and the hand paths.

## Intake dedupe: title matching was never going to be enough (v2.177.0)

- **The finding**: reminder emails kept producing duplicates of events already on the calendar. Dedupe was working; the *key* was wrong. Approval writes the family's EDITED title back onto both the proposal and the calendar event ([main.py](chauffeur/main.py) approve path), so the next reminder's extraction is compared against your wording rather than the school's. The subset rule survives you shortening a title but not adding to it — rename "Fall Picture Day Reminder – Grades K-5" to "Lily – picture day" and neither token set contains the other (overlap ≈0.4, under the 0.75 bar). **Personalising a title breaks title matching for that event permanently**, and personalising is what people do.
- **Rule 2: time + place, title ignored.** Same person, same place, same minute is a double-booking, not two events. Two guards carry the design: **end time is deliberately not in the key** (`normalize_item` invents `end = start + 1h` whenever the email omits one, so requiring agreement would fail against most real calendar entries), and **all-day items are excluded entirely** (they all share midnight, which would make "same start" match everything on the day). Location comparison is punctuation-folded containment with a 4-character floor — an email's "Riverside Park" against a calendar's "Riverside Park, 12 Mill Rd" — and the target calendar corroborates when there is no location. The calendar is only ever corroboration: `calendar_id` is a routing GUESS, so a wrong guess costs a missed dedupe (a visible duplicate), never a wrong one.
- **Ordering fix that made rule 2 possible at all**: routing used to be applied AFTER the duplicate check, so the calendar the rule corroborates on did not exist yet. The proposal is now complete before it is judged.
- **Rule 3: the model annotates, it never omits.** The extraction call already happens per message, so the family's calendar for the window rides along in the prompt (`known_events_block`, capped at 120 title+time lines) and the model returns `duplicate_of` — semantic matching at **zero extra LLM requests**. The instruction is explicit that it must still return the item. This is the whole safety property: an omitted item never existed and no hedge could show it, while a labelled one is recorded, visible and restorable like every other skip. Only the REAL calendar plus *pending* proposals go into that block — an ignored proposal is precisely something the family said is not happening, and listing it as already on the calendar would have the model reason from a lie. Deterministic dedupe still checks proposals of every status.
- **The hedge that makes all of it safe: skips are recorded, not dropped.** A skipped item becomes a proposal with `status='duplicate'` carrying `duplicate_of` / `duplicate_start` / `duplicate_source` / `duplicate_rule`, listed on /intake under "Skipped as duplicates" with the rule named in plain words ("same time and place, on the calendar", "read as the same event"), and **Propose anyway** (`POST /api/proposals/{id}/restore`) puts one back in the queue. The duplicate_* fields survive the restore — the record of what the app thought is worth more than a tidy row. Without this, the title-blind and model-judged rules would be silent failures waiting to happen: a duplicate costs one click to dismiss, a wrongly suppressed event is never learned about at all. Run summaries and log lines now separate `duplicates` from low-confidence `dropped`, which the old shared "N dropped as duplicate/low-confidence" line hid.
- Pinned in `tests/test_email_ingest.py`: caught-with-no-title-overlap end to end, the restore round trip, every time+place guard (disagreeing end times still match, all-day excluded, half an hour apart is a different event, a 3-character place name is too weak, different person with no location), the model's label honoured and recorded AS the model's, the known-block telling the truth, and the hand path.

## Intake: blocking a sender is a button, not homework (v2.175.0)

- **The finding**: after three consecutive ignores from one sender the proposal card said *"consider a Gmail filter"* — the app noticing a problem it could see, could fix, and instead handing the family multi-step configuration in a different application. There was no hand path and no agent path, only advice. Setting up a mail rule is real work; clicking a button on the page you are already looking at is nothing.
- **`ingest_sender_blocklist`** (settings-registry key, `/intake` page — never config.html): entries are `{pattern, label, keywords[], added_at}` and matching is `sender_blocked()`, **deliberately the same lowercase-substring test `sender_default` uses**, so one matcher governs routing and skipping and the two cannot drift.
- **Rules are editable, not just removable (v2.177.1)**: a rule IS its sender and its keywords, so Edit loads both back into the same two fields rather than opening a second editor. Changing the *sender* retires the old pattern first, or the original would quietly go on filtering under a name no longer shown as the one being edited. An edit needs no confirmation (the decision to skip was already made — this is tuning how), keeps the original `added_at` (when the family decided to stop reading a sender is a different fact from when they last adjusted it), and logs "updated" rather than "added".
- **Keywords turn blocking into filtering (v2.177.0)**, which is what the common case actually needs: you subscribe to TeamSnap's calendar, so their *reminders* are guaranteed duplicates while their announcements are not. Empty keywords keep the plain block; non-empty skips only when the **subject** contains one — body matching would quietly eat real announcements, since "reminder" appears in half of all email footers. The log names which keyword fired, so a skip reads as the rule the family wrote. `sender_blocked(..., subject=None)` means "no subject context" and only unconditional entries can match, which is what keeps a partially-filtered sender in the "senders you keep ignoring" offer instead of looking handled.
- **Both readings are offered, because the exact address is usually the wrong one.** School and team platforms rotate senders (`noreply@`, `bounce+7f3a@`), so a literal From-line block lets the next message straight through and the family concludes the button is broken. The card offers "Stop reading `coach.dan@teamsnap.com`" *and* "Stop reading anything from `@teamsnap.com`".
- **The skip happens before `extract_items`** — that is the real saving, one LLM call per message not made, which a mail-client rule only achieves by keeping the mail out of the polled folder entirely. Not at fetch time: the UID cursor must keep advancing or a blocked sender re-fetches forever.
- **Nothing disappears silently.** A skipped message still counts in `checked`, gains its own `skipped` counter, and writes an `ingest_log` row (`skipped: sender blocked (@teamsnap.com)`) rendered in amber on /intake — a block reads as a decision the family made, not as mail that went missing. Blocking and unblocking log their own rows too, the blocklist is listed and reversible on the same page, and blocking clears the ignore streak that prompted the offer. This is the difference from a mail-client filter, which is unnerving precisely because it is invisible.
- **The offer cannot hang off a pending proposal (v2.176.0).** It originally did, and that is backwards: a card in the queue is the transient thing. Ignore *everything* a sender sends and their cards all leave the queue, so the offer disappeared at exactly the moment the family had most thoroughly demonstrated they wanted it — and a family who had been ignoring a sender for months before the feature existed could never reach it at all. **`GET /api/ingest/ignored-senders` reads the proposal LEDGER**, which is never pruned, and drives a "Senders you keep ignoring" panel on /intake (address and domain buttons per row, ≥2 ignores, already-blocked senders filtered out since there is nothing left to decide). `approved` rides along and sorts those senders last: a sender you sometimes act on is not one to block, and a count of ignores alone would hide that.
- **"Not now" is a real answer (v2.177.2).** A row that can only be blocked or left alone sits in the panel forever and trains the family to stop reading it, so ✕ dismisses one. Dismissal stores the sender's ignore COUNT at that moment (`intake_sender_dismissals` app-state), not a timestamp — the offer returns on fresh evidence rather than on a timer, and needs the same number of new ignores it took to appear the first time, because re-asking on the very next ignore would not respect the answer. The row then reports both the total and what is new since (`ignored_since_dismissed`), and the dismissal is logged like every other decision on this page.
- **Activity rows can block too, and they cover the other population entirely.** Mail that yields nothing every time never creates a proposal, so it never appears in the ignore list however long it keeps arriving — yet it costs an LLM call per message. The Activity log is the only place it is visible, so each row naming a real, not-yet-blocked address carries a Block sender button.
- Pinned in `tests/test_email_ingest.py`: the matcher (domain entry catches machine-generated addresses, unrelated senders untouched), extraction genuinely not reached for a blocked sender, the counters, the logged skip, retroactive blocking with no live proposal anywhere in the queue (including the approvals-reported and already-blocked-is-not-offered cases), and a hand-path test asserting both card buttons, the past-senders panel, the Activity-row path, and that the old go-do-it-yourself advice is gone.

## Intake approves-as-configured (v2.168.0)

- **The finding**: the intake card edited title and time only. A parent approving "Volleyball, Tue 5pm" then had to open Google Calendar to fix the location or set it repeating, and then open the schedule, find the event in the inbox and configure who rides — three stops for one email, which defeats the point of a capture layer.
- **The card is now the WHOLE editor** (`/intake`, `ProposalApprove`): location (resolved to a routable address exactly as extracted ones always were), description (leads the event body; the "From family email: …" attribution line survives any edit and also flows into kid/household task notes), and recurrence — `daily | weekly | biweekly | monthly` (+ optional inclusive end date) rendered as a minimal RRULE whose weekday/monthday rides on DTSTART. RFC5545 discipline: UNTIL is a DATE for all-day events and a UTC date-time for timed ones; an unknown cadence is a 400 before anything is inserted.
- **Attendees ARE the Chauffeur setup.** Passenger chips ("Who's going?") and driver chips ("going too:") on the card become the event config on approval — `storage.set_event_config(new_google_id, {passenger_ids, driver_ids})` — which is precisely the object whose absence sends an event to the Drives inbox as "Needs Setup", and a recurring series' instances find it through their `recurring_event_id` fallback. Approve → the refresh already queued → scheduled, one stop. Picking a kid's calendar as the target pre-checks that kid (never overriding a parent's own picks).
- **No attendees → no config.** The inbox stays the honest state for a ride nobody has claimed; approval must not silently vouch for it.
- **Both approval surfaces carry the whole editor (v2.169.0)** — `/intake` (Alpine) and the PWA Family tab's proposal cards (vanilla renderer, `renderProposals`) have identical powers: location, description, recurrence, attendee chips, kid pre-check. **Location fields search real places through Mapbox** — the same `/api/places/autocomplete` + `/api/places/retrieve` (Search Box, session-token billed) pair the errands page uses; picking a suggestion swaps in the canonical "Name, address" and pre-caches its geocode server-side, so the solver never has to look the place up again. The event-location and errand-location fields both get it, on both surfaces.
## Routine times follow the household's clock (v2.173.2)

- **The finding**: a routine's `time_of_day` is stored as `"HH:MM"` and was printed **straight to the screen** at every render site — the PWA's adult routine row, the kid task row, the kid hero card, and both lists on `/routines`. It was never formatted at all, so routine times read as 24-hour regardless of the `time_format_24h` setting.
- **A second, quieter bug in the same place**: `app.html`'s own `formatTime()` (rides, launch lines) called `toLocaleTimeString` with no `hour12`, so it followed the BROWSER's locale rather than the household's setting — right by luck in a 12-hour locale, wrong for anybody who had switched the setting on.
- **`formatClock(hhmm)`** on both surfaces, plus `use24h` loaded from `/api/settings` before the first paint (a late answer would render the day once in the wrong format and then flip it). Deliberately a **pure string transform**: a time of day has no date, and routing it through a `Date` would invent one — and a timezone with it. Midnight reads `12:15 AM`, noon `12:00 PM`, and anything unparseable passes through untouched rather than becoming `NaN`.
- Pinned in `tests/test_routines.py`: no template may render `.time_of_day` raw (regex over `${…}` and `x-text`), each must carry `formatClock`, and each must actually read `time_format_24h` — or the helper is a 12-hour hardcode wearing a formatter's clothes. The rule is written against the bug CLASS, so a fourth render site cannot quietly reintroduce it.

## Verifying finished work is reachable from the admin page (v2.178.0)

- **The finding**: verification was PWA-only. A parent sitting at the admin Chores page could see a kid had finished something and was owed points, and had to pick up a phone to award them. Verification is **the step that pays** — nothing is earned until it happens — so the surface a parent actually sits in front of cannot be the one that can't do it. (`/api/chores/{id}/verify` and `/reject` existed and were correct; only the hand path was missing.)
- **"Waiting for you to verify"** now leads the non-kiosk Chores page: every chore in state `done`, who says they did it, how long ago, what it pays, with **✓ Verify** and **Send back**. Sending back reuses `promptInput` for the reason, because rejecting without one tells a kid they failed and not what to fix.
- **The parent guard is NOT relaxed to achieve this.** The obvious shortcut — drop the token requirement because the admin page sits behind Home Assistant — is wrong twice: the PWA is the same FastAPI app on the same origin, and being behind HA says nothing about *which* parent is at the keyboard. Instead the page mints a real per-device token from the parent's PIN (`POST /api/members/{id}/auth`, the same exchange the phone uses), so `verified_by` names a genuine parent and the points ledger can still explain itself. One parent is used automatically; several offer a `promptChoice`. The token lives under its own storage key so a kid's PWA token on the same browser is never clobbered, and a 403 re-authenticates once rather than telling a parent they are not a parent.
- Pinned in `tests/test_chores.py`: the admin route producing the same `verified_by` attribution as the phone, plus a hand-path check that the page lists finished work, offers **both** answers (approving is not the only outcome), and authenticates rather than posting unauthenticated. The token gating itself stays pinned by `scenario_endpoint_rules`.
- **Reward requests too (v2.178.1).** The "Pending Reward Requests" section one block above had the identical gap and said so on screen — *"approve or deny in the app (parent PIN)"*. It now decides in place through the same `_parentPost` helper (generalised from chores-only to any path), against `POST /api/redemptions/{id}/decide`, which carries the same parent-token guard.
- **The balance is on screen before the click**, compared against the raw balance rather than balance-minus-holds, because that is exactly what the server will deduct.
- **Approval now REFUSES what the balance can't cover (v2.178.2).** The request side already prevented over-asking — `get_spendable_points` subtracts pending requests *and* pool pledges, so a kid cannot stack a second request against points the first is holding — but `decide_redemption` never re-checked, and `adjust_points` downward does not cancel existing holds the way `reset_points` does (a hold against a zeroed balance can never be honoured, so a reset auto-denies them). A request made when the points were there could therefore be approved after they were gone. It now returns `'insufficient'` and the endpoint 409s with the balance, the cost, and how to proceed. **`override=True` is the parent saying "have it anyway"**, stamped on the row as `overridden` rather than being invisible, and it still deducts the full cost — an override is generous, not free. Denials are never bounds-checked: closing a request costs nothing, and `reset_points` depends on being able to deny holds against a zero. Both surfaces turn the 409 into a confirm-and-retry, so neither becomes the one that cannot act.

## Owner and Assign are two different things (v2.173.0)

- **The finding**: v2.172.0 shipped one field, `assigned_to`, that already behaved as an OWNER — it survived every recurrence and was exempt from the stale-claim release — which made the case it was built for impossible. **The dishes have to be done every day and the helper is only in two or three days a week**, so if she owns them they come back to her on the four days she is not there; the work is either untracked or reassigned by hand each time. One field cannot be both a standing arrangement and tonight's decision.
- **`Chore.owner`** — permanent, set in the editor. The chore is that person's job: it never enters the pot, never appears in the kids' available list, and **returns to them every time it recurs**. "My son mows the lawn every week." Member id or `assist:<id>`.
- **`Chore.assigned_by`** — set by `POST /api/chores/{id}/assign`, an **Assign** button beside Claim, parent-only, on an OWNERLESS chore in the pot. It gives out *this occurrence*: assigning is claiming on somebody's behalf, so it reuses the lifecycle exactly, and the only new fact is **who decided**. **Cleared on recurrence**, so tomorrow's dishes are up for grabs again.
- **Being given a job is not the same as choosing one.** `unclaim_chore` refuses when `owner` or `assigned_by` is set — you can put back what you picked up, not what you were handed — and both are exempt from the stale-claim release, which would otherwise quietly undo a parent's decision. Un-assigning (`member_id: null`) returns it to the pot; an owned chore is refused by /assign and changed in the editor instead.
- Points are unchanged and needed no thought: `verify_chore` awards only to a claimant whose role is `child`, so an owner who is a kid still earns for the lawn, and outside help earns nothing exactly as an adult always has.
- **`promptChoice`** joins `promptConfirm`/`promptInput` — a list of people is a prompt too, and the browser has no dialog for it. It exists **twice on purpose**: in `components/control_center.html` for the pages that include it, and again in `app.html`, which does not include that component and carries its own prompt family. *Fixed in v2.173.1: shipped only in control_center, so the PWA threw a ReferenceError and the Assign button did nothing at all — a fourth declared-but-unreachable feature, and the test that was supposed to catch it asserted the symbol existed in control_center.html rather than that the caller could reach one. The test now checks every page that CALLS promptChoice either defines it or includes the component.*
- `migrations.migrate_chore_owner_v21730` copies the old `assigned_to` into `owner` (idempotent, keyed on app state), leaving the old field in place so an upgraded row still shows where its owner came from.
- Pinned in `tests/test_assist_work.py` (9 scenarios), including the case that forced the split: daily dishes stay ownerless, are assigned for one night with the deciding parent recorded, cannot be handed back by the holder, and **are back in the pot the next day rather than given to somebody who is not there** — while a kid's owned lawn returns to him and still pays.

## Assign — the other half of the chore economy (v2.172.0, superseded by v2.173.0)

- **The finding**: recurring housework a helper does — dishes, vacuuming — fitted neither object. A household task's recurrence is coupled to a deadline (`_next_due` returns `None` without a `due_date`), so an undated recurring task completes once and **never comes back**; dishes would need a fake deadline entered daily. And a chore was claim-only, so there was no way to say "this is Maddie's". The everyday word made it worse: in English a *chore* is exactly this routine housework, while the app's `Chore` meant only the kids' claimable points pot.
- **One new concept fixes it: `Chore.assigned_to`.** Kids still CLAIM what they want from the pot; a parent can now also ASSIGN a chore — to a kid, or to outside help. **Assigning is the claim**: the chore is that person's from the moment it is handed over, never sits in the pot, and never appears in the kids' available list.
- **A recurring assigned chore returns to its HOLDER, not the pot** (`_chore_reset_fields` takes the chore). "The dishes are Maddie's" is a standing arrangement, and one that quietly went up for grabs every night would be a different promise from the one the parent made. Assigned chores are also exempt from the stale-claim release — nobody claimed it, so there is no claim to go stale.
- **Outside help differs in exactly one way: no login**, so `POST /api/chores/{id}/record` lets a parent claim-and-finish in one call, landing the chore in the same `done` state awaiting the same verification (which is what sets `reopens_on`). A kid marks their own assigned chore done, as always.
- **Points needed no change at all.** `verify_chore` awards only when the claimant's role is `child`, so an adult has always claimed chores for zero — an outside hand is the same shape, not a new concept, and the kids' economy is untouched. *This corrects v2.171.0, which closed the door on chores partly on a points argument that the role check already handled.*
- Hand path: an **"Or assign it to somebody"** picker on the chores page (household + Outside hands optgroups) and a one-tap **"Maddie did it"** button on an assigned outside-hand chore.

## Outside hands hold work — the second verb (load arc A1 slice 2, v2.171.0)

- **The finding**: the arc shipped the noun and one of its two verbs, and the one it shipped was unreachable. A drive could only be handed over by dragging it onto an assist column, and those columns are built from `assist_assignments` — so a drive with no coverage had no column, and **the first hand-over could not be made by hand at all** (the agent's `cover_with_assist` was the only working door). Housework could not be handed over in any way. `AssistContact`'s own docstring had promised both: *"one referenced by a drive is a carpool driver, one referenced by a chore is house help."*
- **The story, in one sentence**: *an outside hand can hold any unit of work the household hands over, and holding it never counts as the household carrying it.*
- **"Helps with" is a FILTER, never a gate** (`services/assist.py`). Two pills on the contact — 🚗 Driving, 🧹 Housework — stored as ordinary `kinds` tags (so `carpool` on an existing contact still reads as driving, with no migration). The rule: **a contact is offered unless the family has positively said they do the other thing and not this one.** No tags → offered everywhere; only `tutoring`/`pets` → offered everywhere; `housework` → tasks only. Hiding requires positive information, so nobody silently becomes unusable and **a new kind of help still needs no code** — which is the original "nothing branches on `kinds`" rule kept intact, because that rule was about capability, not about what a picker shows. The server refuses nothing on this basis: a housework-tagged contact can still be given a drive if the family says so.
- **Drives**: a **"Hand to someone outside the house"** picker in the event editor, filled from the contacts (not from existing coverage), saving on change. The drag gesture stays as the shortcut it was always meant to be. Whoever currently covers a drive stays selectable even if tagged housework-only.
- **Household tasks**: `assigned_to` accepts `assist:<contact_id>` — a prefix rather than a second column, because "who holds this?" has one answer and a parallel field would let a task be held twice. (Drives keep their separate `assist_assignments` map: there the solver must be told the event is *gone*, which is a different question from who has it.) The owner dropdown gains an **Outside hands** optgroup filtered to housework; `list_household_tasks` resolves the name so an outside holder never renders as "nobody yet"; `claim_household_task` tries the contacts when a name is nobody in the family, so the agent can say what the hand can do.
- **Covering is not carrying**: a contact's finished task appears on the load ledger's *assisting* side and never in the household's split. Their row is created lazily — a contact who did nothing this window is not a gap.
- ~~**Chores stay members-only, on purpose.**~~ **Reversed one version later (v2.172.0)** — the reasoning was wrong twice over and is corrected below.
- **The hand-path guarantee now asserts REACHABILITY.** `test_assist.py` checked that `setAssistCoverage` existed — which a dead function satisfies — and passed for two versions while coverage had no first door. Three features in a row shipped declared-but-unreachable (stage capability switches, the `household_task` intake target, this), all with green guardrails, because the guardrail measured symbol presence. It now asks how the FIRST one is made.
- Pinned in `tests/test_assist_work.py` (6 scenarios): the filter's five cases including untagged-and-oddly-tagged, filter-is-not-a-gate, both hand-overs reachable (with the picker asserted to read from contacts rather than assignments), name resolution + covering-not-carrying on the ledger, agent parity, and the deliberate chore boundary.

## A household task is edited, not claimed sideways (v2.170.1)

- **The finding**: assigning an existing task meant choosing a name in the *create* form's owner dropdown and then clicking the row's "nobody yet" link — `claimTask()` read `#new-task-owner`, so two controls that look unrelated were silently coupled, and picking nobody first produced a scolding alert instead of an action.
- **Replaced with the flow the rest of the app uses**: a ✎ button on every row opens a **task editor modal** (same shape as the errand editor directly below it in `errands.html`) carrying title, owner, due date, recurrence and notes; Cancel / Save Changes; PATCH on save. "nobody yet" now opens that same editor rather than acting invisibly.
- **One eligible-assignee list** (`taskAssignees`) feeds both the create form and the editor, so they can never disagree about who may hold a job — kids appear only once their stage grants `assignable_tasks`. Whoever currently holds a task stays selectable even if no longer eligible (a kid pinned to an earlier stage, a member since removed), because otherwise the select would fall back to "nobody" and saving an unrelated edit would quietly unassign them. A rejected save (the Navigator gate) surfaces the server's reason instead of closing as though it saved; clearing the owner is a real answer that returns the task to "the household owes this".
- Pinned in `tests/test_household_tasks.py`: the hand path now asserts the editor exists, that no `claimTask` function remains, and that "nobody yet" is wired to `openTaskEditor`.

## The calendar is a calendar, not the driving schedule (v2.170.0)

- **The finding**: `refresh_schedule_logic` dropped every all-day event at fetch (`if all_day and not is_trip: continue`) — before the UI payload, not just before the solver. Since every surface reads that one pipeline, **a no-school day, a birthday or "Dad in Chicago" existed on no Chauffeur screen at all.** The renderers were already built for them and were being starved: `calendar.html` maps `all_day` straight onto its event objects, `schedule_timeline` excludes all-day events from the visible-hours computation and styles them at reduced opacity, and the daily-cache loop already handles multi-day spans ending at midnight.
- **One feed, and the solver takes a filtered view.** The drop is gone; the line now sits one step later, immediately after `all_events_for_ui[e.id] = e` — everything above it is what the family SEES, everything below is what the solver reasons about. `is_display_only_event(e)` (all-day, except a `background_trip`, which IS scheduling information) `continue`s there.
- **Why it must stop before the solver, precisely**: a midnight-to-midnight span overlaps every drive that day, and matcher §3c ("Driver Personal Calendar Overlaps") **bans** a driver from any event truly overlapping one of their personal events (`model.Add(assign_vars[(e.id, d.id)] == 0)`). One birthday on a parent's calendar would have taken them off every drive that day. The guard sits before `needs_triage` (no inbox nagging), before `events.append(e)` (never solved) and before `driver_events_map` (never a ban).
- School closure *detection* was never affected — `school.py` fetches the school calendar directly via `gcal._fetch_single_calendar`, so care gaps and half-days always worked; the days were simply invisible.

## Intake: the household list is reachable, and mis-filed to-dos come home (v2.170.0)

- **The finding**: the `household_task` approval branch shipped with load arc A2 and **no dropdown ever offered it**. A household to-do's only plausible targets were a kid's school list, a calendar, or a drive errand — so parents picked a calendar and the item became an all-day 📌 event, landing in exactly the blind spot above. Second instance of the "backend capability with no surface" pattern in a week (after the stage switches), which is what the every-capability-needs-a-hand-path rule exists to prevent.
- **📋 Household list is now a target on both approval surfaces**, leading the list for a to-do (the general case — permission slip, payment, renewal — where a kid's school list is the specific one) and offered on event-kind proposals too, since an "event" the extractor typed wrong is still work.
- **The rescue reads the PROPOSAL LEDGER, not the calendar** (`GET /api/proposals/misfiled`, `POST /api/proposals/{id}/refile`, surfaced on `/intake` as "Filed on a calendar by mistake" with per-row and move-all actions). The other three approval branches write `created_task_id`/`created_errand_id`, so **a task-kind proposal carrying `created_event_id` means exactly "a to-do that became a calendar event"** — no emoji matching, no false positives, no second guess at something already known. Scanning calendars for 📌 was considered and rejected: it re-derives a fact the queue already had, misses any title the parent edited, and collects every legitimate all-day event as a false positive.
- **Refiling does both halves**: creates the HouseholdTask (title with the 📌 stripped, due date and notes intact) *and* removes the stray Google event, because leaving it would put one obligation in two places with no rule for which one you complete. `remove_event` treats an already-deleted event as success; a failed delete still creates the task and says the event needs deleting by hand. Move-all is deliberately sequential — a burst of parallel deletes is how you meet a rate limit while destroying calendar entries.
- Pinned in `tests/test_intake_approval.py` (8 scenarios): the reachable household target (both surfaces, by hand), ledger-not-calendar identification with the three near-misses that must NOT be collected (a real event, an errand, an unapproved proposal), refile-does-both-halves, refuse-a-second-refile, and the display-only guard's position asserted structurally — after the UI payload, before triage/`events.append`/`driver_events_map`.
- Also fixed: `tests/test_board_render.py`'s countdown assertion was racy (`_NOW` stamped at import, the pill computed by jsdom after a node spawn), failing at random on a minute boundary. The offsets now land mid-minute so the exact wording stays pinned.

- Pinned in `tests/test_intake_approval.py` (5 scenarios): the full-edit approve (location wins, attribution survives, RRULE UTC form, config keyed to the new google id), DTSTART-matched UNTIL forms + the 400, no-attendees-no-config, untouched-cards-round-trip-the-extraction, and the hand path.

## The wall panel was compiling its own CSS on every tap (v2.179.0)

- **The report**: the Raspberry Pi 5 wall panel took several seconds to answer a tap and its animations were choppy — while Home Assistant on the same device was smooth. Correct instinct: this was Chauffeur, and it was two things.
- **Every page loaded `https://cdn.tailwindcss.com`.** That URL is not a stylesheet, it is Tailwind's COMPILER — ~400 KB of JavaScript that builds the CSS in the browser at load and then installs a document-wide MutationObserver so it can rebuild whenever a class attribute changes, which Alpine does constantly. The panel shelf is made of ordinary links, so every tap was a full page load that re-downloaded the compiler and re-compiled against a 90–340 KB document. Tailwind's own docs say the Play CDN is not for production; it had been in production here for the life of the project.
- **`panel_skin.html` put a `backdrop-filter` on every card on every page.** The mapped-grey rules (`.bg-gray-950/900`, `.bg-gray-800/*`, `.bg-gray-700/*`, `.bg-gray-600`) each carried `blur(14px) saturate(135%)`. Those selectors are not a handful of elements — 154 `bg-gray-*` sites in `app.html`, 86 in shopping, 77 in the dashboard, and that is the SOURCE count, before a list renders forty rows that are each a `bg-gray-800`. Every blurred element is its own compositing layer whose backdrop — a full-screen photograph — is read back and re-blurred on any frame that touches it. The file's own comment said "NO `backdrop-filter` here, and that is a correctness rule"; a later edit had added it anyway once a `body { filter: none }` override made it visually safe. Visually safe, not cheap.
- **The blur budget is now two things**: `.panel-card` (six leaf tiles on the home board) and `#panel-fade` (one strip behind the shelf). The mapped tiers are opacity only, and `--panel-surface-1/2/3` were raised (dark .45/.55/.68 → .62/.72/.80, light .62/.72/.86 → .78/.86/.93) to carry legibility without it. A scoped reset also strips `backdrop-filter` in panel mode from Tailwind's `backdrop-blur-*` utilities (almost all of which are `fixed inset-0` modal scrims — a full-screen blur held for as long as the modal is open, where the `bg-black/60` under it already does the job) and from FullCalendar's own glass (`.fc-button-primary`, `.fc-popover`, `.fc-daygrid-more-link` — the last is per DAY CELL, up to 35 blurs in a month view). Browsers keep the look; only `html[data-panel]` is touched.
- **Tailwind is precompiled** into `static/tailwind.css` (every page) and `static/tailwind-app.css` (`app.html` only), built by `tools/build_tailwind.py` from `tools/tailwind/*.config.js`. Two files because the driver app's palette is `rgb(var(--c-*) / <alpha-value>)` pointing into `theme.css` — a page that does not load `theme.css` would render every colour as an unresolved variable. Both builds scan ALL templates: components are pulled into a dozen pages and getting the per-page graph wrong shows up as a class that silently does nothing on one page only.
- **The `<link>` goes LAST in `<head>`, immediately before `</head>`, and that placement is load-bearing.** The Play CDN did `document.head.append(<style>)`, so Tailwind's CSS has always been the last stylesheet — after every page's own `<style>` block, after `ha_theme`, after `panel_skin`. Putting the link where the `<script>` used to sit would quietly flip the cascade for every page rule that ties with a utility on specificity, including Preflight.
- **The staleness guard**: a precompiled stylesheet only knows the classes that existed when it was built, so `build_tailwind.py` stamps `/* chauffeur-tailwind-content-hash: … */` over the templates + configs + pinned Tailwind version, and `tests/test_tailwind_build.py` recomputes it. **Add a class to a template and you must run `python tools/build_tailwind.py`** — a forgotten rebuild is a red test rather than a missing style on the kitchen wall.
- **Everything else is vendored too** (`tools/vendor_assets.py` → `static/vendor/`, 3.6 MB committed): Alpine 3.16.1 + collapse, FullCalendar 6.1.15, Mapbox GL 2.14.1, Leaflet 1.9.4 (+ its `images/`), Inter, and the emoji font. Two of these were on floating `3.x.x` CDN URLs, so the panel was silently taking a new Alpine whenever unpkg rolled one. Mapbox and tile servers stay remote — they are network services, not libraries — as does the music widget's `sendspin-js` dynamic import.
- **The emoji font is renamed to "Chauffeur Emoji" on the way in.** Android's own font is called "Noto Color Emoji" and an `@font-face` beats a system font of the same name, so shipping it under Google's name would make every phone in the house download 2 MB it already has. Renamed and placed LAST in the panel font stacks, only a device with no emoji font of its own (exactly Raspberry Pi OS) ever loads it. `unicode-range` slicing survived self-hosting, so a page still pulls two or three slices.
- **One shared-config compromise**: fourteen pages declared `fontFamily.sans: ['Inter', 'sans-serif']` inline and four (calendar, trips, map, trip_kiosk) declared nothing and kept Tailwind's default stack. The shared config is Inter FOLLOWED BY Tailwind's default — which is what the second group already got, and what the eight pages naming Inter without loading the font were falling back to anyway.
- **Also**: `syncHaTheme` wrote seven `:root` custom properties every 5 seconds. Writing a custom property on `:root` invalidates style for the ENTIRE document, so an untouched page paid a full-document recalc twelve times a minute for values that change twice a day. It now writes only what moved.
- Tests: `tests/test_tailwind_build.py` (6 scenarios — staleness, no compiler, no CDN, vendored assets present, emoji rename), and `tests/test_panel_chrome.py`'s filter scenario now asserts the mapped tiers carry NO `backdrop-filter` while `.panel-card` keeps its glass.

## The children kept tapping their way onto the admin side (v2.179.1)
